# Backend — DBS-Operations-OS

Node.js + Express 5 REST API, Prisma ORM on MySQL 8, JWT auth, BullMQ/Redis for image processing,
S3-compatible object storage (Cloudflare R2), Nodemailer for transactional mail.

## Run It

```bash
npm install
npm run setup:local   # prisma generate + migrate dev + seed (first run)
npm run dev           # Redis (in-memory) + nodemon on src/server.js
npm test              # clears leftover test fixtures, then node --test
npm run prisma:studio # optional data browser
npm run prisma:generate # regenerate the client after a schema change - on Windows it first stops
                        # this backend's `npm run dev` (the running server holds the query engine
                        # DLL, so a plain `npx prisma generate` fails with EPERM and leaves a
                        # 21 MB .tmp copy behind each time) - every session holding it, one
                        # forgotten in another terminal too - waits for the file to be released,
                        # then says to start it again
```

### Test data

Several suites write real rows — branches, employees, roles, leads, projects — because what they
check is a SQL rule, and there is no honest way to check one without a database. Each cleans up in
an `after()` hook, which runs exactly as long as the run finishes: not when a test fails hard, not
when the process is killed. What was left behind then sat in the developer's own database and
showed up on screen as real data — the studio's Branches list reached 29 branches, 23 of them named
"Accounts Scope Branch 004915", and one leftover employee made `data-scope-backfill` fail on every
subsequent run.

So `npm test` sweeps first, through `pretest` → `scripts/clear-test-fixtures.js`. It deletes only
rows whose own identifying column begins with a **reserved fixture prefix** — `TCS-`, `TDA-`,
`TDC-`, `TDP-`, `TDS-`, `TES-`, `TLC-`, `TPC-`, `TQE-`, `WCS-`, `TEST_`, `ZZTEST-`, `ZZREP-` — and
never by date or by resemblance. **Do not code real records with those prefixes**, and give a new
suite a prefix of its own and add it to `PREFIXES`: `test/test-fixture-prefixes.test.js` reads both
the script and the suites and fails on a prefix, or a table, the sweep does not know about. A
prefix is matched as written: Prisma sends `startsWith` to MySQL as `LIKE` unescaped, so the
sweep escapes `_` and `%` (`literalPrefix`) - unescaped, `TEST_` deleted every row starting
`TEST` and one more character. The
sweep never fails the run; a database it cannot reach is warned about and skipped.

Defaults to port `5001` via `PORT`. Health check: `GET /api/health` → `{ ok: true }`.
**The Super Admin's password.** `prisma/seed.js` creates `admin@dontblinkstudio.com` with the password written at the top of that file — which is a password in this repository. **The seed applies it only when it creates the account**: reseeding is part of the Rule 4 workflow on the VPS, and an `update` that carried the password would put the studio back on a published credential every time a permission was added. So changing it on a running installation is a database change and the seed will not undo it; `test/seed-super-admin-password.test.js` holds that rule.

`GET /api/health/email` (`ADMIN / System Settings` `READ`) reports whether mail **and SMS** are configured and how each `SMTP_*` sender is faring; `?verify=true` authenticates against each one. It is the answer to "the password reset never arrived", which the reset form itself cannot give — it replies the same way whatever happens, or it becomes a way of asking which addresses have accounts. No password and no API key is ever returned. **Resetting a password** goes two ways: a six-digit code to the mobile number on somebody's employee record (`POST /api/auth/forgot-password/otp`, then `/otp/verify`, two-minute expiry, five attempts, single use — it mints the same reset token the emailed link carries), The screen offers **only** the code; `POST /api/auth/forgot-password` is left on the server, referenced by no screen, as the last way back in for an account whose number has not been set. The number comes from the employee record, or from `user.mobile` for a login with no employee behind it — the Super Admin is that account, and `npm run set:user-mobile -- <username> "+91 9876543210"` is how it gets one (the employee's number wins wherever there is one). Codes need `SMS_PROVIDER` and `SMS_API_KEY` set; they are never routed through the WhatsApp integration, which is a QR-paired personal account that gets banned for automated sending. **Locally there is no gateway**, so with `NODE_ENV="development"` and nothing configured the code is printed on the server console in a box saying nothing was sent — otherwise no screen past the code box could be reached at all. That echo is the only place allowed to print a live code: it refuses to run under any other `NODE_ENV`, including an unset one, `SMS_DEV_ECHO="false"` turns it off, a configured gateway always wins over it, and `GET /api/health/email` reports it as `sms.devEcho`.
`GET /api/user/profile-photo/download` (signed in) answers with the caller's own photo as a file
attachment — reading it here rather than in the browser is what makes the download work for a
photo held on the R2 bucket, which is another origin.

`GET|PUT|DELETE /api/user/preferences[/:key]` (signed in) keep how one login has arranged the
application for itself — column order, hidden columns, frozen columns, column widths
(`table:<slug>`), the density every table reads at (`app:compactView`), the appearance it reads in
(`app:theme`), the order of its own sidebar (`app:sidebarLayout`), and the tab each screen was
left on (`tab:<screen>`). Self-service like the rest of `src/modules/user/routes.js`: `req.user.id`
is the only account any of it touches and nothing in a payload may name another, so `protect` alone
guards it and **no module permission applies** — a table layout belongs to whoever arranged it, and
gating it would mean a role could be refused the right to remember its own screen. The values are
written by a browser and land in a JSON column, so `src/modules/user/preferences/service.js` decides
what each namespace may hold and caps both the value and the number of keys per account; anything
else is refused rather than stored.

The sidebar layout is checked against the sidebar's actual structure rather than stored as sent:
`src/modules/user/preferences/sidebarStructure.js` holds which module owns each screen, and refuses
an arrangement that files one under a module that does not own it — Quotations cannot be saved into
Project Management however the request was written. Only the *structure* is mirrored there; the
canonical sidebar registry, with the labels, routes and icons, is
`frontend/src/constants/navigation.mjs`, and `test/user-preferences-sidebar.test.js` imports it and
fails if the two ever disagree about an id or its default position. A preference orders the sidebar
and nothing else: it grants no access, and what a role may open is still decided by that role's
permissions when the browser draws it.

A changed or reset password may not be one of the account's last six: `src/services/passwordHistory.js`
keeps the previous hashes in `user.password_history` and refuses a match.

`GET /api/release` (signed in) returns the version plus the lead-in of each entry in the
changelog's current section, read by `src/services/releaseNotes.js` for the app's Release
Information dialog. The version needs the `VERSION` file at the repository root (`<major>.<minor>`).
`npm run dev` starts a local in-memory Redis alongside the API so the image queue works without a
Redis install; `npm start` (production) expects a real Redis.

Environment lives in `backend/.env` — never commit it. Setup, credentials, and troubleshooting are
in the [root README](../README.md) and
[docs/project-state/LOCAL-DEVELOPER-SETUP.md](../docs/project-state/LOCAL-DEVELOPER-SETUP.md).
`WISHES_AUTOSEND="false"` turns off the Settings → Wishes sender (on by default, like
`ATTENDANCE_REPORT_AUTOSEND` for the daily attendance report, `LEAD_WELCOME_AUTOSEND` for New Lead
Welcome, `INTERVIEW_WELCOME_AUTOSEND` for Interview Welcome, `PAYMENT_REMINDER_AUTOSEND` for
the two-days-before payment reminder and `SHOOT_CREW_NOTICE_AUTOSEND` for the shoot crew notice). `META_WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN`
and `META_WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET` (both optional) switch on the Meta delivery-status webhook for the
WhatsApp Activity Log and the AI Feature / Photo Selling guest photo-match messages at `/api/public/whatsapp/meta/webhook` - the first is what Meta's dashboard
handshake sends, the second what every status POST is signed with; with either missing the path does
not exist. Neither sends anything, and neither touches the paired-phone sessions.

Photo Selling takes payment through Cashfree: `CASHFREE_CLIENT_ID` and `CASHFREE_CLIENT_SECRET`
(sandbox keys while testing; `CASHFREE_ENV` defaults to `sandbox`), and in production also
`PHOTO_SELLING_PRODUCTION_PAYMENTS="true"` - without keys stores open but checkout says payment is
not set up. `PHOTO_SELLING_NOTIFY_URL` is the public https address of
`/api/public/photo-selling/webhooks/cashfree`, which also goes in the Cashfree dashboard.
Guests find photos only by selfie, so a gallery publishes only while the AI Feature's face engine
(`AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED=true`) is running - Photo Selling has no face setting of its own and calls no
outside service. Readiness is the engine's live `health()` (`faceMatching().state`; see "Face engine"
in docs/project-state/PHOTO-SELLING.md).
All of them, with `CASHFREE_API_VERSION`, `PHOTO_SELLING_PUBLIC_URL`
and `PHOTO_SELLING_EMBEDDED_WORKER`, are described in `.env.example` and
[PHOTO-SELLING.md](../docs/project-state/PHOTO-SELLING.md).

## Code Flow

```text
src/server.js                     express app, mounts every router under /api/*
        │
        └── src/modules/<module>/<feature>/routes.js    router + protect middleware
                    │
                    └── controller.js       request/response, validation, status codes
                                │
                                ├── service.js / src/services/*     storage, email, images
                                └── src/config/db.js  →  Prisma Client  →  MySQL
```

- **Auth** — `src/middleware/authMiddleware.js` exports `protect`, which verifies the `Bearer` JWT
  and puts the payload on `req.user`. A **client** token (`role` CLIENT, made by Create Project →
  Client Credentials) is refused with 403 everywhere but `CLIENT_ROUTES` (`GET /api/user/profile`
  and the portal's `GET /api/client/project`, `/payments`, `/reviews`, `/quotation/pdf` and `/receipt` - a client
  keeps the password the studio gives it, and cannot change or reset it), and there
  the account is re-read each time - inactive, or its project deleted, is 401. Routers apply it once with `router.use(protect)`. Only
  genuinely public routers skip it: `/api/auth`, `/api/onboarding`, `/api/offboarding`,
  `/api/public/image-selection/galleries`. Every credential-taking route on `/api/auth` sits behind
  `src/middleware/rateLimit.js`: failed sign-ins per account from one address
  (`LOGIN_MAX_PER_ACCOUNT_WINDOW`) and from anywhere (`LOGIN_MAX_PER_ACCOUNT_ANYWHERE_WINDOW`), every
  sign-in per address (`LOGIN_MAX_PER_IP_WINDOW`), the reset and OTP requests per inbox or number and per
  address (`PASSWORD_RESET_*`), finishing a reset per token, and `/refresh` per token and per
  address (`AUTH_REFRESH_MAX_PER_IP_WINDOW`), all over `PASSWORD_RESET_WINDOW_MINUTES`; a refusal is
  a 429 with `Retry-After`. With `TRUST_PROXY_HEADER=true` the caller's address is the entry
  `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` (default 1) from the **end** of `X-Forwarded-For`, the one nginx appended, never
  the first, which the caller writes. For a **staff** token `protect` also re-reads the account on
  every request (`staffLoginStands`, one primary-key read, as client tokens always had): a login that
  no longer exists or is not `ACTIVE` is a 401, so an account closed by offboarding (Relieved, Fired,
  Absconded) or by deleting the employee stops working at once rather than when its token expires
  (`JWT_EXPIRES_IN`, 7 days), and the browser signs the person out on that 401. The permission
  guard's own `status: 401` / `code: "USER_NOT_FOUND"` (honoured by `permissionMiddleware`'s
  `checkFailed` instead of a 500) remains the backstop for an account removed between the two.
- **Activity log** — `src/middleware/activityLog.js`, mounted once in `server.js` ahead of every
  router, writes a line to `user_activity_log` for each finished `/api` **write** (POST, PUT, PATCH,
  DELETE - `RECORDED_METHODS`) somebody signed in made; reads are never recorded, since opening one
  screen fires several and the shell re-reads the person's profile on every page load (Administration → User Activity Logs). It decides on `res.on("finish")`, so it never adds time
  to a request or changes its answer; lines are queued and written in batches a second later, and a
  failed write is dropped (logged outside production only). A line holds the user and their login
  **as it was** (a snapshot, not a name copy — the current name is read through `user_id`), their
  home branch, a utility name derived from the route (`/api/sales/crm-leads` → "Sales · CRM Leads"),
  the route handler's name as the action (`METHOD /pattern` where it is anonymous), the method, the
  matched route **pattern** (`/api/sales/crm-leads/:id` — never the URL, its ids or its query
  string; an unmatched URL keeps only its mount — `/api/<first>`, plus the next word under a namespace — and drops the rest), the status, the address (`clientIp` from `rateLimit.js`, so `TRUST_PROXY_HEADER` applies; `::ffff:` IPv4 unwrapped, anything `net.isIP` rejects stored as null),
  the user agent and the duration, with control characters stripped from the text. Never a body, a token or a password — **except `changes`**, which is the one field not derived from the request: a handler
  that wants its write described calls `describeChanges(req, lines)` and those words are stored (`services/recordChanges.js` builds them from before-vs-after, dropping anything whose key looks like a secret). Only on a save that answered under 400, since a refused one changed nothing.
  **Most lines come from the writes themselves** rather than from a handler: `services/changeTracking.js` is a Prisma extension that reads a watched record before an update and describes what moved (`Project DBS-EVT-0001 — Hard Disk Status: — → Pending`), so a screen nobody wired is covered the day it ships - which is how this started, the save the studio was actually pressing having never been wired while the two that were had run four times in a week. It watches `SalesProject`, `ProjectShoot`, `Employee` and `PaymentRequest`: each watched update costs one extra read (~6 ms measured), so the list widens when a question cannot be answered and not before. It drops relations (the resolved-names extension puts them on a write's answer and not on the read beside it, which otherwise reads as a change), the columns every save touches (`COMMON_SKIP`) and anything whose key looks like a secret; `updateMany` records a count, since there is no cheap before-state. Its lines collect in **its own** `AsyncLocalStorage`, not `requestCache`'s, which `forgetRequestCache()` empties after a write - i.e. exactly when they have just been collected. Nothing is collected outside a request, so a scheduled sweep records none. A handler may still describe its own change with `describeChanges(req, lines)` and those words come first, because "Advance of ₹5,000 recorded" reads better than the columns that recorded it. Not recorded: anything without
  a signed-in user, any read, and the writes listed in `SKIPPED` in `src/services/activityLog.js`
  (the usage heartbeat and health checks) — a new timer that writes belongs in that list. The auth
  controller adds `Login`, `Login failed` (no user; the matched account's own login, or `(unknown account)` — what was typed is never stored, since it may be a password; a failed sign-in on a Super Admin carries no branch) and `Logout` (desktop
  refresh-token sign-outs, recorded after the session is revoked and never able to stop it; `/api/auth/logout` is throttled per address by `AUTH_LOGOUT_MAX_PER_IP_WINDOW`, default 300) with module "Auth". Lines older than `ACTIVITY_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS`
  (default **186**, six calendar months at their longest) are deleted daily in batches of 5,000 (`startActivityLogRetention`, started at
  listen, off when `NODE_ENV=test`).
- **Errors** — `server.js` registers a JSON 404 fallback and a final 4-argument error handler, so
  the frontend always parses JSON. Never let an error reach Express's default HTML page.
- **Prisma** — one client from `src/config/db.js`. Schema in `prisma/schema.prisma`, migrations in
  `prisma/migrations/`, local admin from `prisma/seed.js`.

### Module Layout

New work uses one folder per feature under `src/modules/<module>/<feature>/`, with `routes.js`,
`controller.js`, and an optional `service.js`:

```text
src/modules/
  hrm/            master-data (also mounts employees + onboarding-invites + offboarding-invites),
                  employees, onboarding-invites, offboarding-invites,
                  org-chart, payment-requests, separations, attendance, referrals, tasks,
                  interview (the candidate register, plus its own linked Google Sheets and the
                  Google connection behind them, mounted as three routers)
  sales/          projects, whatsapp-group-names
  project-management/ pre-production (the coordinator's own record of each shoot),
                  production (the Production Manager's per-shoot plan and the Production
                  Coordinator's steps)
  image-selection/ dashboard, projects, settings, folders, uploads, gallery, publication, publish-share,
                  deliverables, public-gallery, whatsapp, processing, storage, config,
                  faces (the client gallery's People filter: its own queue, worker and three
                  tables, hooked in only where a photo becomes READY - on whenever the face
                  engine is, AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED=true),
                  album-proofs (its own projects, albums, versions, QC, corrections, client
                  publication and upload-dock mount — separate from projects/ by design),
                  signature-album (the Signature Album tab: a consumer of the project's photos
                  with its own tables - including signature_album_photo, its own uploads - status,
                  link, PIN and permission - never a writer of image_selection_upload, the project
                  or the gallery publication)
  assets/         assets, categories, vendors, client-storage, recycling-bin, asset-requests, inspections
  ai-feature/     the AI Feature (Visual Curation → AI Feature): events, guests, media,
                  processing (its own BullMQ queue and worker), face-engine (the Python sidecar),
                  people (clustering), matches, share, activity, overview, default-settings, public
                  (the guest link) - a module of its own that reads nothing from image-selection/
                  but the Redis connection factory
  photo-selling/  Photo Selling (Visual Curation → Photo Selling): galleries, photos
                  (uploads - each original byte for byte to the private originals bucket, its
                  web-size copies to the public one, under photo-selling/ in R2), settings (its own,
                  photo_sale_setting), shared/cleanup.js (durable R2 deletion), pricing (the
                  one place a price is computed), orders, payments (Cashfree client, webhook,
                  reconciliation), search (face indexing and selfie search, its own queue and
                  worker), watermarks, overview, public (the guest store). Independent of every
                  other module; reuses the AI Feature's derive() and watermark renderer as code.
                  See docs/project-state/PHOTO-SELLING.md
```

`src/modules/assets/` predates the folder-per-feature convention and uses flat
`<feature>.routes.js` / `<feature>.controller.js` files; `src/routes/authRoutes.js` and
`src/controllers/authController.js` are the original auth pair. Follow the folder-per-feature
convention for anything new rather than extending the older shapes.

### Shared Services and Workers

| Path | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `src/services/wordChunks.js` | **Word Chunk Search**, the rule every free-text search and column filter on the server uses (the frontend's `lib/wordChunks.mjs`, again): what was typed is split at spaces and a row matches when every chunk is found in one of the searched columns, in any order. `everyChunk(search, (chunk) => ({ OR: [...columns contains chunk] }))` gives one clause per chunk to push into `where.AND` - beside, never over, the access clauses. Capped at 8 chunks. Each chunk reaches the clause LIKE-escaped (`likeLiteral`: `\`, `%`, `_`), because Prisma's `contains` does not escape them on MySQL; a raw-SQL `LIKE` search wraps its value in `likeLiteral` itself. A new search uses it rather than one `contains: search` |
| `src/services/referenceMatchers.js` | How a branch, a vertical or an employee written as text is matched to its row - by current name, by code or id, by login username, and by the names the row has given up (`aliases`, written on every rename so a stale sheet still lands on the right row) — the one rule behind the match report, the backfill and every write that keeps a reference column in step with its text |
| `src/services/resolvedNames.js` | The reading rule, applied to the Prisma client in `config/db.js`: for every model that keeps a text copy of a name beside a reference (`RESOLVED_NAMES`), a read asks for the referenced row and hands back its current name under the key the screen always read, and the stored text where the reference is NULL. Top level of the query only; raw SQL readers are not covered - see DATA-SCOPE.md D39 |
| `src/services/userNames.js` | Who did it, by name: a `created_by` / `updated_by` / `decided_by` column holds a **user** id, so `withUserNames(rows)` - which reads a field only when it holds an id, never a relation of the same name (a payment request's `requestedBy` is the employee record) - adds `<who>Name` beside each one (`createdBy` → `createdByName`), read through that user to their linked employee's name, else the user's own - never by looking the number up among employees. Every list a table reads runs its rows through it; the shared table never offers the raw id as a column |
| `src/services/shootLocations.js` | A combined shoot ("Haldi + Mehandi") held at several places - Add / Edit Shoot → **Location: Multiple**. Still **one** `project_shoot` row: `shoot_locations` (JSON, migration `20261028120000_project_shoot_locations`) holds one `{ shoot, shootPlace, district, state }` per name in the name's order, and `shootLocationColumns` writes `shoot_place` as the places joined positionally with ` + ` (repeats kept, so each lines up with its name) and `district` / `state` as each distinct value once; NULL is one place, the shoot as it always was. `shootLocationsSchema` + `shootLocationsIssue` are on both shoot schemas in `sales/projects/controller.js` (one location per name, in order, each with a place, the joins within 191); `projectShootData` is the only writer, so a shoot saved at one place clears any it held. Pre Production → Import clears it when the sheet gives a place. **Venues:** such a shoot is asked a Location Address and Link per name in Pre Production - `project_pre_production.locations` (JSON, migration `20261029130000_pre_production_locations`), `{ shoot, locationAddress, locationLink }` per name. `shootVenues(shoot, pre)` reads them matched by name (a shoot never asked per name starts every name on its one address; null for one place) for the team's row, the client's form and PDF (`client/portal/preProduction.js`, fields `locationAddress:<name>`) and the production detail; `shootVenueColumns` writes them with `location_address` / `location_link` labelled one per line ("Haldi: …") for every one-address reader; `shootVenuesIssue` refuses names that are not the shoot's. One address saved on its own, or given by the import, clears them. `test/shoot-locations.test.js`, `test/pre-production-shoot-venues.test.js` |
| `src/services/handoverNames.js` | "New (Old)": a role handed over at offboarding (`employee_handover`, written by `hrm/separations/handover.js`) reads "Asha (Ravi)" while the person it was handed to still holds it - a change by hand ends it with nothing to clear. `withProjectHandovers(rows, projectIdOf)` for project roles, `productionHandedFrom(ids)` for a shoot's coordinator and crew places. Applied by the screens' read endpoints only, never by the shared name resolver |
| `src/services/nameCopies.js` | Where a branch's, a vertical's, an employee's or a login's **name** is written out as text on another record (`source_branch`, `project_manager`, `assigned_to_name`, `crm`, ...) and how a rename reaches each copy: by the reference beside the text where there is one, by exact text where there is not, and people only ever by reference. One list, read by Settings -> Branches, Field Settings (whose `usedBy` for branches and verticals *is* this list), the Employee Repository and Administration -> Users, each inside the rename's own transaction. Audit rows are not copies and are not named here |
| `src/services/activityLog.js` | The user activity log's writer and rules: `recordActivity` (queued, batched, never throws), `recordAuthEvent` for the sign-in / sign-out lines, `entryForRequest` and the route-pattern, utility-name and action derivation the middleware uses, the `SKIPPED` poll list, `statusTextFor`, and the daily retention sweep (`ACTIVITY_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS`, default 186 — six calendar months at their longest, so the screen's "Last 6 months" always has its whole span) |
| `src/services/employeeReferences.js` | The references beside an employee's master-data text - designation, work type, gender, blood group, marital status, relationship, both work locations, the vertical and the branch - and the department / skill / language rows beside the ", "-joined lists. `referenceColumns(data)` for a write, `syncLists(tx, id, data)` after it; resolves only the keys the save carries, matches a list row by folded name and only where exactly one row has it. Every employee write (repository create, import, update; onboarding submission and resubmission) goes through it - see DATA-SCOPE.md D36 |
| `src/services/dataScope.service.js` | Whose rows a request gets: turns a grant's data scope (ALL / BRANCH / TEAM / SELF) into a Prisma `where` and a per-record check, with the recursive org-chart walk TEAM needs. Screens and AI tools both resolve through it — see [DATA-SCOPE.md](../docs/project-state/DATA-SCOPE.md). `seesEverything(userId, { module, feature })` asks the first half of that question on its own — is this person narrowed at all — for a reader with no rows to narrow: the topbar bell, which widens from somebody's own work to the studio's for anybody the scope does not confine. It is here so that no caller has to write a `req.user.role === "SUPER_ADMIN"` test of its own, which would be a second answer to this file's question and one no role could be granted |
| `src/services/bulkDelete.js` | Deleting a ticked set of rows, from any module's table — the counterpart to `tableImport.js` beside it. `mountBulkDelete(router, { path?, guard, deleteOne, noun })` registers `DELETE /bulk` and mounts `checkPermission("ADMIN", "Table Select All", "READ_WRITE")` **itself**, so a router cannot be wired with a bulk delete and no guard; `guard` is whatever the module needs on top, exactly as its single-row delete needs it. It holds no Prisma client and deletes nothing: `deleteOne(id, req)` is the module's *own* delete — `deleteAssetRecord`, `removeEmployee`, `deleteProjectRecord`, `removeVendorRecord` — which is the whole of the security argument, since the ids arrive in the body and only the module knows what makes a row reachable (CLAUDE.md Rule 4). A module handing over a bare `prisma.thing.delete` would open a hole this permission does not close. Ids may be strings or numbers and are echoed back **as sent**, because the selection bar clears its ticks by looking each one up in the set it sent. Walks them one at a time and reports per id the way the importer reports per row: a refusal lands in `missedIds` rather than failing the request, so a selection of forty with three that are not yours deletes thirty-seven and says so; nothing deleted at all is a 404, which is not the same answer as a partial success. Capped at 500 per request. `test/bulk-delete.test.js` |
| `src/services/messageTemplateSwitches.js` | Settings → Templates' **ON / OFF** per WhatsApp message (`message_template_switch`; no row = ON). `GET /api/settings/message-templates/switches` (whoever reads or sends the messages) and `PUT /switches/:key` (`SETTINGS / Message Template` Read / Write, `SETTINGS / Wishes` for the three wish families). OFF refuses the message's manual send on the server (`requireTemplateOn(key)`, `409 TEMPLATE_SWITCHED_OFF`, on the Cash Received, Employee Invite, Login Credentials, Interview Application Invite, Client Login Credentials and CRD Document Link sends; the offboarding form's four messages - `POST /api/hrm/offboarding-invites/:id/send-whatsapp` `{ kind, message }`, each only in its form's state - and the feedback reply - `POST /api/project-management/feedback/:id/send-whatsapp`, to the project's client main number - ask it by key in the handler) and stops its automatic sends (`isEnabled`, asked by the Cash Received group send, Payment Reminder, Shoot Crew Notice / Removed - settled without a message so no backlog is sent later - Production's three messages and the Wishes). Attendance Report, Photo Selection Submitted, New Lead Welcome and Interview Welcome keep their own switches. |
| `src/services/emailService.js` | Nodemailer transactional mail (invites, credentials) |
| `src/services/imageStorage.js`, `objectStorage.js`, `documentStorage.js` | Uploads and reads against R2/S3 (`src/config/r2.js`) |
| `src/services/imageCompression.js` | Sharp derivatives and EXIF |
| `src/services/windowsAppAlias.js` | `isWindowsAppAlias(command)`: whether a bare command resolves, on Windows, to a Store app alias under `WindowsApps` (the `python3.exe` stub), answered from PATH with `lstat` and no spawn; always false off Windows. Spawning one makes Node abort on its next spawn ("AssignProcessToJobObject: (87)"), so both Python searches - the call transcriber and the face engine - skip such a candidate |
| `src/services/imagePresets.js` | The Visual Curation preview and thumbnail presets (High … Smallest), the ladder, each module's recommended pair; mirrored in the web and desktop apps |
| `src/services/imageBytes.js` | JPEG / WebP / PNG by their first bytes, for routes that store a derivative as it arrived |
| `src/services/shortCode.js` | The short public codes behind shortened links — client gallery URLs and onboarding invite links share one alphabet and generator |
| `src/services/quotationPdfService.js` | Renders the public quotation page to PDF in headless Chrome (Puppeteer) as **one sheet the length of the document, 1080px wide** - the reference design is a continuous proposal, and cut into A4 pages it broke a card wherever a page ended. The viewport is set to the sheet's width before the page loads, the height is measured once every picture the page asked for has arrived (CSS backgrounds included; one still loading after 20s fails the render rather than printing a hole), blurred box-shadows are taken off the printed copy because Chrome writes each as a print-resolution bitmap (sharp ones stay; `withoutBlurredShadows`), and `pageRanges: "1"` guards against the blank page a fractional height can add. Needs `FRONTEND_URL` to point at the running frontend; one browser is shared per process. While it prints, the page's photographs are handed to Chrome as JPEG no wider than 1280px (`src/services/pdfImageCompression.js`) - Chrome keeps a JPEG in a PDF as it is but stores WebP as raw pixels, which made a quotation about 6 MB; pictures with transparency, SVGs and anything it cannot read load as they were. Tamil prints from the Noto Tamil web fonts the page carries, never from a font installed on the server |
| `src/services/pdfImageCompression.js` | The quotation PDF's pictures as JPEG sized for the 1080px sheet (`compressImageForPdf`, and `installPdfImageCompression` on a Puppeteer page). Only the quotation renderer uses it; the client's link still serves the originals |
| `src/services/documentBranding.js` | The logo a document was issued under, frozen on its row. `snapshotDocumentBranding()` is called once where a quotation version or CRD link is *created* and returns the `logoUrl` column to write; `brandingForDocument(row)` is what every reader of a stored document asks and answers from the row alone, never Settings. A row older than the column answers `null` and shows the bundled default mark. Any new document type follows the same two calls |
| `src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/` | The brand behind a quotation - Dum Dum, Qua Qua, Corp - and what it froze onto each version. `service.allowsClientDownload(row)` is the one answer here that is deliberately *not* frozen: whether the client holding this quotation's link may download it, read live off the brand's `allow_client_download` so withdrawing it reaches the links already sent, and `false` for a brand row, a brand code or a database that will not answer. `service.brandConfiguration(code)` is what the generator loads: the brand's services and deliverables (each with the project definition it points at), packages, terms, PDF wording and pictures, one read per table. `service.presentationFor(code, { payload, quotation })` gathers everything the client's copy prints beyond the rows as it stands now - the Global Name and Global Logo from Settings → Organization (a brand has no logo of its own), the studio's shared content from `sales_quotation_settings.pdf_content` / `pdf_assets` / `pdf_sections` (the four section switches - notes, trademark, journey, infrastructure - where only a stored `false` hides one) (the notes and trademark cards, Journey & Legacy with its map and timeline, Our Infrastructure with its collages - Settings → Quotation Settings → General) laid over the brand's own pictures and words (`sales_quotation_brand.pdf_content` / `pdf_assets`: cover and Thank You photographs and the rest of the wording), the quotation's branch (by its resolved `branchId`, then by the text it holds, against the branch register) with its website, phone and email, the head office standing in for a quotation that names no branch, and every active branch as the locations list. `service.snapshotForQuotation(payload)` is called once as a version is raised and freezes that answer plus the brand's own wording overrides (never the template's defaults, which are the code's to change - `LEGACY_PDF_CONTENT` in `defaults.js` names the old defaults an early snapshot baked in, and they read as nothing chosen), the terms and the GST rate into `payload.brandSnapshot`, which the public page and the PDF read instead of the tables - so a brand's settings can change every week and `DBS-QO-DD-ERD-000001` keeps printing what it was sent with. `service.presentationForQuotation(row)` is what `GET /api/public/quotations/:token` answers with: the version's own snapshot, and today's answer only where a snapshot older than this shape is silent. `service.paymentDetailsForQuotation(row)` is the other live answer beside the download one: the Assigned Branch's account holder, bank, account number, IFSC, UPI IDs and payment QR as Organization → Branches holds them *now* - found by `branchForPayment`, stricter than `presentationFor`'s match because a wrong guess here misdirects money: the resolved `branchId` (the row's, or the one its snapshot froze), answering nothing if that branch has since been closed, and only where the id names no row the quotation's branch text as an exact name - no city, no prefix, and two branches of one name is no answer; never the head office, never the first branch - shaped by `paymentDetailsOf` into the six strings and one list of UPI IDs the public payment page prints and nothing else off the row, and null where the branch holds no account number, UPI ID or QR, or the register cannot be read. Read live because where the money goes is the studio's standing fact about the branch, not part of what was sent. `defaults.js` holds what prints when a brand has configured nothing - the reference design's words and timeline - held to the frontend's copy by `test/sales-quotation-brands.test.js` and `test/sales-quotation-presentation.test.js`. `assertNewSelectionsOffered` refuses a line newly chosen off a retired or another brand's master while keeping every line an earlier version already held |
| `src/modules/image-selection/processing/{queue,worker}.js` | BullMQ image pipeline; pending jobs are recovered on boot |
| `src/modules/image-selection/ai-culling/` | **AI Photo Culling**, the staff-side triage before Photo Selection, on the project's **Photo Culling** tab (`photoCullingTab`). Mounted at `/api/image-selection/projects/:projectId/ai-culling` behind `protect` + `requireModule("IMAGE_SELECTION", "Project Management")`. A run is an `image_selection_culling_session` scoped to a folder subtree or the whole project; the BullMQ worker (`worker.js`, in the API unless `AI_CULLING_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false`, then `npm run worker:culling`) scores each READY photo's *preview* for blur, exposure and perceptual-hash duplicates and bursts, and asks a face provider about each preview - `AI_CULLING_PROVIDER` `auto` (default) picks the server's own InsightFace engine when `AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED=true`, else Rekognition when its credentials are set (preview bytes only - no original leaves the studio), else none. Results and groups stay on the session; finalizing stamps `image_selection_upload.cull_rejected_at` + `cull_rejected_session_id` on every non-Keep photo *this session analyzed* (never on one the client already selected, and lifting an older run's stamp on those photos), which `gallery/repository.cullEligible`, the client media routes and the publication counts filter out; reopening clears that session's stamps and hands each photo it settled back to the newest other finalized run's verdict (`stampOwnership.js`). Starting holds the project's row lock (`repository.withProjectLock`) so two Starts make one run. A run refuses to start while an active run's scope overlaps its own (`scope.js`), seeds its result rows once (`seeded_at`) and reports later-processed photos as `newPhotoCount` rather than absorbing them; preview fetches time out at 30 s and a failed run stores `safeFailureMessage()`, never the raw error. A session's `analysis_source` is SERVER (the worker reads previews) or DESKTOP (the workstation posts signals through `pending` / `signals` / `run`; boot recovery leaves a waiting DESKTOP run alone — `repository.recoveryWhere()`); `engine_versions` names the engines that stamped it. Face providers: `insightface` (the server's own engine, `providers/insightface.provider.js` → `engineFacesToFaceDetails`, the one mapping the server and the desktop hand-off share) or `aws-rekognition`. The client gallery's People face scan asks the engine for faces only (`describe(buffer, { signals: false })` - box, embedding, quality; the landmark model still runs for the pose quality reads) and skips these signals, which only culling reads; every other caller gets them. **Culling V2 face signals** (engine `face-v2@1`, `AI_FACE_SIGNALS`): eye openness from the 68-point landmarks' eye aspect ratio (read only within 30° of frontal; `HALF_CLOSED_EYES` below 55, a blink below 25), head pose (`head_pose_score`, `LOOKING_AWAY` past 45° of yaw), face sharpness measured on the face crop (`face_sharpness_score`; severe blur with a face at 55+ is not rejected for blur) and expression from FER+ (`expression_label`, `expression_score` = 50 + 50·P(happy) − 25·P(anger, disgust, contempt)); each optional model missing only removes its measurement. After faces, a **subject stage** (`analysis/subjects.js`) joins each photo's face boxes to the project's People clusters (`image_selection_face_person`, for identity only) and stores `people_count`, `subject_person_id`, `subject_score` and a prominence-weighted `eyes_open_score` on the result (each face by its size and centrality in the frame, `analysis/subjects.js`; no role or photo count is read); ranking nudges the final score by it. Grouping (similarity v2, `analysis/duplicates.js`) reads the hash, the **structure signature** (`analysis/structure.js`, the thumbnail's 8×8 layout as 64 hex, stored as `structure_signature`) and the people in each frame (`listMappingsForUploads`, READY photos only): a DUPLICATE needs the hash and no disagreement from layout or people, a BURST the hash, or a looser hash (≤26 bits) with a close layout and somebody in common; members are ranked (`duplicate_rank` / `burst_rank`, 1 = the keeper) on a **balanced keeper merit** (`groupMeritOf`: with faces technical 35% · eyes 30% · expression 20% · face quality 15%, plus who is in the frame and head pose; without faces technical alone), and the manual best re-ranks and re-derives both frames' decisions in Balanced/Strict (Target Count swaps them; Assisted decides nothing). **Similar series** (`SIMILAR` groups, `similar_group_id` / `similar_rank`) chain frames of one set-up from the scene engine's CLIP embedding: cosine ≥ 0.92 within 120 s, or, for photos with no capture time, adjacent in file order at ≥ 0.95; relative only — the alternates get `ALTERNATE_FRAME` and are never penalized or rejected for it. `quality_score` is the photo's own quality (no group, no taste), `preference_adjustment` the photographer's taste, and `final_score` the decision score (quality + taste + group terms). A **scene stage** (after subjects, before grouping) adds `scene_label` / `scene_confidence` / `aesthetic_score` from the optional scene engine (`scene/engine.js` → `scene/scene_engine.py`, CLIP ViT-B/32 int8 over previews, `AI_CULLING_SCENE_ENABLED`, labels from `scene/profiles/event.json` → `scene/assets/` built by `scripts/build-scene-assets.py`; cache-first through `image_selection_upload_scene`) and `composition_score` / `FACE_CUT_OFF` from `analysis/composition.js`; `ACCIDENTAL_FRAME` only when the technical stage also found the frame poor (`analysis/scene.js`). Target Count's story coverage (`analysis/coverage.js`) keeps the photographer's own Keeps first and never picks their Rejects, then guarantees each key person's best frame (`COVERS_PERSON`), then each sure scene's best frame per folder (`COVERS_SCENE`), then each **moment** (a run of capture times with no gap over 180 s; larger moments first; `UNIQUE_FRAME`), then fills best-first with a 6-point penalty per frame already picked from the same similar series; the overview's `coverage` (`scene/profile.js coverageOf` over `repository.coverageCounts`, from effective decisions) reports moments and key people covered by Keeps. **Photographer preferences** (`analysis/preferences.js`): the starter's overrides of AI calls in their ten latest runs (since `image_selection_culling_preference.reset_at`, switch `enabled`) train two L2 logistic models over explainable traits (defect/style codes and sure scenes, never generic quality badges); a run snapshots the profile (`session.preference_profile`, ≥ 20 overrides) and ranking nudges each photo ±8 at most, badged `TASTE_UP` / `TASTE_DOWN`. Culling V2: evidence is weighted by how each decision was made (`learning/labels.js`: preview panel 1, key/click 0.8, bulk 0.3, an untouched call 0.2 seen / 0.1 unseen; a trait needs 5 photos' worth), a reject that lost to its duplicate or burst twin teaches nothing, each code is also learned per sure scene (`<code>@<scene>`), and the photographer's burst-winner picks (`GROUP_BEST` events, `repository.listGroupChoices`) move their keeper-merit shares by at most 0.1 each (`learnGroupTaste`, snapshotted as `preference_profile.groupTaste`, applied by the grouping stage). Photos are never deleted or moved |
| `scripts/auditImageSelectionDerivatives.js` | Read-only: which READY photos still have both derivatives in R2 and which do not, per project, and whether this server could rebuild each lost one (an absolute original inside the Original Storage Path, or a browser upload still under the upload root) or the photo must come back through the desktop app (`storageRootId`) or the browser (a drop-zone trail). The gallery API composes each URL from the key on the row and never checks the bucket, so a lost object shows as a broken image while the card and filename load. Prints the oldest object still under the photo-selection root: a date that keeps moving forward is a bucket-side expiry rule, which has to go before anything is rebuilt. `--project <id>`, `--list`; one `r2.listKeys` listing, no HEAD per photo |
| `scripts/recoverImageSelectionDerivatives.js` | `npm run recover:derivatives -- --project <id>` (`--all`, `--apply`, `--force`, `--list`). Preview-only recovery, a dry run by default: for each READY photo whose thumbnail or preview is gone from R2 and whose original this server can read, [processing/recovery.js](src/modules/image-selection/processing/recovery.js) renders the missing object(s) again with `service.renderDerivatives` - the same recipe the pipeline uses - and writes them under the keys already on the row, then fetches each public URL and counts the photo rebuilt only on 200 image bytes. No row is written, nothing is queued, the face scan is not told (the test pins that the module loads neither the hook nor the pipeline). Desktop-registered and browser drop-zone photos are counted as skipped-unavailable. An apply first reads the bucket's lifecycle rules (`r2.lifecycleRules`): an enabled expiry rule over the photo-selection root, or rules the token cannot read, block it unless `--force` |
| `src/modules/image-selection/faces/` | The client gallery's **People filter**: a plugin on the side of Image Selection. `hook.js` is told - never awaited - where a photo becomes READY (`processing/service.js` and `uploads/derivativeController.js`) and queues it on `image-selection-faces`; `worker.js` + `processing.js` read the photo's *existing* preview, ask the AI Feature's face engine (`faces/engine.js` is the one import of `ai-feature/`, so one Python process serves both modules) for faces and vectors, and `clustering.js` joins each face to the project's nearest person above the threshold or starts a new one - two faces in one frame are never one person, and a photo added later is compared with the people already found rather than re-clustering the gallery. What is stored is only `image_selection_face_scan` (how far each photo got), `image_selection_face_person` (a running centroid and which existing photo, and where in it, stands for the person) and `image_selection_face_mapping` (person ↔ existing `image_selection_upload` id, with confidence); no photo is copied and no crop is written anywhere - the People row's circles are cut from the cover photo's thumbnail in the browser. All three cascade from the photo in the database, so deleting a photo or a folder cleans them up without the delete paths knowing; the project purge takes the person rows in one added line. `filter.js` is the whole of the read-side integration: one more `where` condition on `gallery/repository.listPublishedImages` - one `faceMappings.some` per person under `AND`, so several people mean the photos **all** of them are in together, never either. `service.listPeople` counts people for the scope asked for - the gallery, one folder, or one shoot's cards with `folderGroupId` - and **numbers them by how many photographs they are in**: Person 1 is whoever appears in the most, down to the fewest, with a tie settled by which was found first so two reads cannot disagree. The number is a **label for that scope**, never an identity - the same face leads the shoot it dominates and is further down another - and everything meaning a particular person carries the cluster's own `id`. A count is of photographs rather than faces, because `image_selection_face_mapping` holds one row per person per photo (`@@unique([personId, imageId])`), so a frame somebody appears in twice counts once. `search`, `page` and `pageSize` are the gallery's People picker's, applied to the whole ranked list before a page is sliced from it, which costs nothing: the list is already in memory by then, which a gallery of thousands of faces cannot be handed in one answer; the count is **one grouped query over the mappings** rather than a counting subquery per person, and only the page's cover faces are read. On whenever the face engine is (`AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED=true`; `IMAGE_SELECTION_FACE_RECOGNITION_ENABLED=false` keeps the engine for the AI Feature alone); the worker rides in the API unless `IMAGE_SELECTION_FACE_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false` (`npm run worker:faces`), scans stuck by a restart are re-queued at boot, and an engine outage parks a scan rather than failing it. A gallery whose photos were never handed over - one from before the filter existed - is queued in the background the first time a client opens its People tab (`service.ensureQueued`: one count per people request, never awaited, never twice at once), and can also be queued by hand with `POST /api/image-selection/projects/:projectId/faces/queue` |
| `src/modules/ai-feature/processing/{queue,worker,service}.js` | The AI Feature's own queue (`ai-feature-media`) and worker: per photo, read the preview from R2, ask the face engine for faces and vectors, store each face and its embedding, cluster it into a person, match it against the event's registered guests. Embedded in the API unless `AI_FEATURE_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false`, in which case `npm run worker:ai-events` runs it alone; pending rows are recovered on boot |
| `src/modules/ai-feature/processing/runs.js`, `src/modules/ai-feature/notifications/` | Processing runs and the guest WhatsApp summary - see [AI Feature guest WhatsApp summary](#ai-feature-guest-whatsapp-summary). Off unless `AI_FEATURE_WHATSAPP_ENABLED="true"` |
| `src/modules/ai-feature/face-engine/{engine.js,face_engine.py}` | The face engine: one long-lived Python process per worker speaking JSON lines, running InsightFace's SCRFD detector and ArcFace recogniser fetched from the Hugging Face Hub (`public-data/insightface`). Off unless `AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED=true`; probed at boot, never awaited; when it is down, face jobs stay queued with the reason on show rather than failing. Packages in `requirements-face.txt`. Its packages live in **`backend/.venv-face`**, an environment of its own (apart from Node and from the call-transcript `.venv`), made and refreshed on every deploy by `scripts/face-engine-setup.sh` from `requirements-face.txt` - which names every import with the headless OpenCV, installing insightface `--no-deps` so the desktop `opencv-python` it declares never lands on a server - and proved by `face_engine.py --check` (imports, model files, one detection). `engine.health()` is the cheap report behind `/api/health` (`faceEngine`) and `/api/health/face-engine`; the deploy's health gate fails on `offline`. Nothing else depends on the engine: down, it is a reason string and parked jobs, never an error in another module |
| `src/modules/image-selection/ai-culling/learning/` | The culling **learning layer**, off unless switched on: `featureSet.js` (the 69 named, ranged signals a model reads, `features_v2`: v1's 49 plus face sharpness, head pose, the expression class, the half-closed/looking-away codes, the profile-v2 scenes and the similar series; a model trained on another version is refused), `labels.js` (how a decision was made and how much it counts as a label), `pseudonym.js` (HMAC ids under `AI_CULLING_TRAINING_HMAC_KEY`), `treeModel.js` (LightGBM dumps evaluated in JS, validated at load, with per-signal contributions), `scorer.js` (global model, bounded photographer offsets, burst order, then today's decision code), `registry.js` (checksummed bundles from `AI_CULLING_MODEL_DIR`; serves nothing unless `AI_CULLING_SHADOW_ENABLED` / `AI_CULLING_LEARNED_RANKER`), `runtime.js` (the hooks in the ranking stage; any error falls back to the formula), `exporter.js` (consent-gated pseudonymous dataset), `shadowReport.js` (the promotion gate), `operations.js` (register, promote, roll back). One intelligence for both apps: the web and the desktop app review through the same routes, so their feedback lands in one event log and one dataset. Each event carries `platform` (`WEB` | `DESKTOP`, where the review happened), and each exported example carries `reviewPlatform` and `inferenceLocation` (`SERVER` | `DESKTOP`, where the signals were computed, from the run's `analysisSource`). These are metadata, never a feature and never a different weight. The shadow report and the trainer's verdict judge the two environments apart as well as together, so a model that helps one and hurts the other is refused. Decision events (`image_selection_culling_decision_event`) are written on every review action and seen photo; only the export is consent-gated. Commands: `npm run culling:export | culling:train | culling:register | culling:shadow-report | culling:promote | culling:retention | culling:pipeline` ([scripts/cullingLearning.js](scripts/cullingLearning.js)); the trainer is [ml/culling/](ml/culling/README.md) |
| `src/modules/image-selection/culling-learning/` | The studio's training consent and a read-only list of trained models: `GET/PATCH /consent` (who changed it and when are kept; the same answer again writes nothing) and `GET /models` (status and headline numbers, never a file path), behind `protect` + `requireModule("SETTINGS", "Organization")` |
| `src/modules/image-selection/ai-culling/scene/{engine.js,scene_engine.py}` | The culling **scene engine** (Phase 6): one long-lived Python process per culling worker, same JSON-lines contract as the face engine and the same interpreter (`backend/.venv-face` — no extra package). It runs the pinned CLIP ViT-B/32 int8 vision tower (Hugging Face `Xenova/clip-vit-base-patch32` @ `d15189d7`, sha256-verified, downloaded once — about 89 MB, needs internet the first time and a writable `AI_SCENE_MODEL_DIR`, default `~/.cache/dbs-culling-scene`) on previews only and answers a scene from `scene/profiles/<AI_SCENE_PROFILE>.json` (default `event`, vectors in `scene/assets/`, rebuilt by `scripts/build-scene-assets.py`), an aesthetic score (LAION head, MIT) and the unit embedding. `AI_CULLING_SCENE_ENABLED` `auto` (default) follows `AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED`, `true`/`false` force it; the `event` profile is v2 (18 wedding/event scenes, measured 71% top-1 on the studio's own shoots against 48% for v1). Only the scenes its `calibration` validated (at least 5 sure calls on the hand-labelled set, at least 80% right: Couple, Bride portrait, Family, Group, Details, Kids) count toward a decision - a Target Count guarantee, the coverage line, a learned taste (`scene/profile.js unvalidatedSceneIds` / `uncountedSceneIds`); the others (Ceremony was called 11 times there and wrong every time) are shown as the AI's guess and stay filterable, their confidence stored as measured; warmed when the culling worker starts; CPU by default, CUDA only when onnxruntime offers it (`AI_SCENE_DEVICE=cpu` forces CPU), `AI_SCENE_THREADS` caps threads. Start-up has a deadline (`AI_SCENE_START_TIMEOUT_MS`, 10 min), each request one (`AI_SCENE_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, 2 min) after which the process is stopped and restarted on the next run; requests go one at a time; the child sees only system and `AI_SCENE_*`/`HF_*` variables. `/api/health` reports `cullingSceneEngine` (status word, fixed reason, never a gate). Down (or still starting past `AI_SCENE_READY_WAIT_MS`, 60 s), a run skips scenes and the aesthetic score but still judges framing from the face boxes. The reading each photo gets is kept **on the photo** (`image_selection_upload_scene`, `upload.sceneCache`: one row per engine version, the others pruned by every run), and `scene/filter.js` reads it back as the client gallery's **Scene filter** - one more `where` condition on `gallery/repository.listPublishedImages` (`sceneWhere`, at or above the review's 0.4 confidence line, `SCENE_MIN_CONFIDENCE`), the readings of a scope listed by `gallery/repository.listSceneReadings` under the listing's own eligibility, shoot and People conditions, counted once per (photo, scene) by `sceneCounts` and named in the profile's order and words by `profile.scenesOf` (`gallery/service.listScenes`) - so a gallery never says more than the review would. A photo no culling run has read - one that went straight into Photo Selection - is read in the **background** the first time its gallery asks for scenes (`scene/backfill.js`: the gallery-eligible photos without a reading at the engine's current version, a page at a time and `MAX_BATCH` per engine request, through the same engine, `analysis/analyzer.loadPreviewBytes` and `repository.saveSceneCache` / `pruneSceneCache`, so a later culling run finds them read; one pass per project and at most two in the process at a time, a photo that cannot be read - its preview gone from the bucket - parked for an hour rather than fetched again on every poll, a pass that fails parking its project the same way, the engine started from here at most once per ten minutes when it is down, and nothing at all with the engine off or with the culling worker in another process, `AI_CULLING_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false`), and the list answers `processing: true` while there is more to come - the People filter's `ensureQueued` applied to scenes, behind its own per-address budget (`scenesLimiter`, 600 per 15 min); `test/public-gallery-scenes.test.js`, `test/public-gallery-scene-backfill.test.js` |
| `src/modules/ai-feature/shared/vectors.js` | Face vectors at rest and compared: base64 float32 in a TEXT column, cosine similarity, running centroids, confidence bands - MySQL 8 has no vector type, so this is done in Node one event at a time |
| `src/modules/image-selection/whatsapp/worker.js` | WhatsApp send queue |
| `src/modules/image-selection/whatsapp/group.service.js` | The project's WhatsApp group and the invite sent into it: name-matching against the coordinator's own groups (`sessionManager.listGroups`), one live project per group, a jid from the browser linked only when that person's WhatsApp answered for it, and a send through `sessionManager.sendMessageToGroup` that is **synchronous** — the socket lives in the API process and the send worker may not — and idempotent per publish version through the same notification table the Meta path writes |
| `src/workers/imageProcessingWorker.js` | Standalone worker process (`npm run worker:image`) |

Both workers run embedded in the API process by default; set
`IMAGE_PROCESSING_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false` or `WHATSAPP_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false` to run them separately.
The face worker (`src/workers/imageSelectionFaceWorker.js`, `npm run worker:faces`) does the same
behind `IMAGE_SELECTION_FACE_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false`, and starts nowhere at all unless the face
engine is switched on (`AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED=true`, or `IMAGE_SELECTION_FACE_RECOGNITION_ENABLED=true`).
The culling worker (`src/workers/aiCullingWorker.js`, `npm run worker:culling`) is embedded unless
`AI_CULLING_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false`; `AI_CULLING_CONCURRENCY` (1–8, default 2) is how many sessions
it analyzes at once. `AI_CULLING_ENABLED=false` refuses new runs (on by default; existing runs stay readable). Boot recovery for the image pipeline re-queues only PROCESSING photos the
server can read — a workstation-registered photo (`storage_root_id` set) waits for the desktop's
derivatives instead.

### Mounted API Surface

| Prefix | Module |
| --- | --- |
| `/api/auth` | Login, super-admin bootstrap (public) |
| `/api/client` | The client portal, for **client accounts only** (`requireClient`; staff are refused - the portal belongs to the CLIENT role outright, which holds no IAM role a permission could be granted through). `GET /project` is the client's own project, found from the account (`clientLogin`), never from an id in the request: its status, Event ID and the client's own name (`clientName`, as their invoice names them via `clientOwnName` - never the WhatsApp group name, the studio's internal code), the stages exactly as `GET /api/sales/projects/:id/stage` works them out (`projectStage`), cut to what a client is told - each stage's state and counts, the facts naming where, what, when and who (no converted value, converter, recorder or enquiry), each shoot as planned / finished with its day and its `progress` - the plan and four of the crew's steps as client keys (`ON_THE_WAY`, `REACHED`, `STARTED`, `COMPLETED`), each `{ done, at }`, plus `halt` (`PAUSED` / `HOLD`) while the crew has stopped the shoot; never the Pineapple meeting, Reached studio, Photo copy, the crew's step names or remarks, no update history - and each client gift's status and sent / received days when it is Applicable (columns from Client Gifts' `GIFTS`, status through its `readStatus`) - no money, remarks, phone numbers, quotation, or who sends the gift (the client's own money has routes of its own, below). The CRD document comes as a state (`notStarted`, `awaiting`, `inProgress`, `submitted`) with its fill link (`/crd/<publicToken>`) only while it is sent and not yet submitted - the link the studio already sends on WhatsApp - and `null` for a project whose CRD Document is Not Applicable (`APPLICABILITY_ITEMS`). `GET /payments` is the client's own money, added up exactly as Edit Project → Payments adds it (`listProjectPayments`): `summary` (`totalQuotation` = Initial Converted Value, `totalExtra` = approved extras, `totalSigned`, `totalReceived`), the signed `quotation` (`id`, `date`, `downloadable`) or `null`, the approved `extras` (`id`, `service`, `amount`, `approvedOn`), and every Received cash entry (`id` = receipt number, `category`, `date`, `amount`, `mode`) - never Spent entries, entry descriptions, remarks, who took the money, or extras still pending (the invoice PDF below is the studio's own, unchanged). `GET /quotation/pdf` is the signed quotation's PDF (`sendQuotationPdf`), 404 with no quotation; unlike the public link it is offered whatever the brand's Allow Client Download says and after the link has lapsed - a lapsed one is printed with a two-minute, one-quotation print pass (`services/quotationPrintPass.js`, header `x-quotation-print-pass`, accepted only by `publicQuotation`), so the public link stays lapsed and nothing is renewed. `GET /receipt?entry=<DBS-MR-…>` is one payment's invoice PDF (`receiptFor` + `renderReceiptPdf`, the file Cash Received downloads), only for money Received on the client's own project - anything else is 404; a query rather than a path segment so it stays one line in `CLIENT_ROUTES`. `GET /reviews` is what is waiting for the client: a published Image Selection gallery not yet submitted and not lapsed (`expiryOf`), with the items still to choose, and each Album Proofing album in `CLIENT_REVIEW` with a live client publication (version, round, `CLIENT_STATUS_LABELS`) - each with its client link (`galleryBaseUrl()/<code>`, `FRONTEND_URL/p/<code>`) and PIN, the ones the studio already sends on WhatsApp. Read-only: it never revokes or re-codes as the studio-side `status()` does. The review itself happens on those existing pages. The two PDFs share a per-account download limit (20 per 15 minutes). Rate-limited per account, every answer `Cache-Control: no-store`. Each route is also listed in `CLIENT_ROUTES`, or `protect` refuses a client token before it arrives. [modules/client/portal](src/modules/client/portal/controller.js) |
| `/api/admin/branch-admin-permissions` | The **Admin Permission** set: the one permission matrix every role marked `isBranchManager` carries on top of what that role grants in its own right. `GET` reads it, `PUT` replaces it whole, both under `ADMIN / Role Management` — `READ` to look, `READ_WRITE` to save. Guarded by role management rather than a permission of its own because it *is* role management, and a larger act than editing one role: saving it changes what every branch administrator may do without any of those roles being opened. One set for the studio, not one per role — two branch administrators in two branches do the same job in two places, and what differs between them is the branch list on their own role, which this does not touch. It is merged in `IAMService.getUserPermissions` through the same most-permissive rule as every other grant, so it can only ever widen somebody: a role already holding a permission at Read / Write keeps it when the set names it at Read. Empty until somebody saves one, and replaced whole on save so a permission cleared in the editor actually goes |
| `/api/admin/branches` | The branch register behind Settings → Organization → Branches — `GET /` the whole register (disabled branches included), `POST /` and `PATCH /:id` to add and edit one. `requireModule("SETTINGS", "Organization")` on the router, so this is an administrator's job while *choosing* a branch stays everybody's through `/api/hrm/branches`. There is no delete: a branch is disabled with `PATCH { isActive: false }`, because a year of quotations, every user's home branch and every asset's location go on pointing at it. Everything past the name is optional — **including the GSTIN and the PAN**, which were required until a branch that trades before its registration comes through turned out to be unaddable; both are still checked against their real shapes once there is something in the box, and a code, name or GSTIN another branch already holds comes back 409 with a message per field. `bankAccounts` is the **list** of accounts the branch is paid into - rows in `branch_bank_account` since 20261007090000, four columns on the branch before that, which held exactly one. At least one, each row carrying all four fields (holder, bank, account number, IFSC), at most ten, and the list's order is written to `position`, which is the order the client's payment page prints the cards in. The list is **replaced** on an edit that mentions it and left alone by one that does not, inside the branch's own transaction. An account number another branch holds comes back 409 as `fieldErrors.bankAccounts[<row>].accountNumber` - the same rule the single account was held to, said against the row - and the same number twice in one list is refused by the schema. It is **not** unique in the database: the check has only ever been the application's, and a unique index would be a migration that fails on production data. These accounts are also the studio's only bank-account register: `services/branchBankAccounts.js` builds the "received into" list the cash book, payment requests and a project's advance offer, labelled bank · last four digits · branch and capped at the 120 characters those records store it in, since each keeps the label as text. `qrCodeUrl` is the branch's payment QR: the browser sends the compressed data URI, the controller puts it through `storeImage` into `branches/qr-codes` and stores the link, and an existing link sent back by an edit passes through without being re-uploaded. `upiIds` is the **list** of UPI IDs beside it — a JSON column, the studio's own order, optional and empty by default (`[]` is a branch that takes no UPI payment, and there is no second way to say it), each entry trimmed, stored as typed and checked only for the `address@handle` shape every UPI ID shares, blanks and repeats of one handle dropped rather than refused, at most ten, and a single string read as a list of one. The read always answers a list, so a branch written before the column cannot answer NULL to a form. Printed on the client's quotation payment page (`/api/public/quotations/:token/payment`), one row each, beside the QR. The External URLs' `locationUrl` and `googleReviewUrl` are optional text columns and the social media links are `customSocialLinks`, a JSON list of up to ten `{ label, url }` the studio adds (read as `[]` when unset; a blank link or a repeated label is refused; there are no fixed social media columns). A blank link is sent as null; every link is checked loosely but with nothing other than http(s) allowed before the address (so `javascript:` is refused), and stored with `https://` in front when typed without a scheme |
| `/api/admin/activity-logs` | Administration → User Activity Logs, read only. `GET /?from=&to=&username=&module=&action=&method=&clientIp=&status=&page=1&pageSize=50&sortBy=createdAt&sortOrder=desc` → `{ rows, total, page, pageSize }` (`total` counted on page 1 only, `null` after; `page × pageSize` over 10,000 is a 400 "Narrow the period or filters to see older lines."), each row only what the grid draws, `{ id (string), createdAt, username, module, action, method, clientIp, statusCode, statusText }`, read with no join; `GET /:id` → one line in full for the details panel, `{ …those, userId, userName, path, userAgent, durationMs, branchId, branchName }`, 404 for an id that does not exist or is outside the reader's branch scope (the same scope as the list); `username` is the snapshot written at the time, `userName` the person's current name through the relation (null once deleted), `statusText` the HTTP reason phrase. Text filters are contains (case-insensitive by collation; `username` also matches the current name), `method` exact, `status` a code (`404`), leading digits (`4` → every 4xx) or reason-phrase text (`Not Found`); `pageSize` max 200; `sortBy` one of createdAt, username, module, action, method, clientIp, statusCode. `GET /export?format=csv|xlsx&timeZone=` with the list's own filters, period and sort → the whole filtered log as a file (not a page of it), 13 columns - the grid's seven plus full name, branch, route path, status code, user agent and duration - Log time in the reader's `timeZone`; any cell starting `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or return is written with a leading apostrophe so it cannot run as a formula; `.xlsx` is a real Office Open XML workbook zipped with `archiver` (no spreadsheet library), header bold and frozen, numbers typed; over 100,000 matching lines is a 400 asking to narrow the period (`admin/activity-logs/export.js`). `GET /options` → `{ modules, methods }`: methods the fixed POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE (no read is recorded), modules the distinct utilities in the reader's reach over the last 30 days, cached 5 minutes per reach. `requireModule("ADMIN", "User Activity Logs")` on the router, mounted ahead of `/api/admin`. Branch scope means **people homed in** the branches of the roles granting that permission (`reachableBranchIds`; a line's branch is its actor's home branch at the time), and a branch-limited reader never sees a line a Super Admin made; a Super Admin sees every line, including those with no branch (a failed sign-in naming no account) |
| `/api/settings/activity-log-purge` | Settings → Activity Log Purge: deleting the User Activity Logs' older lines by hand. `GET /?months=1|2|3|6` → `{ months, cutoff, count, choices }`, how many lines are older than the cutoff (`months` calendar months back from now, held to a short month's end - the log screen's "Last N months" rule), `POST / { months }` → `{ months, cutoff, removed }` deletes them in batches of 5,000 through `deleteActivityLogsBefore` (shared with the daily retention sweep); any other `months` is a 400. "3 months" **keeps** the last three months and deletes everything before. A custom range instead takes `from` and `to` (ISO moments, in the query for the preview, the body for the delete): `GET` → `{ from, to, count }`, `POST` → `{ from, to, removed }`, deleting every line between them, both included, through `deleteActivityLogsBetween`; From after To, a From in the future, a non-ISO value, or both `months` and a range at once is a 400, and a To past now is held at now. Studio-wide, not narrowed by branch. `requireModule("SETTINGS", "Activity Log Purge")`: Read previews, Read / Write deletes; the POST is itself recorded in the activity log, newer than anything it removed |
| `/api/hrm` | Master data, employees, onboarding invites, separations. Reads of the **reference lists** need only a session — departments and branches feed pickers all over the app, and gating those on HRM would lock an administrator out of their own screens — while every write asks for `HRM / Employee Management` `READ_WRITE`. The employee rows (`GET /employees`, the next id, the import template, alternative candidates), the separations and the invite list are not reference data and each carries `HRM / Employee Management` `READ` of its own; Saving a separation with a Proposed Last Working Date today or later starts a notice period (`servesNotice` / `separationEffect`): status and login stay, `relieved_at` stays null, and `separations/relieve.js` relieves the day after the date - status lands, `relieved_at` is stamped and the login closes, so `protect` refuses the token on its next request; a date already past relieves at once. A Resignation or Mutual Separation served on notice is marked `salary_held` (from its resignation date) while Payroll rules → Hold salary during notice is on (`payroll-register/noticeHold.js`); `POST /employees/:id/reactivate` (Move to Active, a rejoin) reopens the login on a new password from `newTemporaryPassword` (`services/loginCredentials.js`), sets `mustChangePassword`, revokes the account's `auth_session` rows, emails it with `sendEmployeeCredentialsEmail` after the commit, and returns `credentials` - the password itself only to a caller holding `ADMIN / User Management` `READ_WRITE`; A review in an employee's `reviews` carries `reviewType` - `CYCLIC` (default; may award an increment and sets the next review date) or `SPOT` (outcome and remarks only: saved with no increment and no next date, and left out of `reviewTotals`, so it moves neither the salary nor the schedule; `employee_review.review_type`); `GET /employees/directory` is the one session-only employee read, and answers id, Employee ID and name alone for the asset and client-storage pickers. A list kept per brand (`scopedBy: "vertical"` in `master-data/definitions.js` — the quotation Categories, resource `religions`) files each row under a brand code, requires one on create, and answers one brand's rows to `?vertical=DUM_DUM`. A master-data row is keyed by a `code` as well as a name, and `POST` **mints the code from the name** when one is not given (`codeFromName` in `master-data/definitions.js`): no screen shows the code, so a caller that sends a name is enough. Settings → Field Settings sent exactly that and was refused for three weeks because the schema demanded a code the screen does not have. An explicit code still wins, which is what Accounts needs for a key derived from the bank and branch rather than from the display name Each employee row from `GET /employees` also carries `clientFeedback` - the Production Completion Feedbacks naming them (`project_feedback_employee`), Appreciation as `Shine` and Not Satisfied as `Shadow`, oldest first with its date, and the Feedback ID, project and Event ID only when the reader also holds `PROJECT / Feedback` (Read) - which the review timeline draws beside the salary reviews without touching them. The **offboarding invite** is the resignation's own form, mounted here: `GET /offboarding-invites` and `GET /offboarding-invites/candidates` carry `HRM / Employee Management` `READ` of their own (candidates being the working employees with no open form; each listed form also carries `stage`, where its leaver stands - an Approved form reads Serving Notice, the status they left on, or Withdrawn once its separation is removed - `offboardingStage`), and `POST /offboarding-invites`, `POST /:id/send-email`, `PATCH /:id/cancel`, `PATCH /:id` (the notice period, the note and the expiry of a form still **open** - never who it is for, which is a cancel and a fresh form, and never after it is filled in, where Send Back is the move), `POST /:id/review` and `POST /:id/send-back` take the router's `READ_WRITE`, as do `DELETE /offboarding-invites/:id` and `DELETE /offboarding-invites/bulk { ids }` (the bulk one also `ADMIN / Table Select All`). Both run `deleteInviteRecord`: any status, reach-checked at write level, the letter removed with it, and a separation the form recorded left alone. Its four messages are the studio's wording, in `message_template` behind `SETTINGS / Message Template` and read with `HRM / Employee Management` (already in that router's `mayRead`): `EMPLOYEE_OFFBOARDING_FORM`, `_REMINDER`, `_APPROVED` and `_NOT_ACCEPTED`, seeded by migration `20261027090000_offboarding_form_message_template` with the wording the screens shipped with, which is also each one's fallback. HR states the **notice period in days** when raising it; the employee answers the public form with the **resignation date** (today by default), a **reason** from `RESIGNATION_REASONS` - the same list `separationFormConfig.separationReasonsByType.Resignation` offers HR, enforced with `z.enum` so the column stays reportable - and any comments, over a read-back of their own record (employee ID, name, designation, teams, reporting manager, joining date, official email: `autoFilledFrom`, linked names preferred over the text copies). The **proposed last working day** is `lastWorkingDateFrom(resignationDate, noticePeriodDays)` - the first day of notice counted as served - computed and stored by the server and **never read off the request**: the browser draws the same sum only to show it, so nobody agrees to a date they cannot see and nobody can post a shorter one. Leaving earlier is HR's to set on the separation itself. A submission that is nearly right - the wrong date, a reason that does not match what they said, no letter - goes back rather than being refused: `POST /:id/send-back { remark }` (the remark is required and is what they are shown) sets the status to **Sent Back**, clears only `submitted_at`, pushes the link's last day out to at least a week, and emails them; their own answers stay on the row and come back with the verify step (`answers`), so the form is an edit. Nothing on the employee changes until HR **approves**: that calls `separations/controller.js` `recordSeparation` - the same function `POST /separations` calls, so an approved form and a hand-written separation take one path - and `separationEffect` relieves them at once - the status, `relieved_at` and the closed login, as for any separation saved now. `POST /employees/:id/certificates/:kind` (and `/pdf`) fills a certificate from the record - `experience` with the `duties` sent, only for somebody InActive or serving notice; `employment` with nothing sent, only for somebody Active (working, not serving notice) - and only one the caller may change (`scopedEmployee` at Read / Write); nothing is stored. |
