| `/api/hrm/attendance` | Employee Attendance. `GET /today`, `POST /login`, `POST /logout` for the day itself (the logout answers with `day`, what the day was marked as, and `GET /logout-preview` says what logging out now would mark it as - both by `dayReadingAt`, the register's own rules, for the caller's own login only) — the login time is the server's clock, never the browser's, and the one-per-day record is enforced by a unique index on `(employee_id, work_date)`. Both captures require a live selfie, a position **with** its accuracy, and a `captureToken` from `POST /capture-token { type: CHECK_IN \| CHECK_OUT }`, taken when the camera opens: it is bound to the login and the capture type, lives 120 seconds, and is signed with a key derived from `JWT_SECRET` so it is never accepted as a session (`captureToken.js`). A stale, foreign or missing token answers `400 CAPTURE_EXPIRED`. `GET /summary?from&to` returns one row per calendar day with the status and totals already decided, so no arithmetic is repeated in the browser. A login is open for 18 hours from `last_login_at` (`SESSION_VALID_HOURS` / `sessionExpired` in `attendanceRules.js` - the one place the number lives, which the server's refusal and the dashboard's notice are both built from, and which `test/attendance-session-hours.test.js` holds the browser's own copy to): `GET /today`, logout and breaks act on `service.currentSession` - today's session, or yesterday's while it is inside them - and past them an owed day reads `Absent` (a holiday, weekly off or leave day reads as the calendar says), worked out on read with no scheduler. An `INCORRECT_LOGIN` request (shown as **Correction**) carries `requestedStatus`; the server reads `currentStatus` itself (`GET /requests/correction-day?date=` gives the form the same reading and its options), and the final approval writes `attendance_status_override` - one row per person per day, read by every classifier through `override` - inside the decision's transaction, refusing with 409 a day that reads differently since. `GET /overview?from&to&branchId&employeeIds` is the administrator's Dashboard: every employee's figures over a range, and `timeOff: { casualLeave, permissions, compOffs }` - who used approved Casual Leave (working days, Loss of Pay left out), permissions (count and minutes) and comp-off days off in it, one row each, the most first (`timeOffUsed`) (at most 366 days, and cut at today - a working day still to come would otherwise read as Absent; `employeeIds` a comma-separated list of ids), totals, a row per day and per branch, and each card's change against the period of the same length before it - one tally of fixed queries run twice, never a query per day. **Administrators only** (`isAdministrator`, checked in the controller like the calculator), because it reads the whole studio at once; a bad date, branch or employee list is a 400. `/requests` raises leave, permission, on-duty and **comp-off** — a day off for a holiday or weekly off worked: `workedDate` must be a holiday or weekly off that is over, with a check-in on it, not already claimed by a pending or approved comp-off (re-checked inside the write under a lock on that day's attendance row), and the day off (`fromDate` = `toDate`) a later working day not already on leave, with no expiry. **A day off costs one whole day of credit**, and a worked day earns what its approvers granted (1, 0.5 or 0), so the claim names one worked day or two: `workedPairDate` is the second half day, and the two must make exactly one day off between them as `compOffCredits.claimableUnits` pairs them — half a day on its own is refused, saying so. Both columns are locked and re-checked inside the write, and both days read CLAIMED in the ledger, so one half is never spent twice; the earlier day is always stored in `workedDate` (`compOffDayColumns`), the day off falls after the later and expires on the earlier one's clock; an approved one reads as On leave on the day taken and moves the person's leads to their cover like a leave. `/requests` and `GET /settings` sit above the router's guard and accept `HRM / Employee Attendance` **or** `HRM / Requests` (the HRM → Request screen). `/requests/:id/{approve,reject}` is for the three Leave Approval slots on the requester's employee record — a leave and a comp-off need all of them — or, on a **permission**, the one Permission Approval slot, falling back to those three where nobody is named in it — or an `HRM / Attendance Approval` holder, with `/requests/:id/send-back` the third answer (a remark required): it clears every approval row and leaves the request `SENT_BACK` with the raiser. A request's status is **computed** by `outcomeOf(approvals, type)` after every change to an approval row and never assigned by an approver - the six are `PENDING`, `SENT_BACK`, `APPROVED`, `REJECTED`, `REVERTED`, `DELETED`, plus legacy `CANCELLED` rows that are never migrated. An approver may change their own **rejection to an approval, never an approval to a rejection** (`mayAnswer` in `approvals.js`), and nobody answers a request they raised, however they came to be on its list (`answerableBy`). `PATCH /requests/:id` (edit, same body and same `assertRequestAllowed` gate as raising, clearing every answer and re-resolving the approvers of today), `POST /requests/:id/revert` and `DELETE /requests/:id` (a new or edited request is refused with 409 `REQUEST_CLASH` and the request in the way - `clash` - where a pending or approved leave, comp-off or permission already covers that day, a leave and a permission included; a **leave or comp-off on a day the person is on a shoot** is refused too (`shootClashProblem`), naming each clashing shoot - its id and name, the day, the place and what they are on it for - and the project's Production Manager to ask, since freeing a seat is re-planning the shoot. All three ways of being on it count (a service seat, the production team, the shoot's Production Coordinator); a **permission** is not checked, being an hour inside a day. The day is decided on `localIsoDate`, not on the stored instant: `project_shoot.date_created` carries a time of day, so a shoot written at 18:30Z is the next day in the studio's zone and a UTC comparison refused the wrong one (which sets `DELETED` and deletes nothing) are the employee's own; `DELETE /requests/bulk` is the one delete that removes rows — a selection, by a **Super Admin** alone (the SUPER_ADMIN column or an active SUPER_ADMIN role, `services/administrator.js` `isSuperAdmin`), any status, with its approvals, history and notifications, an approved Correction's corrected day kept. The employee's own are each held to the raiser by `service.assertRaiserMayAct` and each re-checked under `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the request row inside its transaction, as `decide` is. An **approved** `CORRECTION` or `INCORRECT_LOGIN` is frozen to all three (`frozenByItsOwnApproval`): approving one has already written the day, and nothing undoes that. The old `/requests/:id/cancel` is gone, and the old withdraw no longer deletes the row. `GET /requests/approvers` answers who this login's own requests go to, keyed by type (`{ LEAVE: [{ id, employeeName, employeeCode }], ... }`) from the same `approversFor` a request is routed with, for HRM → Request's Raise Request form to show; HR sets them on Employee Preference. `GET|PUT /settings` holds the working day, including `attendanceStartDate` (`YYYY-MM-DD` or null; before it no day is counted — the summary, totals, register, Dashboard, salary calculator, missed-logout notices, Nebula's attendance tool, regularization and comp-off all start from the later of it and the joining date); the **holiday calendar moved out** to `/api/settings/holidays`, since a company-declared holiday is a studio setting rather than an attendance decision — attendance still reads the same table when it judges a day. Mounted ahead of the master-data router, which would otherwise let any signed-in session read its GETs `POST /login` takes `workMode` office / work-from-home / client-site / **flexible** (Flexible Shift): flexible is refused 403 unless the employee's `allowFlexibleShift` is ticked, and `GET /today` says `flexibleShiftAllowed` so the Login box offers it. A flexible day is judged on its hours alone (`attendanceRules.js`) - never Late or Early exit, a full day Present - and an allowed employee with no login reads Yet to log in all of today (`notInYet`). A leave's `days` in every request answer are its working days - a weekly off or declared holiday in the range is not counted (`leaveDayCounter`, one read per page) - and `GET /requests/leave-balance?from=&to=` answers `preview.days` with `preview.skipped` (`[{ date, reason }]`, `leaveSpan`) even with no Casual Leave set. **Ledgers** (`ledgers.js`): `casual_leave_ledger`, `permission_ledger` and `comp_off_ledger` hold what the rules come to per employee, for history and audit - `rebuildEmployee` rewrites one employee's rows (after a request is raised, edited, decided, sent back, reverted or deleted, and after a logout, via `refreshSoon`, after the response), `rebuildAll` everybody's (after Attendance Rules or Settings → Holidays change, `refreshAllSoon`); `npm run ledgers:rebuild` (`scripts/rebuild-attendance-ledgers.js`) fills them once, writing those three tables only; asks for the same rebuild are coalesced in-process (one running, at most one queued after it), each rebuild locks the employee row (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`) so two never interleave, and an archived or excluded employee's rows are cleared. A LEAVE request carries `leaveType` (`CL` | `LOP`, defaulting to `CL`; `attendance_request.leave_type`, earlier leave backfilled CL): only CL takes from the Casual Leave balance (`CASUAL_LEAVE_ONLY`), and `calculator.periodCalculation` counts an LOP leave day as unpaid (`lopLeaveDates`). **Casual Leave**: `PUT /settings` also takes `casualLeavePerMonth` (half days, 0 = off), `casualLeaveMonthlyCarry`, `casualLeaveYearCarryMax` and `permissionsPerPeriod` (0 = no limit) and `permissionPeriod` (`CALENDAR_MONTH` / `PAY_CYCLE`; `service.permissionUsage` counts pending, sent back and approved permissions in the period, a request past the limit is refused, and `GET /requests/permission-usage?date=&excludeRequestId=` answers the caller's own `{ limit, used, label, from, to }`), `compOffExpiryDays` (whole days a comp-off can be used after the day worked, 0 = never; `compOffExpiresOn`, enforced in `compOffDatesProblem`, and `GET /requests/comp-off-options` answers `{ options, pendingHalf }` — one option per **day off** claimable, each with the one or two worked `days` that pay for it and its `expiresOn`, read from the balance worked out now (`ledgers.compOffBalanceNow`) so the list, the ledger and the refusal cannot disagree; `pendingHalf` is an odd half day with nothing to pair with yet), `casualLeavePeriod` (`CALENDAR_MONTH`, or `PAY_CYCLE`: a day counts in the payroll month whose cycle holds it, `service.casualLeavePeriods` over the payroll register's `settingsBook`/`periodFor`, and the year turns with January's payroll); `GET /requests/leave-balance?from=&to=&excludeRequestId=` (requests guard, the caller's own employee) answers the balance, the pending leave days and a preview of how many of the asked working days would be Loss of Pay. The balance is `casualLeaveLedger` (`attendanceRules.js`) over approved LEAVE on working days, read by `service.casualLeaveFor`; `calculator.periodCalculation` adds the days beyond it (`tally.leaveBeyondBalance`) to `lossOfPayDays`, which payroll brings in as Attendance Leaves. **The daily WhatsApp report's settings** live under `/report-group` (`GET`; `PUT` the group name, `PUT /send-at`, `PUT /senders `{ mode, senderUserId, fallbackUserIds, userIds }`` (the report's `whatsapp_sender_policy` row, `ATTENDANCE_REPORT`), `DELETE` to clear - an administrator's; `PUT /enabled { enabled }` switches the report off or on, `attendance_report_enabled`, true by default: off, `reportGroup.runOnce` answers "off" and sends nothing, keeping the time, the senders and the group) and `/report-group/session` for a chosen sender's own WhatsApp pairing. |
| `/api/hrm/referrals` | Employee referrals: `GET` / `PUT /settings` is what a referral pays, and `GET /` is the register with each referral's instalments. Guarded as a whole by `HRM / Employee Referral` — the permission that opens the Employee Referral screen these serve — so a GET needs `READ` and everything else `READ_WRITE`. **An instalment is paid one of two ways, each settling it once.** `PATCH /payouts/:payoutId` (`{ status: "Paid", paidOn?, remarks? }`, `READ_WRITE`) marks one paid by hand for money handed over outside payroll - only a Pending instalment, never a stopped one, written conditionally on it still being Pending so it cannot race payroll (409 otherwise), with no `payroll_entry_id`; payroll then leaves it out, since it carries only what is still Pending. Otherwise it is paid through payroll as the referrer's **Referral Bonus** on Payroll → Main Page in its due month (`referralBonusDueIn`), and paying that month records it (`settleReferralBonus`, inside the row's own transaction, only an instalment still Pending, and only when the recomputed figure still matches the row's stored `referral_bonus` - otherwise 409, refresh the month first). The instalment keeps the row that paid it (`employee_referral_payout.payroll_entry_id`, `SetNull`), so setting the month back to Pending un-settles exactly its own instalments (`unsettleReferralBonus`) and a paid row reads its Referral IDs through the link rather than from what is still due. `DELETE /:id` and `DELETE /bulk` (the latter also behind `ADMIN / Table Select All`, `mountBulkDelete`) delete a referral and its instalments - only one in the reader's reach (404 otherwise) and never one with an instalment Paid (409), the rule `syncEmployeeReferral` keeps when Referred By is cleared. `GET /`'s `status` filter takes only a stored status - `Stopped` is worked out on the way out. **Either person having left:** read live off both statuses (`countsAsWorking`, never a stored Cancelled) by `stoppedReasonFor` - the hire, because the bonus is for them staying, and the referrer, because the money is theirs and a cleared `referrer_employee_id` leaves nobody to pay - so a stopped referral is off the register entirely while nothing was paid, and settled with its unpaid instalments reported `Stopped` (and a `stoppedReason` of `REFERRED_LEFT` / `REFERRER_LEFT` / `BOTH_LEFT`) once something was; while both are working, a half-paid referral stays Outstanding — and an instalment is brought into payroll only where both the referrer and the employee they referred are working. Mounted ahead of `/api/hrm`, which claims everything else under that prefix **Whose referrals:** the register answers the studio's usual rule on the referrer — yours if you made it, if somebody reporting to you made it, or if you entered it on their behalf — and `ADMIN / Branch Administration` adds every referral whose *referrer* works in a branch the role reaches. The branch is read live through `referrer.branchId` rather than copied onto the row: `EmployeeReferral` has no `branch_id` and should not grow one, because a referral's branch is the referrer's and a copy goes stale the day somebody transfers. `PATCH /payouts/:payoutId` is held to the same rows and answers 404 out of scope — hiding a row from the register is not protection while its id can still be sent to the write that says money left the studio. The settings either side of it are studio-wide and answer to the permission alone |
| `/api/hrm/payroll-recoveries` | HRM → Payroll → Recovery, behind `HRM / Payroll Recovery` (`requireModule`: GET Read, the rest Read / Write). `GET /` is the register, `GET /employees` the working employees the form picks from, `POST /` records one (minting `RECO-ID-0001`, `identifiers.js` PAYROLL_RECOVERY, inside the create's transaction), `PUT /:id` corrects it (its ID never changes), `DELETE /:id` with `{ reason }` takes it off the register and keeps the row (`deletedAt`, who, why). Only the employee, total, start month/year, months and reason are stored; the monthly deduction (total ÷ months), start date (1st of the start month), end date (start + months) and status (Completed once every instalment month's payroll on the Main Page is Paid, Active until then; `instalmentsDeducted` counts them - from `paidMonthsOf`, never the calendar) are worked out on read. Narrowed by branch reach: a recovery sits in its employee's branch, and a reader reaches the branches of the roles granting them `HRM / Payroll Recovery` (`iamService.reachableBranchIds`) - the register, the employee list and every write; one outside reach answers 404. [modules/hrm/payroll-recovery](src/modules/hrm/payroll-recovery/service.js) A recovery converted from advances on Advance Salary names them (`advanceIds`), keeps its employee and total (400 on a change), moves its start or months only across unpaid months (409 otherwise), re-totalling the unpaid Main Page rows of the old and new run (`syncRecoveryMonths`), and is not removed (409); `POST /:id/undo-conversion` (also needing `HRM / Payroll Advance` Read / Write, every advance in the caller's Advance reach) removes it, kept with why, and hands its advances back to Advance Salary and their months - refused (409) once a month either covers is Paid. |
| `/api/hrm/payroll-policy-breaches` | HRM → Payroll → Policy Breach, behind `HRM / Payroll Policy Breach` (`requireModule`: GET Read, the rest Read / Write) - a fine for breaking a studio policy, taken from salary over months exactly as a recovery is, and **not** the register's typed Policy Breach Leaves (loss-of-pay days). `GET /`, `GET /employees` (Recovery's list, narrowed by this permission's reach), `GET /:id/history`, `POST /` (minting `PB-ID-0001`, `identifiers.js` PAYROLL_POLICY_BREACH), `PUT /:id`, `DELETE /:id { reason }` (row kept). Stored and worked out as a recovery: only the employee, amount, start month/year, months and reason are kept; the run, monthly deduction, end date, status and history reuse `payroll-recovery/service.js` (`scheduleOf`, `paidMonthsOf`, `checkRunNotPaid`, `paymentHistory`). Refused (409) where a new run would cover a month already paid or frozen, or while a frozen month holds the employee. No conversion from advances. Its month's instalment is the Main Page's `policyBreachDeduction` (`policyBreachDueIn`), skippable there as `policy-breach`. `deductBy` (`AMOUNT`, the default, or `LOP`; `deduct_by`) chooses how it is taken: an LOP breach sends `totalDays` (whole or half days, `total_days`) instead of `totalAmount` (stored 0), split over its months in half days by `lopDaysSplit` (extra halves to the earliest months); each month's share is the register row's `policyBreachLopDays` (`policy_breach_lop_days`, brought in with `policyBreachDueIn().lopDays`), added to Total Leaves. Its list row carries `lopDaysPerMonth`, and `GET /:id/history` answers in days (`deductBy`, `totalDays`). [modules/hrm/payroll-policy-breach](src/modules/hrm/payroll-policy-breach/service.js) |
| `/api/hrm/payroll-advances` | HRM → Payroll → Advance Salary, behind `HRM / Payroll Advance` (`requireModule`: GET Read, the rest Read / Write). `GET /` is the register, `GET /employees` the working employees the form picks from (Recovery's own list, narrowed by this permission's reach), `POST /` records one (minting `ADV-ID-0001`, `identifiers.js` PAYROLL_ADVANCE, inside the create's transaction), `PUT /:id` corrects it (its ID never changes), `DELETE /:id` with `{ reason }` takes it off the register and keeps the row (`deletedAt`, who, why). Only the date given, employee, amount, deduction month/year and reason are stored; the status (Pending until the payroll it comes off - its month, past any skip - is Paid on the Main Page, Deducted from then; from `paidMonthsOf`, never the calendar) is worked out on read. An advance is taken back in full as that month's **Salary Advance** on the payroll register (`advanceDueIn`): every write re-totals the affected month's payroll row if it exists and is not Paid, and a write touching a month whose row is already Paid is refused (409). Narrowed by branch reach through the permission; one outside reach answers 404. [modules/hrm/payroll-advance](src/modules/hrm/payroll-advance/service.js) **Convert to Recovery**: `POST /convert-to-recovery { advanceIds, startMonth, startYear, months, reason }` turns one employee's live, unconverted advances into one recovery (minting `RECO-ID-...`, total = their sum) and links each advance to it (`converted_recovery_id`, `converted_at`) - a converted advance is the recovery's: no longer listed by `GET /`, never taken back in its own month (`advanceDueIn`, the register's `advanceSelect`), and not edited or removed (409). It also needs `HRM / Payroll Recovery` Read / Write (`checkPermission` on the route), the advances in the caller's Advance reach and the employee in their Recovery reach; it refuses (409) an advance month or a recovery month already Paid, and re-totals the unpaid Main Page rows of both in the same transaction (`syncPayroll`, `syncRecoveryMonths`). Undoing it is the recovery's: `POST /api/hrm/payroll-recoveries/:id/undo-conversion`. |
| Project Activity | `project_activity` - every change made to a project from Projects (area `PROJECT`) or Pre Production (area `PRE_PRODUCTION`), written beside the change by `services/projectActivity.js` (`logProjectActivity`, never failing the change; `logAfterSuccess` for a route whose handler another screen shares - gift, storage, WhatsApp group). Production keeps its own per shoot (`project_production_activity`). Both are read as part of the project's **flow** (`services/projectFlow.js` `buildProjectFlow`): one list `[{ id, stage, at, title, detail, by, shoot }]`, newest first, capped at 1,000 (500 per source), merging ENQUIRY (the lead, its CRM activity log, call logs and meetings - `SALES / CRM Leads` Read and inside the reader's lead scope; a change to a phone, email, address, link or attachment field is "<Label> changed" with no values), QUOTATION (every version for the enquiry - `SALES / Quotation Management`, narrowed by the register's own `narrowQuotations`), BOOKING (booked, sale edited, sent back - no extra permission), PAYMENTS (`ACCOUNTS / Cash Management`: cash entries inside Cash Management's own scope - `CASH_SCOPE_COLUMNS` through `read.service.js` `scopeWhere` - extra quotations requested and decided inside their own screen's project reach under `PROJECT / Projects`, and the advance line), PROJECT (project activity, stage updates, applicability changes - `PROJECT / Projects` or `Show All Projects`), PRE_PRODUCTION (its activity, follow-ups included - `PROJECT / Pre Production`), PRODUCTION (each shoot's log within the reader's Production board - `PROJECT / Production`), IMAGE_SELECTION (the linked gallery project: created, published, opened, submitted, revoked, downloaded - `IMAGE_SELECTION / Project Management`), FEEDBACK (within `feedbackScope` - `PROJECT / Feedback`) and GIFTS (sent / received - `PROJECT / Client Gifts`); a stage whose permission the reader lacks is left out; the permissions are read once per request (`getUserPermissions`). Read at `GET /api/sales/projects/:id/activity` (the register's readers, `REGISTER_READERS`, then `projectAccessWhere` - 404 outside reach) and `GET /api/project-management/pre-production/projects/:projectId/activity` (the board's own reach: its Pre Production Coordinator, or whoever sees every project). |
| `/api/project-management/{pre-production,production}/project-status` | `POST { projectIds, status, remarks }` - a project's overall Pre Production or Production status set by hand - PLANNED, HOLD or REJECTED - or `null` back to automatic; Hold and Rejected need remarks, Planned takes none. Behind each board's own `requireModule` (Read / Write for a POST). Each project must be the caller's - its Pre Production Coordinator or Production Manager - unless they see every project (`Show All Projects` Read / Write, or no hierarchy); the rest are counted (`{ updated, refused, missing }`). Writes `sales_project.pre_production_status*` / `production_status*` and nothing else - never a shoot (`modules/project-management/departmentStatus.js`). |
| `/api/accounts/scheduled-payments` | Accounts Manager's Due / Overdue / On Hold tabs, behind the screen's own `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` (`requireModule`: GET Read, the rest Read / Write), narrowed to the projects that grant reaches (`projectAccessWhere` with `req.permission`). `GET /` the live `project_payment_schedule` rows still owed (a payment received in full is left out - it is its cash entry under Received), each with its project, `amount`, `received`, `pending`, `status` (On Hold / Overdue / Due - five days, or a part payment's remainder - / Active, worked out on read in `service.js`), and the project's `projectAccountsStatus`; `GET /:id` one with its receipts. `PATCH /:id/hold` `{ hold, remarks }` (remarks required to hold). `POST /:id/clear` `{ entry }` records the whole pending amount as a Received cash entry (`recordCashEntry`, then `cash_entry.payment_schedule_id`); `POST /:id/partial` `{ paidAmount, pendingDueDate, entry }` records the part paid, fixes this payment at what was received and adds a remainder row (`split_from_id`) due on that date. Both go through `settleInTx` (`FULL` / `PARTIAL`), which `POST /api/accounts/cash-entries` (body `settles: { paymentScheduleId, mode, pendingDueDate }`), the booking advance and the CSV import (Scheduled Payment, Balance Due Date) call too, in the entry's own transaction. `GET /project/:projectId/open` lists a project's payments still open, for Add Entry's Settles Payment (the project reached through Cash Management or on the caller's board). Hold, clear and part payment each lock the payment's row and read it again in one transaction, so a second press is refused rather than paid twice. A project's schedule itself is read and saved at `GET`/`PUT`/`POST /api/sales/projects/:id/payment-schedule` (`paymentSchedule.js`; PUT under `plansAProject` + `editsAStartedProject` for Start / Edit Project, POST under Sales Manager right after Create Project, and only for a project with no schedule yet - 409 otherwise); every payment is the save's to change or withdraw, worked ones included - withdrawing a paid one unlinks its receipts (`cash_entry.payment_schedule_id` set to NULL) and never removes them, and a payment fixed at an amount stays fixed while the share sent comes to it. The GET also returns `ledger` (`projectLedger` in `scheduled-payments/service.js`): the Updated Total Amount (Initial Converted Value + published Sale Extras + approved Extra Quotations), money received, Client Refunds, `balance` (never below zero, `overpaid` apart), money received on no payment, and each saved payment's id and what it has received (the screen offers Receive on those) - read from the cash entries every time, never stored. `services/projectHolds.js` `projectPaymentsDue` (`{ [projectId]: "Overdue" | "Due" }`, Overdue when both, held payments left to the hold) is returned as `paymentsDue` beside `holds` by the Projects list, the Pre Production rows and the Production shoot, for the badge beside every project title. |
| `/api/accounts/cash-entries` `POST /` `PUT /:id` — Branch Transfer | `entryType` is `Received`, `Spent` or **`Transfer`**. A transfer is the studio's own money moving between two branches, recorded as **one** entry: the body names `fromBranchId` and `toBranchId` (must differ, both open branches - `transferFields`), the entry is filed in the from-branch's books (`branch` / `branch_id`, numbered by `CASH_TRANSFER`, tag `MT`) and `cash_entry.to_branch_id` names where it landed (read back as `toBranchName`). `paidFromAccount` is the from-branch's account and `receivedAccount` the to-branch's, both required for a mode with an account; no project, shoot, client (`client_name` NULL) or Paid To; category `Branch Transfer`; `signedAmount` 0. The caller must reach **both** branches on `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` (`canUseEntryBranches`, create and edit). `GET /options` returns `branches` - the reachable open branches, each with its own account labels (`branchBankAccounts.accountsByBranch`). The CSV import and payment-request approval stay Received / Spent. |
| A long picker's own search — `GET /shoots`, `/projects`, `/vendors` | Every dropdown over a list that outgrows a page is **searched on the server**, through `services/pickerSearch.js`: Word Chunk Search (`everyChunk`) over the row's id, its name and the code of what it belongs to, `PICKER_PAGE` (50) rows with the real `total` and `capped` beside them, so the menu can say "Showing 50 of 312". Each module's `/options` still carries one page for the picker to **open** on. It used to carry 200 and nothing else existed - the picker filtered what it had been given, so the cap was the limit of what a record could be **filed against**, and ordered newest-first what it dropped was the oldest, which is a studio's imported history (200 of 986 shoots and 200 of 640 projects on the dev database). Live on `/api/hrm/payment-requests` (`/shoots`, `/vendors`), `/api/accounts/cash-entries` (`/projects`, `/shoots`) and `/api/sales/extra-quotations` (`/projects`, `/shoots`). A shoot search takes `projectId` **only** where the form's own rule narrows it - the cash entry and the extra quotation belong to one project's shoot - and never on a payment request, whose line derives its project **from** the shoot (`resolveExpenseLinks`). Each route answers for its own module's permission and is mounted above that router's `/:id`, or "shoots" is read as an id. Frontend: `Dropdown`'s `onSearch` / `searchLoading` / `searchTotal`, fed by the `useRemoteOptions` hook, which also keeps a chosen row's label when a later search no longer offers it. |
| `/api/accounts/cash-entries` `POST /import` | Accounts Manager → ⋮ → Import, behind `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` Read / Write. Every entry's `source` is set by its writer through `recordCashEntry(…, { source })` - `Manual` by default, `CSV Import` here, `PDF Import` when `POST /` is sent `source: "PDF Import"` (the only value a body may choose); NULL on entries from before it was kept. Columns by heading (`CASH_ENTRY_IMPORT_COLUMNS`, mirrored in `frontend/src/constants/importColumns.js`). Each row is filed against a project by `importProjectFor`: **Event ID** when given, else **Client Name** read as the project's WhatsApp group name in full (case and spacing ignored) - one match links it, a name shared by several projects or matching none refuses a Received row with the Event IDs to choose from; a Spent row with no project goes in unlinked on its **Branch** (no longer required). The client and branch then come off the project through `linkFields`. **Entry Date** is read day first (`importDate`, the statement parser's `parseDate`) and **Handled By** turned into its employee (`resolveImportHandler`) before the row is checked, by `importEntrySchema` - the form's rules except that the transaction ID, the account and the mode's remark may be empty, and so may a Spent row's Paid To and Handled By (`entryRules({ referencesOptional: true, spentPartiesOptional: true })`; `handlerFields` stores no handler when none is named). |
| `/api/accounts/statement-import` | Accounts Manager → ⋮ → Import from PDF, behind `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` (`requireModule`, so the POST needs Read / Write). `POST /extract` `{ fileName, data, password }` reads **one** PDF (data URI, ≤ 10 MB, `%PDF` signature checked) - the screen sends a batch file by file so one failure stops nothing. `pdfText.js` opens it with `pdfjs-dist` in memory (no eval, no XFA, no system fonts, ≤ 200 pages and 50k text runs, given up after 30 s, two files at a time per process; 60 files per person per 10 min by `createRateLimiter`); the password is used and dropped, never stored or logged. `parser.js` (pure) finds a statement by its header row - a Deposit / Credit column, or one Amount column with Cr / Dr (an amount under Balance is never the payment) - or, where the headings are not text (SBI), by its running balance: three or more dated rows each with a payment and a balance, the direction read from whether the balance rose or fell by it - or, with one amount per row and no balance (PhonePe, GPay, Paytm), by its +/-, Cr / Dr or Credit / Debit words. Headings that wrap onto a second line are read together; a UTR / RRN is preferred over an app's own transaction ID. Where the statement prints a Total Credits or Total Debits, `check` / `debitCheck` `{ stated, found, matches }` say whether what was found adds up to it. A leading serial column is allowed, a row printed as a dated line with its amounts on the line just below (IOB) is joined back together (`tableLines`), a bracketed value date is not a row, and a narration's lines join the dated row nearest them by height. Credits come back as `transactions`, debits as `debits` (each with `entryType` Received / Spent in the response, in date order). Anything else is read as a receipt: one amount, date, UTR and payer by their labels. Each transaction comes back with `date`, `amount`, the studio's own `paymentMode` (`MODE_CANDIDATES`, from `paymentOptionLists`), `transactionId` and `duplicate` when a live `cash_entry` of the same Received / Spent type already holds the same transaction ID, date, amount and details - all four (`paymentKey`; details compared without case or spacing; the reference is looked up by the indexed `transaction_id`, the entry's day taken in UTC or IST) - across every branch - with `duplicateOf`, its `entryId`, only when that entry is inside the caller's `dataScope` (`CASH_SCOPE_COLUMNS`). The file's SHA-256 is kept in `cash_statement_import` once read, so the same bytes under another name come back with `previousUpload`. Status `read`, `empty` (no credits) or `failed` (password, not a PDF, no text - a scan) is per file and answered 200. Nothing is recorded here: each row is filed through `POST /api/accounts/cash-entries`. |
| `/api/hrm/payroll-register` | HRM → Payroll → Main Page and Settings → Payroll rules, behind `HRM / Payroll Register` (`requireModule`: GET Read, the rest Read / Write). `GET /?year=&month=` is `{ rules, cycleDay, paybackDay, period, payableOn, components, rows }` for a month - `period` the days its rows were worked out over when they agree (`generated: true`), else the cycle as Payroll rules set it, and `payableOn` the payable day of that same month (`payableDateFor`) - a payroll month is named for the month it is paid in, so October's is the cycle that ended in September, paid 10 Oct; `POST /sync { year, month }` brings the month up to date as the Main Page opens it, in place of Generate and Refresh (`syncMonth`) - but only once the month's pay cycle has ended (`cycleGate`: from the day after its last day, in India); before that nothing is made or read again (`{ ..., opensOn }`), and `GET /` answers no rows, with `opensOn`, even where rows exist: for the month still being paid (`openPayrollMonth` - the first whose payable day has not passed in India) and every later one, every unpaid, unfrozen row in reach is read again (`refreshMonth` - a Base Salary or Attendance Leaves typed by hand in `PUT /:id`, marked `base_salary_typed` / `attendance_leaves_typed`, is kept as typed) and a row is added for every working employee in reach without one (`generateMonth`); rows are made no further ahead than the month after it (`ahead` beyond that: only existing rows read again); a row is written only when a figure changed, and only while its typed flags are still as read; an earlier month is only filled in, once, where it has no rows in reach - `{ earlier, ahead, added, refreshed }`. Each row's `attendanceLeaves` is every day away in the cycle (the calculator's `awayDays`: absences, half days as half, approved leave) and `casualLeaveDays` the leave the Casual Leave balance covered (`days.casualLeaveTaken`; all of it with no Casual Leave set), stored on the row; Total Leaves is `attendanceLeaves + policyBreachLeaves + daysBeforeJoining - casualLeaveDays`, never below 0; `daysBeforeJoining` is the cycle's days before the employee's joining date in the unit a day's pay is counted in (`daysBeforeJoiningIn`: working days under WORKING_DAYS, calendar days otherwise), and `generateMonth` makes no row for somebody who joined after the cycle ended; it does make one for somebody relieved on or after the cycle's first day, whose `daysAfterRelieving` (`daysAfterRelievingIn`, the same units, FIXED_30 capped at 30 with the days before joining) Total Leaves adds too, attendance counting stopping at the relieving date (`countedPeriod` `relievedOn`). Irregularity Penalty (`irregularity_penalty_days`) is attendance's `irregularityPenaltyDays` - Attendance Rules' penalty times the cycle's days still Absent - brought in as the month updates and added to Total Leaves. Base Salary is the employee's `totalSalary` (as the reviews left it), each month afresh; a figure typed on the row (`base_salary_typed`) is that month's alone and survives the month's refresh, and the next month starts from `totalSalary` again (`base_salary_breakdown` was dropped by `20261013100000_drop_base_salary_breakdown`). `DELETE /:id { reason }` marks a row removed (`removed_at`, `removed_by_user_id`, `removed_reason`) instead of deleting it, so the month's update does not add it back; `GET /` answers `removed` beside `rows`, and `POST /:id/restore` brings one back. Net Salary is Total Earned less Total Deductions; nothing is carried from an earlier month (Prior Month Adjustment was retired by `20261012320000_drop_prior_adjustment`). `compOffTakenDays` (`days.compOffTaken`: approved comp-off days off) is stored and shown only - it never enters Total Leaves or the pay. `PUT /:id { costCentreBranchId }` books a row to a branch - Payment → Cost Centre, `payroll_entry.cost_centre_branch_id` with its key to `branch` (an active branch, or null; part of the payment, so a settled row can still be booked), read back as `costCentre` through the link, and `GET /options` answers `branches`, every active branch. `PUT /:id { useCalculated: true }` hands typed figures back (both flags cleared; refused on a paid or frozen row like any figure change). A row brings in (base salary from `totalSalary`, loss-of-pay days from the Attendance calculator's `periodCalculation` over the pay cycle - the one that ended in the month before, 25 Aug - 24 Sep for October with the cycle on the 25th - the cycle's working and calendar days, the salary advance from Payroll → Advance Salary `advanceDueIn`, the recovery due from Payroll → Recovery `recoveryDueIn`, and the policy breach due from Payroll → Policy Breach `policyBreachDueIn` as `policyBreachDeduction`, added to Total Deductions); each row also names its `advanceIds`, `recoveryIds` and `policyBreachIds`; `POST /:id/skip/:kind` (`advance` | `recovery` | `policy-breach`) skips a row's Salary Advance, Recovery or Policy Breach Deduction for its month - every advance taken back in it moves to the next month not skipped (`payroll_advance_skip`), every recovery or policy breach instalment due in it is left out and the run ends a month later (`payroll_recovery_skip`, `payroll_policy_breach_skip`) - and `DELETE /:id/skip/:kind` undoes it (`skips.js`); each also needs `HRM / Payroll Advance`, `HRM / Payroll Recovery` or `HRM / Payroll Policy Breach` Read / Write and the employee in that permission's reach, is refused (409) on a paid row or where a paid month's figure would change, re-totals the touched months' unpaid rows in one transaction, and answers `{ kind, year, month, items }`; each row names `skippedAdvanceIds` / `skippedRecoveryIds` / `skippedPolicyBreachIds`; `PUT /:id { frozen }` freezes or unfreezes a row (`frozen`, `frozen_at`, `frozen_by_user_id`): a frozen row is **settled** like a paid one - its figures refused (409) until it is unfrozen (in the same save or later), the month's update passes it by, Remove refuses it, and every guard that protects a paid month (an advance or recovery into it, Skip, Convert to Recovery and its undo, removing an extra column) treats it the same - while its payment can still be recorded; until the frozen month's payable date has passed it also holds its employee (`payroll-register/freezeHold.js`): `GET /api/hrm/attendance/requests/correction-day` gives a day in that row's stored pay cycle no current status, with the reason, so an Attendance Regularization (`INCORRECT_LOGIN`) is refused on raise and edit, and creating a Salary Advance, a Recovery or a Convert to Recovery for them is refused (409) - the advance and recovery `GET /employees` pickers carry `frozenHold: { month, payableOn }` so the forms say it first; freezing keeps the rules of that moment in `paid_lop_divisor` / `paid_rounding`; `POST /mark-paid { ids, paymentModeId, paymentDate, paymentFrom?, paymentRemarks? }` marks up to 100 ticked rows Paid with one payment (the mode and paying account checked once, first - a wrong one refuses the lot, 400), each row through `updateEntry`; an already-paid, refused or unexpectedly failing row is reported in `missed` with why and the rest still go (`{ paidIds, missed }`); the page sends larger selections a hundred at a time, and `DELETE /bulk` removes ticked rows through the single Remove (`mountBulkDelete`) - both also need `ADMIN / Table Select All`; `PUT /:id` saves what is typed (strict: incentive, leaves, other deductions, remarks, payment, and `components: { "<column id>": amount }` for the extra columns, written in the same transaction as the row and deleted at 0 - never the salary advance, which is Advance Salary's) - a Paid row only its payment, and Paid needs a mode and a date; `DELETE /:id` removes an unpaid row. Marking a row Paid stores the rules it was paid under (`paid_lop_divisor`, `paid_rounding`) so a later rule change never moves it; every status-dependent write is conditional on the status it read (409 otherwise); `PUT /rules` is refused (403) to a caller limited to some branches, and applies **from the payroll month whose cycle is running today (India) on** (`runningPayrollMonth` - on 9 Oct, November's, October's cycle being over; `GET /rules` names it as `changesFrom`): each change is a `payroll_rule_version` effective from that month - the Attendance calculator's cycle day and payable day are kept in the same versions, saved through `PUT /api/hrm/attendance/calculator/period` (`settingsVersions.js`) - and every month is worked out under the latest version on or before it (`rulesForMonth`, `settingsBook`); a month's pay cycle starts the day after the month before ended, so a change of cycle day neither skips nor repeats a day; `GET /period?year=&month=` is a month's `{ from, to, payableOn }` for the Main Page header - earlier months, generated or not, keep the rules they had (the first change also records the rules before it, from January 2000); it answers `effectiveFrom`. `GET /payroll-start` / `PUT /payroll-start { startsFrom: "YYYY-MM" | null }` (strict; every branch, else 403) is Settings → Payroll rules → Payroll starts from (`hrm_settings.payroll_starts_from`, `payrollStart.js`): answers `{ startsFrom, chosen, defaultFrom }` - unchosen, the first payroll month whose pay cycle starts on or after the attendance start date; a month before it is never made by `/sync`, `GET /` answers it with `beforeStart` and no rows, and it has no payslip. `GET /payslip-generation` / `PUT /payslip-generation { generateDaysAfter }` (0-30, or null - once the cycle ends; strict) is Settings → Payroll rules → Generate payslips on (`hrm_settings.payslip_generate_days_after`), every branch's, so a branch-limited caller is refused 403 as for the rules. `GET /notice-hold` / `PUT /notice-hold { enabled }` (strict; every branch, else 403) is Settings → Payroll rules → **Hold salary during notice** (`hrm_settings.hold_salary_during_notice`, off by default): it decides only for separations recorded while it is on - a Resignation or Mutual Separation served on notice is marked `employee_separation.salary_held` from its resignation date. For such a leaver every row reads a `noticeHold` (`noticeHold.js`, worked out on read, null for everybody else): `held` - unpaid, payable on or after the resignation date and before the month whose pay cycle holds the last working day - `settlement` for that month, with `released` (the held months and their rounded salaries, each under its own month's rules) and `finalSettlement` (its own rounded salary plus theirs), and `settled` for a held month the settlement paid (`payroll_entry.settled_in_entry_id`). `PUT /:id` refuses (409) Paid on a held row and Pending on a settled one; paying the settlement marks its held months Paid in the same transaction - its payment, their own month's rules, their referral instalments, the stamp - and setting it back to Pending reopens them. A settled month's payment is the settlement's (its payment fields refused, 409), Remove refuses a settlement while it pays held months, Mark Paid pays settlements first so a held month ticked beside one counts as paid, and once a settlement is paid a month found later is not held. A paid month is never held, and a removed or un-held separation releases the hold at once. Offboarding forms answer `salaryHeld` and `finalSettlementOn` (the settlement month's payable date) for the Offboarding Confirmed message's `{final settlement date}` and `{salary hold}`. `GET|POST /components`, `PUT|DELETE /components/:id` are Settings → Payroll rules → Extra columns (`components.js`): columns added under Earnings or Deductions (`payroll_component`, kind fixed once made), each row's amounts in `payroll_entry_component` (an earning adds to total earned, a deduction to total deductions); changes refused (403) to a caller limited to some branches, a name matching a built-in column refused (400), `DELETE` removes a column with its amounts on unpaid months (`{ cleared }`), refused (409) for one a paid month holds - archived instead; `GET /` returns the month's `components` (every column not archived, and an archived one where a row of the month holds an amount) and each row's `componentAmounts`. `GET /options` the payment modes and paying accounts; `GET`/`PUT /rules` the loss-of-pay divisor (WORKING_DAYS, CALENDAR_DAYS, FIXED_30) and rounding (NEAREST_10, NEAREST_1, DOWN_1) on `hrm_settings`. Totals, LOP, net and rounded salary are worked out on read, at full precision. Narrowed by branch reach through the permission. [modules/hrm/payroll-register](src/modules/hrm/payroll-register/service.js) **A month locks once its payable date has passed** (India time; `monthLock.js`): every write - a row saved, marked Paid, removed or restored, a deduction skipped or its skip undone, a month never made filled in by `/sync` - is refused with a 403 naming the month unless the caller holds `HRM / Payroll After Payday` (Read / Write, granted to SUPER_ADMIN only by `20261015120000_payroll_after_payday`); Mark Paid lists a locked row in `missed`. A Salary Advance, Recovery or skip that would change a locked month's figure is refused (409, `assertFigureOpen`) for everybody - the Super Admin corrects a locked month on its row. Reading is never locked, and `GET /` answers `lock: { locked, payableOn, canEdit }` beside `data` so the Main Page shows a locked month read only. |
| `/api/hrm/tasks` | Tasks one person gives another. `GET /mine` and `GET /given` are the two sides of the same row and answer for `HRM / Task Management`; `GET /all` is the whole studio's list and asks for `HRM / All Tasks` as well, which is why it is a route of its own rather than a parameter on the list — a screen may never hold a finer permission than the endpoint feeding it. `GET /dashboard?range=` is the Tasks Dashboard (`dashboard.js`): ten figures over the reader's own tasks - five for what is assigned to them, five for what they gave - each with its position at the start of the range replayed from the task's activity log, plus the rows each card counts (`cards` on the row); for a reader of `HRM / All Tasks` (`req.canSeeAll`) five more (`allTotal`…`allCompleted`) over everything `allTasksScope` reaches; mounted before `/:id`. `GET /options` is the narrow assignee picker: ids and names only, so giving a task is never a way to read the user directory. `POST /` gives one; `POST /:id/start`, `/complete`, `/reassign` and `/cancel` are transitions, each its own verb because they belong to different people and record different things. `/complete` is the holder's **Finish & Send Back**: the task becomes `SUBMITTED` and waits for whoever assigned it (it closes at once when the holder is the assigner). `POST /:id/approve` is the assigner's **Mark as Completed**, refused until the task is `SUBMITTED`. `POST /:id/revoke { reason?, assignedToUserId? }` is the assigner's alone: it takes the task back from its holder while it is open and, with `assignedToUserId`, reassigns it straight to that person, Pending. `refusalFor` answers `canApprove`, `canRevoke` and `completeSendsBack` per row; each writes its own activity (`SUBMITTED`, `REVOKED`). `DELETE /:id` takes a task out of every list - the giver's and a Super Admin's, refused to everybody else - and is a **soft delete**: `deleted_at` is stamped, the row keeps its holders, history and files, and every read in the module narrows by it, so the id then answers 404 to every verb and to its own attachments. Who may do which is ownership, decided per task against the signed-in user's id; deleting is the one verb a role widens, and it is read in the service beside the task's two people rather than in the controller. `POST /:id/attachments` takes one file (multipart, 15MB, an allowlist of images, PDFs, office documents, CSV, text and ZIP - see `attachments.js`), `GET /:id/attachments/:attachmentId` streams one back with `Content-Disposition: attachment`, and `DELETE` removes it; the files themselves are one `attachments` JSON column on `hrm_task` and are read with the task, so there is no list route and nothing to join. Reading a file needs only what reading the task needs, while attaching and removing are the two people's alone - the same ownership rule the verbs keep. Nothing is public: the bytes are private objects in R2 under `hrm/tasks/<task code>/`, and a file belonging to a task the reader may not see answers the same 404 the task does. Mounted ahead of `/api/hrm`, which claims everything else under that prefix A task that is a feedback issue (`feedbackIssue` on the row, `canResolveFeedback`) is answered with `POST /:id/feedback-outcome` `{ outcome: "Issues sorted" | "Rejected", remarks }` by its holder (a reason required for Rejected), which completes it and answers the issue; it refuses `complete`, `cancel` and `delete` (403) and may still be reassigned. |
| `/api/hrm/whatsapp-groups` | The studio's employee WhatsApp groups (Settings -> Employee -> WhatsApp Groups) and adding an employee to them, behind `HRM / Employee Management` (GET Read, the rest Read / Write). A group is typed by its WhatsApp name (`employee_whatsapp_group.name`, unique). `GET /` the groups; `POST / { name }` adds one (409 if already added); `DELETE /:id`. `POST /employees/:employeeId { groupIds }` queues a `PENDING` row per group and an in-process queue adds the employee's `contactNumber` one group at a time, 5 s apart: `findAdder` takes the first linked WhatsApp - the requester's, then any staff login's - that is an admin of a group with that name (`sessionManager.listAdminGroups`), and `sessionManager.addToGroup` answers 200 `ADDED`, 409 `ALREADY`, 403/408 the group's invite link is sent to them (`INVITED`), else `FAILED` with `detail`. `GET /employees/:employeeId` reads the rows and resumes a queue left by a restart. |
| `/api/hrm/events` | HRM → Events, the studio's shared wall. `GET /` (`?from=&to=`, posting days), `POST /` and `DELETE /:id` behind `HRM / Events` Read; removing is the poster's or an administrator's, decided per event. `GET /:id/download` also needs `HRM / Event Download` Read: one photo is answered as that picture, several as a ZIP stored rather than deflated, each read by its bucket key or inline data and never by fetching a stored link (`modules/hrm/events/download.js`); a photo that cannot be read is named in `NOT-INCLUDED.txt` |
| `/api/hrm/interview` | The Interview register: `GET /candidates` (with `?status=`), `POST /candidates`, `POST /candidates/bulk` for Bulk Entry's batch, `GET` / `PUT` / `DELETE /candidates/:id`, **`POST /candidates/:id/remarks`** (a note appended to the candidate's own `statusNotes` log without moving their stage — the log is append-only and this is the only way to write a remark that is not part of a decision), **`POST /candidates/:id/whatsapp`** (one message to one candidate, through `sendWhatsAppToPerson`; the schema carries **only** the body, so a number named in the request is dropped by the object schema and the server reads it off the record the id names — there is no "send WhatsApp to this number" route in this application and there must not be, the studio's paired number being bannable for exactly that — and what was sent is appended to the log so a candidate never contacted can be told from one contacted and silent), **`GET /candidates/:id/call-logs`** (the calls on this candidate's number, for the popup's Call Logs tab. The studio's call log is one table fed by the handset integration and **keyed by the number dialled**, so it lives with the CRM only because that is where it was first read: `listCallLogsForNumber` sits beside `listLeadCallLogs` in `sales/crm-leads/callLogService.js` and shares its columns, its ordering and its table guard rather than being copied here. A `numberKey` that comes back empty returns **no rows** rather than a `WHERE` that matches everything, which would hand a screen asking about one candidate the whole studio's call history. The number is read off the candidate the id names, never from the request. **The CRM's reading of that client is stripped**: a number that is a candidate here can be a client there, so `clientName`, `enquiryId`, `crmStatus`, `note`, `recordingUrl` and `raw` are all dropped and what is passed on is the call — when, which way, how long, and which of the studio's own people was on it. `HRM / Interview` is not a grant of `CRM / Leads`. A log that cannot be read answers an empty list, the same way the CRM treats one: the call history is something extra about a candidate, not a reason the record cannot be worked), **`POST /candidates/:id/schedule`** (a round put in the diary, as one act: the round is appended or the one being edited rewritten, the candidate **moves to Interview Scheduled** with the reason appended to their decisions, and a round whose mode is **Google Meet** gets a real Calendar event — created on the caller's own primary calendar through the *same* stored Google connection the linked sheets use, whose consent already carries `calendar.events`, with the candidate, the interviewer and any guests invited, and Google's link written onto the round. The event is created **before** the write, so `GOOGLE_NOT_CONNECTED` / `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_SCOPE_REQUIRED` refuse the whole schedule rather than filing an interview nobody can join. A round that already has an event is **patched** — the entry everybody holds moves, the link the candidate was sent still works, and Google sends its own update — and a 404/410 from the patch means the event is gone, which is answered with a new one rather than with a refusal. A round that stops being a Meet has its event cancelled, and that cancel never fails the save. **Editing does not move the stage**: moving a date is not a decision about a person. The day and the hour are handed to Google as a **clock face** — `dateTime` with no offset plus the caller's `timeZone` — because `interview_date` and `interview_time` are text somebody typed and converting them to an instant is how a 10:30 interview gets booked for 5am; `meetings.js`), **`GET /meet-guests`** (the working employees with the work address an invite belongs on, for the guest picker — guarded `checkPermission("HRM", "Interview", "READ_WRITE")`, a notch tighter than the router's own guard, because the roster with everybody's email on it is for somebody booking an interview and not for everybody who may read the register; this is also why it is not part of `GET /options`), **`POST /candidates/bulk-status`** and **`DELETE /candidates/bulk`** (what the register's tick boxes lead to — every ticked candidate moved to one stage with the reason written against each of their own records, or deleted together — both behind `checkPermission("HRM", "Interview Select All", "READ_WRITE")` on top of the router's guard, with a candidate already gone counted in `missedIds` rather than refusing the selection), `POST /candidates/:id/resend-form` (reopen the application this candidate already submitted and email them their own link again — the Employee Repository's Send Back, said for a candidate: the newest **Submitted** invite goes back to `Verified` with `submitted_at` cleared so the next submission is accepted, its expiry is extended to at least a week out and never shortened, and the candidate moves back to Form Submission Pending **only from Form Submitted**, so anybody since scheduled, held or selected keeps the stage somebody moved them to. An invite with no short code is refused rather than reopened — its token is stored hashed, so there is no link left to send. A refused email answers **502 carrying the link and the new deadline** rather than 500, because the reopening already happened and what a failed send costs is only that the candidate has not been told), `GET /options` — departments, **positions** (the designation list, then the register's own `interview_position` rows after it, deduplicated case-insensitively by `mergeNames`: Position Applied For is mostly a real role, but "General" is a position nobody is ever *employed* as, and a row in `designation` is also a row in every employee's designation picker, so the two questions get two lists), active branches, genders, the **sources the studio maintains** (`interview_source`, read through `optionLists.js`, falling back to *nothing* rather than to an array, since Source is an optional combobox everywhere and a fallback here would be the hardcoding back), and the fixed status, mode and employment-type lists — and `GET /import/template` + `POST /import`, the two paths every module's CSV import answers at (`services/tableImport.js`). Guarded as a whole by `HRM / Interview`, so a GET needs `READ` and everything else `READ_WRITE`; nothing in it reads `req.user.role`. All four write paths — the form, the batch, the CSV and the public application form — go through one `candidateSchema` and one `prepareValues`, so a candidate cannot arrive through one door in a shape another would refuse. A candidate carries **which paid ad brought them in** — `campaign_name`, `adset_name`, `ad_name`, migration `20260918170000` — because a studio running hiring ads on Meta needs to know not only that an application came from Meta Ads, which `source` already says, but which push, which audience and which creative, and that is what decides what keeps running. Three columns rather than one string, since the table is filtered by one of them at a time; nullable text like everything beside them, filled only by a linked sheet or a CSV, and listed in `SHEET_WRITTEN_FIELDS` — which is what a sheet row fills when the candidate is **first created**, and never afterwards: a sync only ever adds, so a campaign renamed mid-flight reads in the register as whatever it was called when the candidate arrived. A candidate's code is `DBS-INTV-000001` from the shared `app_sequence` counter; dates are folded to `YYYY-MM-DD` through `leadDates.js` and the status onto the module's own spelling (`candidates.js`), with an unknown stage kept as typed — which is what a sheet saying `Shortlisted`, `Interviewed`, `Joined` or `Declined` gets, none of the four being stages here and none foldable onto one that is without moving a candidate nobody moved. The seven the register keeps are **Yet to Connect**, **Form Submission Pending**, **Form Submitted**, **Interview Scheduled**, **Selected**, **On Hold** and **Rejected** last. There is no interviewer in the payload either: the roster is not read by everybody who may open the register (`interviewers()` stays for the sheet assignment rules, and `meetGuests()` serves the schedule dialog's own picker behind `READ_WRITE`). A batch row that fails is named by its position and the rest are written. `GET /candidates` is **paged** — `offset`, `limit` (default 100, capped at 500) and `appending`, answering `{ data, total, counts }`, ordered `created_at DESC, id DESC` because a sync writes thousands of rows sharing a timestamp to the millisecond and without the tiebreak a row repeats between pages while another never arrives; `counts` is one `groupBy` over the register so a tab describes the stage rather than the page, and is skipped on an append. **Delete is hard** — the row goes and its screening-form invites go with it in one transaction, the way `purgeLeads` removes a CRM lead, because a soft-deleted candidate still answered to a phone number and kept a linked sheet refusing to re-import somebody nobody could see. A deleted candidate is kept out — by the linked-sheet ledger, and by a match on their own phone, email or source row id, which still recognises them after the spreadsheet has shifted their row. Mounted ahead of `/api/hrm`, which claims everything else under that prefix |
| `/api/hrm/interview/invites` | The **screening form** a candidate is sent: `GET /`, `POST /`, `PATCH /:id`, `PATCH /:id/cancel`, `POST /:id/send-email`. `employee_onboarding_invite`'s own design for a candidate rather than a new hire (`interview/invites.js`) — a hashed token with a four-and-four preview, an 8-character short code the shared link carries, an expiry, and the candidate's own email or phone as the gate. Behind `HRM / Interview`, because asking a candidate for the rest of their application is a step of working the candidate. Every invite belongs to a register row: one is found by email or phone, or created, so a form can be sent to somebody not yet on the register and they appear on it from that moment. Raising one moves a candidate at **Yet to Connect** to **Form Submission Pending**, cancelling moves them back, and a candidate further along keeps their stage — sending the form to somebody already scheduled is asking for the rest of their details, not starting them over. An edit keeps the link, which is the whole point over cancelling and re-inviting, and moving an expired invite's date forward revives it. A second live form for the same person is refused (409) |
| `/api/interview` | The public application form, answered by token rather than by a session: `GET /invites/:token` (masked contact details and the option lists), `POST /invites/:token/verify` (the email or phone, matched the forgiving way `phonesMatch` matches one), `POST /invites/:token/submit`. Rate-limited by `createPublicLinkLimiter` (30 misses - a 4xx or 5xx answer - per 15 minutes per address, 600 requests in all, so opening many real links is never refused) and `Cache-Control: no-store` throughout, both for the reasons the onboarding form's own routes give — short codes must not be enumerable, and a 410 is cacheable by default, which once left a candidate reading a stale refusal after their link was reopened. The submission writes the candidate's own answers through `prepareValues` **partially**, so it cannot blank a screening answer HR already gave, and moves the stage to **Form Submitted** only from the three stages the form is the answer to. The résumé is stored to `hrm/interview/resume` before the transaction opens; `updateMany` on `status: "Verified", submittedAt: null` is what makes a double submit a 409 rather than two writes |
| Interview sheet recovery | `scripts/resetInterviewSheetImport.js` — Interview only, dry-run by default. A sync refuses to re-write anybody the register has seen, held by two locks: `hrm_interview_google_sheet_row` and the soft-deleted candidates themselves (matched on phone, email or source row id). `--restore` clears `deleted_at`, which is lossless and needs no sync — the spreadsheet holds none of the status, interviewer, remarks or dates a restore brings back. `--flush` removes both locks so Sync Now rebuilds from the sheet, permanently, under new candidate codes and orphaning any invite already sent. `--since=YYYY-MM-DD` scopes either. Nothing written without `--apply` |
| `/api/hrm/interview/google-sheet-links` | The Interview module's linked spreadsheets: the same routes and reply shapes as `/api/sales/google-sheet-links` — list, link (`POST`, which runs the first import and rolls the link back if the sheet cannot be read), edit (`PUT /:id`), `POST /:id/sync`, `POST /:id/restore`, `GET /:id/import-preview`, a soft `DELETE /:id` that removes the candidates the sheet imported, and `GET /assignable-employees` — on the module's own tables (`hrm_interview_google_sheet_*`) and behind `HRM / Interview` rather than the CRM's permission, so an HR role links its own sheets without holding the CRM. The importer is `interview/sheetImport.js` (pure: heading aliases, row mapping, validation, classification — `test/hrm-interview-sheet-import.test.js`, which pins a **Meta lead export's own header row** reading whole: Campaign/Adset/Ad Name, `Years of experience`, `Current Salary`, `Languages known?` and `When Can you join?` all map, and the sheet's own serial column is the one heading left unread, since the table numbers its own rows) and `interview/sheetSyncService.js` (the read through `readLinkedGoogleSheet`, the ledger, the code allocator and the writes). **A sync only ever adds**: a row whose candidate is already in the register is skipped as `skippedExisting` and never rewritten, so a status, an interviewer or a remark set here survives the next pass; the only rows a pass writes are new ones and ones that were rejected or failed to write, which are never recorded as imported and are therefore retried on their own (`test/sheet-sync-add-only.test.js`); the assignment rules reuse `leadAssignmentRules.js` and the CRM store's `validateRules` unchanged, stored through Prisma in `interview/sheetRulesStore.js`. `interview/sheetAutoSync.js` polls every active link on the CRM poller's interval |
| `/api/hrm/interview/google-oauth` | The Google account behind the Interview integration: `POST /start`, `GET` / `DELETE /connection`, `GET /picker-token`, `GET /spreadsheets/:id/tabs`. The **same** stored connection and the same handlers as `/api/sales/google-oauth`, mounted a second time behind `HRM / Interview`; only `start` differs, asking for consent with the `connect:interview` purpose so the single public callback sends the browser back to `/hrm/interview` rather than the CRM (`OAUTH_RETURN_PATHS` in the OAuth service). A refused consent returns to the same page, read off the state token |
| `/api/notifications` | The top bar's bell: `GET /` for this reader's notices with an unread count, `POST /:id/read` and `POST /read-all`. Every route is scoped to `req.user.id` in the query itself, so no request shape reads or clears somebody else's. The store is general — a notice carries a type, what it is about and where pressing it goes — and two modules write to it now: Tasks, and the CRM when a lead is put in somebody's name. So it is gated per **type** rather than per endpoint: `TYPE_PERMISSIONS` in [services/notifications.js](src/services/notifications.js) maps each type to the module it answers for, the controller asks once per distinct module, and `listForUser` narrows the rows **and the unread count** to the types the reader's permissions reach — a badge counted over every row beside a list narrowed to some of them is a bell that says three and opens showing one. A type absent from the map reaches nobody, which is the safe direction |
| `/api/onboarding` | Public onboarding application by token. Rate-limited by `createPublicLinkLimiter` in [rateLimit.js](src/middleware/rateLimit.js): only misses count (30 per 15 minutes per address), so HR opening many real links in a row is never refused, and 600 requests in all bound a flood |
| `/api/offboarding` | Public offboarding (resignation) form by token: `GET /invites/:token` for what to fill in - the employee's name, designation, the notice period HR set, and their email and phone **masked** to the domain and the last two digits, which is what the verify step asks them to match (five wrong answers close the link for half an hour, the try being taken in the same conditional update that checks for one - the per-address limiter is defeated by changing address, a counter on the form is not, and a *permanent* lock would let anybody holding a forwarded link shut the leaver out of their own form; HR emailing the link again hands the tries back) - `POST /invites/:token/verify`, and `POST /invites/:token/submit`. The same `createPublicLinkLimiter` as `/api/onboarding`, and `Cache-Control: no-store` on every reply, because a 410 for a spent or cancelled link is cacheable by default. An uploaded resignation letter goes through `storeDocument(..., { trustUrls: false })`: a public caller may hand over a file, never a URL for the server to fetch, and `types` narrows it to a PDF or an image whose **first bytes** carry the type it claims. The letter is stored **before** the submission is claimed, so a file the storage refuses leaves the form open to try again instead of spending the employee's one submission on an error, and a submission that loses the race deletes what it uploaded. Both that claim and HR's decision are conditional updates rather than check-then-write, so two sends cannot overwrite each other and two approvals cannot write two separations; an approval whose separation fails returns the form to the queue, and one whose separation succeeded is never taken back |
| `/api/settings/payment-reminder` | Settings → Project Management → Payment reminder, behind `SETTINGS / Payment Reminder` (`requireModule`: GET Read, PUT Read / Write). `GET /` answers the reminder in Attendance WhatsApp Report's shape (`senderMode`, `sender`, its `fallback`, the rotation `senders`, `sentTodayCount`); `GET /senders/candidates` (Read / Write), `PUT /send-at { sendAt }`, `PUT /senders `{ mode, senderUserId, fallbackUserIds, userIds }`` - Sender or Rotation (`whatsapp_sender_policy` `PAYMENT_REMINDER`, sent through `senderPolicy.trySenders`). **Sending** is `sender.js` (`startPaymentReminderSender`, every minute, off with `PAYMENT_REMINDER_AUTOSEND=false`): payments due two days from today still to be received go into each project's group (`whatsappGroupJid`, else the sender's group of the project's name), five a try, stamped `project_payment_schedule.reminder_sent_for`. `receivedMessage.js` sends the Cash Received receipt into the group from the same senders after `POST /api/accounts/cash-entries` (typed entries) or a scheduled payment's `POST /:id/clear` / part payment answers (`cash-entries/controller.js` `sendReceiptToGroup`). |
| `/api/settings/shoot-crew-notice` | Settings → Project Management → Shoot crew notice, behind `SETTINGS / Shoot Crew Notice` (`requireModule`: GET Read, PUT Read / Write) - **two arms on one permission**, the studio's own people and a shoot's confirmed vendors. `GET /` answers both switches, each arm's last send and last trouble, and **what is waiting**; `PUT /enabled { enabled }` and `PUT /vendor/enabled { enabled }`. **Who sends it is not a setting**: the crew arm sends from the project's `pre_production_coordinator_employee_id` and the vendor arm from its `production_manager_employee_id` (`projectSender.js`), so the number changes with the project. There is **no fallback** - where that person has nobody named, no login, no Allow WhatsApp, their chat switched off or their WhatsApp not connected, that project's messages wait and `service.waiting()` says which of the five it is; the `SHOOT_CREW_NOTICE` row in `whatsapp_sender_policy` is deleted by the migration rather than left deciding nothing. **Crew, owed** (`due.js`): a submitted plan, not on Hold / Rejected / WCR, whose shoot falls between today and two days out, still for thirty minutes (the latest of the plan's, its seats' and the shoot's `updatedAt`); each person given a service is compared against `project_shoot_crew_notice` by a `digest` of their own services plus the shoot's day, times and places, so an edit elsewhere reaches nobody, a seat handed over reaches those two, and the shoot moving reaches everybody. Somebody no longer on it is a `REMOVED` notice and the row is dropped. **Vendor, owed** (`vendorDue.js`): **every** shoot from today on, not two days - a vendor is told thirty minutes after being confirmed, whenever that is - compared against `project_shoot_vendor_notice` by **three** digests (day and times / places / services), so the change names its own wording; the most disruptive of several is sent alone, the two-days-before reminder is owed even to a vendor already told on confirming, and a change inside those two days answers for it. A vendor told and then un-confirmed, dropped, or whose plan is withdrawn or shoot deleted is chased from the `last_*` snapshot on the row, because the plan that would compose it is gone; one with no number is marked `needs_number` and goes the moment one is saved. **Sending** is `sender.js` / `vendorSender.js` (`startShootCrewNoticeSender`, `startVendorShootNoticeSender`, every minute, off with `SHOOT_CREW_NOTICE_AUTOSEND=false` / `VENDOR_SHOOT_NOTICE_AUTOSEND=false`): five a sweep a few seconds apart, at most three from any one manager's phone, and at most `senderRate.MAX_PER_SENDER_HOUR` (30) an hour from one number **across both arms** - the same person is often both roles, and the two sweeps are separate timers. Each arm claims on its own column (`last_attempt_at` / `vendor_last_attempt_at`) at the sweep's start, and `keepError` must not stamp it again or the next tick finds the claim still live. A number WhatsApp has no account for is marked `needs_number` rather than retried every minute; rows for shoots now past are dropped, since the owed-list cannot reach them. Messages are written down before the send and put back only where it certainly did not go. Switching the vendor arm **on** records every already-confirmed vendor as told (`adoptConfirmedVendors`) so nothing is back-filled. Wordings: `SHOOT_CREW_ASSIGNED`, `SHOOT_CREW_REMOVED`, and `VENDOR_SHOOT_CONFIRMED` / `_REMINDER` / `_DATE_CHANGED` / `_PLACE_CHANGED` / `_SERVICE_CHANGED` / `_REMOVED`. |
| `/api/settings/wishes` | Settings → Wishes, behind `SETTINGS / Wishes` (`requireModule`: GET Read, PUT Read / Write). `GET /` answers `{ birthday, weddingAnniversary, workAnniversary }`, ten wishes each - message templates keyed `BIRTHDAY_WISH_01`..`10` and `WEDDING_ANNIVERSARY_WISH_01`..`10`, written by migration `20261012200000_wishes`, and `WORK_ANNIVERSARY_WISH_01`..`10` by `20261103120000_work_anniversary_wishes` (`INSERT IGNORE`, so a reworded wish is never put back); `PUT /:key { body }` rewords one, and only a wish key - the other templates answer to `SETTINGS / Message Template`. `{employee name}`; an anniversary wish also `{spouse name}`; a work anniversary wish also `{years}` ("3 years"). `GET /` also answers `delivery { groupName, sendAt, lastAttemptAt, lastError }`. **Sending** is `sender.js` (`startWishesSender`, every minute, off with `WISHES_AUTOSEND=false`): on each active employee's birthday, wedding anniversary and work anniversary (each year since their Date Of Joining, from the 1st; `celebrationsOn` → `workAnniversaries`) a random wish of that kind goes into the Wishes Scheduler's group at its time (`scheduler.js`: `GET /scheduler`, `GET /scheduler/senders/candidates`, `PUT /scheduler/group { groupName }`, `/scheduler/send-at { sendAt }`, `/scheduler/senders `{ mode, senderUserId, fallbackUserIds, userIds }`` - Sender or Rotation, `whatsapp_sender_policy` `WISHES`), through `reportGroup.deliverToGroup`, logged once a person a day in `employee_wish`. |
| `/api/settings/whatsapp-activity` | Settings → User Settings → WhatsApp Activity Log, behind `SETTINGS / WhatsApp Activity Log` (`requireModule`: GET Read, POST Read / Write). `GET /?search&mode&status&module&trigger&recipient&sender&from&to&sentFrom&sentTo&offset&limit` answers `{ data, total, offset, limit }` newest first (`recipient` matches a name, or the last four digits of a typed number against the mask; `sender` the name of the login that pressed Send; `from`/`to` bound the row's creation, `sentFrom`/`sentTo` the moment WhatsApp accepted it - the table's Sent At box) - never the raw number or group id (`recipient_masked` only); `GET /summary` the tiles under the same filters; `GET /options` the mode, status, module and trigger catalogues; `GET /facets?column=trigger|module|recipient|mode|sender|status` the values that column actually holds in the log, `[{ value, label }]`, for the table's multi-select boxes (`sender` lists the initiating logins' names and `System` for the app's own sends; `recipient` the names the rows carry); the list filters `mode`, `status`, `module`, `trigger`, `recipient` and `sender` each take a comma-joined list; `GET /:id` one message with its `attempts` (an attempt's raw `errorMessage` only when `errorSafe` - an error this app wrote, never the library's) and `resend { eligible, reason, ambiguous }`; `POST /:id/resend { confirmAmbiguous }` (10 a minute a person) sends a FAILED message again through [services/whatsappActivity.js](src/services/whatsappActivity.js) `resend`: claimed with a conditional update so two clicks cannot both send (the loser gets 409), a new `RESEND` attempt beside the original failure, at most 6 tries, never two inside 30 seconds (429), from the original sender's own WhatsApp after both chat switches, their active login and - for a group - the project's current group are re-checked; a person goes through `sendMessageToPerson` (number re-confirmed on WhatsApp), a lead through `sendMessageToLead` (filed in the thread), a Meta template is re-queued. A production message or gallery invite resent here marks that module's own row SENT too. **Every send path records itself** with `track(context, sendFn)` - one `whatsapp_activity` row per logical message (`logical_key`, so an automatic event already SENT is answered from the row and never sent twice; a FAILED one gets a new attempt) and one `whatsapp_activity_attempt` per try; the log never blocks a send. Baileys ticks reach it from `sessionManager`'s `messages.update` (`onDeliveryStatus`, forward only); Meta statuses from `POST /api/public/whatsapp/meta/webhook` ([metaWebhook.js](src/modules/settings/whatsapp-activity/metaWebhook.js)) - which also reach the AI Feature's and Photo Selling's guest photo-match messages by the same message id; a report naming an id no message has stored yet is kept in `whatsapp_pending_status`, applied by the send that stores it (or the 5-minute sweep, which also runs at start-up), and deleted after 7 days ([services/whatsappPendingStatus.js](src/services/whatsappPendingStatus.js)) - mounted only when `META_WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN` and `META_WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET` are set, `X-Hub-Signature-256` checked over the raw body, 600 a minute an address. |
