The **number is not**: it is read off `invite.candidatePhone` / `employee.contactNumber`, which is what keeps a signed-in account from being a way to send WhatsApp to any number in the world from the studio's paired phone. There is deliberately no endpoint that takes a number; sending is [`directSend.sendWhatsAppToPerson`](src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/directSend.js), a **function** called by a feature that has already decided who it is writing to, which asks both kill switches and the length cap for every caller. Under it `sendMessageToPerson` is `sendMessageToLead` without the half that files the message: a candidate is not a lead, and a colleague's temporary password has no business in a CRM thread — but it keeps the pacing, the is-this-number-on-WhatsApp check and the typing indicator, which are why the module is allowed to exist. Both mount the WhatsApp module's own `limits.send`, keyed per **user**, so one paired account has one budget across the CRM and these rather than two. The screening form's is the interview register's own, guarded by that router's `HRM / Interview`; the interview flow's **Send Onboarding Invite** adds nothing, since it opens the employee onboarding dialog. The credentials send additionally checks `ADMIN / User Management` Read / Write in the handler — the router answers for the employee record, which is a different question, and this message carries a password |
| `/api/sales/whatsapp-chat/leads/:id/stream` | A `text/event-stream` the chat panel holds open for as long as a lead is on screen. It says one word — `changed` — whenever anything is written to that conversation, and the panel re-reads the thread through the endpoint it already used; the message itself is deliberately not pushed, so what a message looks like is decided in one place. This is what replaced re-reading the whole thread every 1.2 seconds. Not rate-limited: it is one request that stays open, and counting it against a per-minute budget would spend the whole budget on opening a handful of leads. Needs the proxy to leave it unbuffered — see [DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../docs/project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md) |
| `/api/sales/whatsapp-chat/messages/:messageId/media` | One chat file, **fetched from WhatsApp when View or Download is pressed and never stored** — not in R2, not on disk, in either direction. The message row keeps WhatsApp's own reference to the file (`rawMessage`: its address and decryption key), and [mediaOnDemand.js](src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/mediaOnDemand.js) streams it straight through with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. Only the session that received the message may ask, and a message taken back has no file. WhatsApp keeps media for a few weeks; past that the answer is a 404 saying to open it on the phone. The thread listing sends each file's name, size, type and WhatsApp's small blurred preview, never `rawMessage` itself. |
| `/api/hrm/attendance` | The attendance register: who was here, per person per day. **Derived** from `app_usage_session` - the same measured heartbeat the Work Hours report reads - so an ordinary day needs nothing entered, and a row in `employee_attendance` *overrides* it for what a measurement cannot know: approved leave, a public holiday, a half day, a shoot with no laptop open. `GET` reads a range; `POST` marks or corrects one day; `POST /clear` takes a correction back so the day reads as measured again. Guarded in its controller rather than at the route, because who may see whose depends on whose is being asked for: everybody reads and marks their own, `HRM` / `Employee Management` reads the studio's and corrects anybody's, and a caller without it is narrowed to themselves rather than refused Each register row also carries the day's breaks (`breakCount`, `breakMinutes`, `breakLabel`, `onBreak`) beside its approved `permissions`, and the summary counts `onPermission` and `onBreak` (who is inside an approved permission's window, and who is on a break, at this moment - not earlier or later in the day) - the Permissions and Break cards; each row carries `onPermission` too. `permission`, the day's count of anybody with an approved permission, stays for the Send report. A row's `available` (and the summary's `available` count, the Available card) is whether the person is working at this moment: a session today with no logout, still inside its 18 hours, no break running, no approved permission whose window holds the current time and no Correction setting the day to something other than attended - always false for a day other than today. |
| `/api/notifications` | The topbar bell: everything waiting on the signed-in person, gathered from every module they may read — follow-ups due or late, clients who replied on WhatsApp, calls not yet an enquiry, approvals awaiting a decision, and the leads that have just been put in their name. A registry in [notifications/sources.js](src/modules/notifications/sources.js); each source names the permission it answers to and is read only if the viewer holds it, so the bell can never be a way around the guard on the module a notice is about. The route itself asks only that the caller is signed in — it is on every screen, so requiring a module would 403 whole roles on every page. A source that fails contributes nothing and the rest still answer. **How wide it answers is the data scope's answer, not a role test:** `dataScope.seesEverything`, asked per source's module, so a reader the scope does not confine — a Super Admin, or anybody whose branch reach is not narrowed — gets the studio's notices rather than only their own, and a studio-wide line names whose work it is. A bell narrower than the board beneath it would be the one place an administrator's view stopped. The derived notices are computed once a minute per reader and shared by the requests that arrive meanwhile, because every open tab polls and the sources are heavy queries; the permission checks still run on every request, and the stored rows are never cached |
| `/api/sales/crm-reports/:report` | One report of the CRM's own data, filtered by the query string. Six read the studio's call log (`summary`, `employee-analysis`, `lead-analysis`, `never-attended`, `not-picked-up`, `call-history`) and three read how the staff use the application itself: `whatsapp-usage`, `application-usage`, and `work-hours` — the timesheet, a row per person per day with the day's start, finish, hours and idle time. `work-hours` is the one report that joins the two halves of the studio: its hours are **Call Hours + Application Usage**, because the browser cannot see a phone call and a salesperson on a forty-minute call was being recorded as forty minutes idle. Both halves are kept on the row beside the total. It is effort across two systems rather than wall clock and can exceed the length of a day — a call taken with the application open counts in both — so Idle stays measured against the application half alone and Day Span stays wall clock. The two sides are matched on the **name**, the only key there is between a Callyzer handset and an ERP login (the same match `whatsapp-usage` makes); a name on one side with no match on the other still gets a row, showing that half and nothing of the other. **A person on these reports is somebody in the Employee Repository**, at the working statuses and not archived — the same list `GET /options` offers, so the filter and the tables under it name the same people. Employee Analysis, `work-hours` and the call half of `whatsapp-usage` grouped the call log by `employee_name`, which is whatever the handset was labelled in the Callyzer app, so they listed people the filter refused to offer. The match is made **once, when the call is delivered**, and written to `sales_crm_call_log`.`matched_employee_id`: Callyzer's **Employee Code** against the repository's own code (`DBS-10`) first, the handset's **label** against the employee's name second — trimmed, collapsed and casefolded, but never fuzzier than that, because a wrong id files one person's calls under another's. The handset **number** is no part of it: Callyzer reports the company SIM and the repository holds the employee's own number, so the two never meet. `staffCallSql` then only *reads* that id, joining the repository for the name — so a person renamed there is renamed on every report at once, including on the calls they made before, and a handset relabelled in the Callyzer app changes nothing about whose row its calls are on. A call matched to nobody keeps the label Callyzer gave it and is shown to a reader narrowed by nothing; it stays off a scoped reader's report, because it is in no branch and on no team and there is no honest way to decide whose it is. The match is never re-made once written — a company SIM passed to somebody else must not re-file the calls its previous holder made — but a handset matched to nobody is tried again on every start, so an employee added after their first call still picks those calls up. The **call** reports (`summary`, `lead-analysis`, `never-attended`, `not-picked-up`, `call-history`) still count every call: they are about the work rather than the people. `npm run report:employee-match` lists both sides of that match before a deploy. `GET /options` is the filter bar's own lists |
| `/api/sales/quotation-calculator` | The CRM's Quotation Calculator, behind `SALES / Quotation Management`. `GET /catalogue` is the studio's active services and deliverables as Settings → Project holds them: a service as one line at its one Settings price (`<id>:flat`; the per-booking pricing lines it may still store are not read) with the deliverable variants the service brings where it answered Deliverable Added (`serviceDeliverables`) — and a deliverable with its flat fields and price plus every brand and variant it is sold as at the variant's own price, its No. of Count answer and its Settings note, so the calculator picks the way the Quotation Generator picks. `GET /bookings?date=` the shoots booked on a day. `GET /leads/:leadId` and `POST /leads/:leadId` read and apply a quote onto a lead within the caller's lead scope — the calculator still reads a saved draft back to reopen on it, but no longer writes one: its Apply to Quotation hands the choices to the Quotation Generator in the browser instead, and the POST stays for any earlier client; the draft stored with it is the calculator's own state — the service rows as the screen holds them and the quantities by line they resolve to, deliverable quantities by variant key or deliverable id, No. of Count per row, which rows a service line brought in, and the priced lines the total was built from — bounded in size, read by nothing else |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads/branches` | The branches Assigned Branch offers, from the `branch` table, narrowed to the ones the caller's roles reach — `branchOptions` in `src/services/branchOptions.js`, the same rule and the same rows the quotation's Source Branch answers from. It replaces a hard-coded array in the browser whose six names (`Erode`, `Tiruppur`, …) matched no branch the studio has, so a branch added or renamed in Settings never reached the CRM. Behind the router's `SALES / CRM Leads` |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads/column-values` | The values one column actually holds on the tab being read, which is what that column's filter dropdown offers. The menus used to be the registers themselves - the whole Sales roster under CRM, every branch under Assigned Branch, every status the module can produce - so most of a menu was values no row in front of the reader held, and picking one emptied the board. This answers a spreadsheet's question instead: what is in this column, here. Asked with the same board, tab, date range and filters the page is read with, and narrowed by the same data scope, because these are values off rows `/page` is already serving - which is also why it carries no permission of its own. **A column's own filter is dropped** before the query runs: a dropdown narrowed by its own selection offers one value and no way back to the others. Answered from the lead table, or from the call log on the Calls board, so the reader never has to know which store they are looking at. Capped at 500 values; a column with more is one to type into |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads/page` — column worth | With `values=true` (a CRM Board column's first page asks it) the answer also carries `values: { estimated, converted }` over the same rows its `total` counts - same WHERE, scope and My Leads. `estimated` sums Estimated Value where it reads as a number once commas and spaces are dropped ("TBD" adds nothing); `converted` sums each lead's newest live project's Initial Converted Value (`sales_project.enquiry_id`, a lead booked twice counts once). `VALUE_TOTALS_SQL` in `leadQueryService.js`. No permission of its own: the rows are the caller's. |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads/bulk-status` | Bulk Update over the ticked leads (`SALES / CRM Lead Select All`, Read / Write; ids narrowed to the caller's own reach): `status`, `lastFollowUpDate`, `nextFollowUpDate` and - optionally - `vertical`. A vertical moves first (`moveCrmLeadsToVertical`): a lead whose number is already an enquiry in the target vertical stays and is reported (`skipped`, `skippedCount`); the rest move, losing what only the old vertical asks (`VERTICAL_ONLY_COLUMNS`), its custom fields and, off Qua Qua, its occasions; Converted & Followup becomes Converted off Qua Qua. The Enquiry ID is kept. |
| `POST /api/sales/crm-leads/:id/vertical` | One lead to another vertical, from the Vertical field in its popup's Client Details, on Update (`{ vertical }`; `SALES / CRM Leads` at Read / Write, the lead in the caller's reach). The same move as Bulk Update (`moveCrmLeadsToVertical`), but a number the target vertical already holds is refused with 409 naming that enquiry instead of skipped. Answers the moved lead. |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads/duplicates` | The enquiries already held against a phone number, matched on its last ten digits — the CRM's one rule for "the same client". Read by Add Lead as a number is typed and by the lead popup when one is edited, so a second lead is never written against a client the studio already has without the existing Enquiry ID being named first. `excludeId` leaves the lead doing the asking out of the list. The forms compare each row's vertical against the one they are writing into: inside it the save is refused, across verticals the enquiry is named and the save goes on. `POST /` and `PUT /:id` hold that same rule and answer **409** `DUPLICATE_PHONE_IN_VERTICAL` — the browser's check is a convenience, this is the guarantee. The bulk and CSV import paths are deliberately outside it |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads/bulk` | Several leads written at once (`POST`), or a whole selection deleted at once (`DELETE`, `{ ids }`), the record shape the single create takes. The delete is **one** `UPDATE` over the selection: it used to run the same statement twice, because `sheets.deleteCrmLeads` - which had long since stopped touching a spreadsheet - was called first and was that same statement, and on a whole board that is seconds a person is watching a spinner for (~4.5s against ~2.2s at 17,573 leads). One statement rather than slices, too: a soft delete costs the row and not the statement, so slicing 17,573 ids into thousands measured *slower*. The webhook is fired rather than awaited, and the optional legacy `leads` mirror is abandoned after its first refusal instead of being retried per slice. The `DELETE` is what the board's red **Delete** over a selection calls, and it — along with `DELETE /:id` — mounts `checkPermission("SALES", "CRM Lead Deletion", "READ_WRITE")` on top of the router's own `SALES / CRM Leads` guard: working the board and destroying its records are different acts, and a select-all reaches a whole vertical. Every id is held to the caller's data scope by `allowedLeadIds` before anything is removed, and the reply names the ids actually deleted, so a lead outside that scope is dropped silently rather than confirmed to exist. What **Import Leads** posts. Each record is minted its Enquiry ID here, one at a time from that record's *own* vertical and branch — the batch can span all three, so it cannot be one reserved range — and each is stamped with its creator, given a `CREATED` row in the activity log, and announced to the CRM it names. It did none of that: `lead.id` is optional on the schema, so every caller that left it out wrote rows with a blank `enquiry_id`, which is the column the board's row key, the Call Logs join, the activity log and the duplicate check are all keyed by |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads/:id/projects` | What this lead's client became, for the lead popup's **Converted History** tab. Matched on the **contact number** — the lead's own number folded to its last ten digits against each project's `client_main_number`, the same rule the Contacts directory and the duplicate check use — rather than on `enquiry_id`, so a booking raised against another of the client's enquiries still appears. Carries enough of each project for the record view the row opens; the workflow (shoots, allocations, deliverables) is deliberately not read here, since this route answers the CRM's permission rather than Projects' |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads/:id/transcripts` and `/api/sales/crm-leads/transcripts/*` | Call transcripts for the lead popup's Transcripts tab: the list per lead, and one transcript with its segments. **Read-only** — there is no longer a route that writes anything from a transcript onto a lead; `POST /transcripts/:id/apply`, which wrote chosen AI suggestions, has been removed with the panel that called it |
| `/api/sales/crm-leads` — duplicate rule | **One number is one enquiry within a vertical**, asked on every write path rather than two: `create` and `update` through `verticalClashResponse`, `createBulk` through `batchVerticalClashes` (which also compares the batch against *itself* — two new rows sharing a number are found by no lookup), and the CSV import through `loadVerticalPhoneKeys`, one query per chunk rather than one per row. An edit is refused only where it **changes** the number: the paths that never asked left pairs of leads sharing one, and guarding an unchanged number would make those pairs uneditable and so uncorrectable. Cross-vertical stays allowed. `test/crm-duplicate-phone-guard.test.js` |
| `/api/sales/google-sheet-links` | The CRM's linked spreadsheets: list, link (`POST`), edit (`PUT /:id`), `POST /:id/sync`, `POST /:id/restore`, `GET /:id/import-preview` and a soft `DELETE /:id`. **A sync only ever adds** — a row whose lead is already in the CRM is skipped as `skippedExisting`, never refreshed, so what the studio has since put on the lead is never overwritten from the spreadsheet; see `sheet-sync-add-only.test.js` and PROJECT-STATE. Behind the router's `SALES / CRM Leads` guard, GET at `READ` and everything else at `READ_WRITE`. A link carries its **lead-assignment rules** in its own create and update payload — a column, a condition and the employee whose lead a matching row is — rather than through endpoints of their own: creating a link runs a full import immediately, so rules arriving in a second request would land after that first import had already assigned nobody, and nesting them means no new permission to seed. `GET /assignable-employees` is the Sales roster those rules may name, served here rather than reused from `/api/sales/crm-employees`, which answers to `REGISTER_READERS` — a permission somebody who may link a sheet need not hold, and which would have shown them an employee picker that refused them. Both read `salesEmployees()` from [employeeOptionLists.js](src/services/employeeOptionLists.js), which tests **membership** of the employee's comma-joined department list rather than comparing the whole column — an employee set to `Sales, Technical` is a salesperson |
| `/api/sales/crd-documents` | Lists CRD document rows with vertical, link and submission status (`GET`); issues the public CRD document link for a project (`POST /:projectId/link`) or returns the one already issued - issuing it marks a never-sent document Sent; `GET /:projectId/whatsapp-group` names the group CRD Link Ready would send to, and `POST /:projectId/whatsapp-group` (`{ message }`, paced by `whatsappLimits.send`) posts its message there from the sender's paired WhatsApp ([groupSend.js](src/modules/sales/crd-documents/groupSend.js): the group linked on the project (Pre Production → General), else Image Selection's for the sale, else the one group named like the project and held by no other project, archived ones included - never written back, since linking is Pre Production's and Image Selection's). `POST /` still creates a document for a client with no project yet, but **nothing calls it**: the Create CRD Document button is gone from the screen, because a CRD is asked *of a project* — every row is a booking that owes one, and Send Link on that row is where the client's link comes from. A document belonging to no project had no Event ID, no Enquiry ID and nothing to attach the answers back to. The rows already created that way are still listed and still readable |
| `/api/project-management/client-gifts` | The two gifts a project owes its client — Welcome Gift and Shoot Completed Gift — behind Project Management → Client Gifts. `GET /:gift` lists the projects that owe one (`welcome` or `shoot-completed`), `PATCH /:gift/:projectId/status` moves it between "Yet To Sent", "Sent" and "Received by Client". The first was "Yet To Receive", which named the wrong end of the journey — a gift in it has not been *sent* yet. The column is plain text and rows written before the rename still hold the old word, so `readStatus` folds it on read rather than a migration rewriting them: it is the same answer, it needs no operator step, and the next write puts the new spelling on the row. The dates are stamped here, not sent by the screen, so a gift cannot be recorded as arriving before it left. Guarded by PROJECT → Client Gifts |
| `/api/project-management/feedback` | Reviews of a project, behind Project Management → Customer Issues and Feedback - its **New Project Feedback**, asked for as soon as the project is created in Bookings & Converted (Create Project), and a **Shoot Completion Feedback** (`SHOOT_COMPLETION`, `project_feedback.shoot_id`) for each shoot once it is Completed on the Production board (plan submitted, `current_step` `DONE`), and an **Overall Feedback** (`OVERALL`) once every live shoot of the project is (a `REJECT` shoot set aside, at least one completed; `productionComplete`) **and** every one of them has its Shoot Completion Feedback, so it is always the project's last version, **and** the project's `projectStatus` is `Delivered` - Projects → Completed (`overallFeedbackRefusal` says which of the three is missing, 409). One per project per stage per shoot: unique `project_id, stage, shoot_id`, and since the key does not compare a null shoot, the save locks the `sales_project` row first so a project's New Project Feedback is still one. The later stages - Production Completion and Project Completed Feedback - were **removed**; the rows they wrote (`PRODUCTION_COMPLETION`, `PROJECT_COMPLETED`, with their `project_feedback_area` and `project_feedback_employee` rows) are still listed and read back. `GET /` lists the feedback the caller gave - or, with the action permission PROJECT → View All Feedback, everybody's in the branches of the roles granting it (all of it for a Super Admin) - newest first, with its Feedback ID (`feedbackCode`, `DBS-FB-00001-01`: the project's `sales_project.feedback_code`, `DBS-FB-00001` from the shared `app_sequence` counter, key `PROJECT_FEEDBACK_ID`, minted with its first feedback, and the feedback's `feedback_version` - 1 for the New Project Feedback always, otherwise the next after the project's highest; unique `project_id, feedback_version`; both taken in the save's transaction under the project lock), its status (Issues, Issues sorted, Completed or Rejected - set from the review when the feedback is given: Excellent and Good are Completed, Average and Poor are Issues), its stage, its project (Event ID, Event Date - the main event date -, client, status, branch, CRM), who gave it and its issues. `GET /pending` returns every live completed shoot with no Shoot Completion Feedback yet (newest shoot first, `id` `shoot:<key>:SHOOT_COMPLETION`, with `shootKey`, `shootCode`, `shootName`, `shootDate`), then every live project whose shoots are all completed with no Overall Feedback yet (`id` `project:<key>:OVERALL`), then every live project with no New Project Feedback yet, newest first, each in the shape of a feedback row with `pending: true`, `id` `project:<key>:NEW_PROJECT` for a project, `projectKey`, `stage`, `stageLabel`, status Yet to be done and no review, with the project details Give Feedback's card shows - not narrowed to the caller's projects. `GET /options` returns the four reviews (the project is never picked on the form). `GET /projects/:projectId/people?shootId=` (PROJECT / Feedback at Read & Write) lists who worked on that shoot by area - Sales (the CRM), Pre Production (Pre Production Coordinator, Creative Manager, Project Manager), Production (Production Manager, and the shoot's Production Coordinator, Production Team and service crew) - for Shoot Completion Feedback's Appreciation and Not Satisfied lists (404 for a shoot that is gone or not that project's). `POST /` saves `{ projectId, stage?, shootId?, review, feedback, assignees?, appreciatedEmployeeIds?, notSatisfiedEmployeeIds? }` - the people only on a Shoot Completion Feedback, only people of that shoot, never one in both lists (400 otherwise), stored in `project_feedback_employee` - as the project's `stage` - New Project Feedback when none is sent, Shoot Completion Feedback for `shootId` (required with it, refused without), or Overall Feedback (409 until every shoot is completed) - `projectId` is the project's key; a deleted or unknown project, or a shoot that is deleted or not that project's, is a 404, a shoot Production has not completed a 409, and a second for the same project a 409 naming the first (the unique key refuses it even when both arrive at once) - and answers with the feedback plus its branch's `socialLinks` for the client's thank-you; there is no WhatsApp send here, the message is copied from the page. `POST /bulk` `{ items: [{ projectId, stage, shootId? }], review, feedback, assignees? }` is Give Feedback over the ticked rows, at most 500 items a request (the page sends a larger selection in batches): the review's assignee is checked once, the projects without a Feedback ID get theirs in one counter step (`reserveFeedbackCodes`), and the rows are saved 8 at a time, each through the same checks, lock and transaction as `POST /` - a refused row does not stop the rest, and the answer is `{ saved, refused: [{ ...item, message }] }`. **Issues as tasks:** an Average or Poor review needs `assignees: { REVIEW: <userId> }` - an active login holding HRM / Task Management at Read & Write, so they can answer it (400 otherwise) - and becomes an HRM task (`tasks.insertTask`, in the feedback's own transaction; its department is the feedback's - Sales for New Project, Production for a shoot's, Post Production for Overall) and a `project_feedback_issue` row; `GET /assignees` (PROJECT / Feedback at Read & Write) lists who can be assigned. Answering is the Tasks module's (`feedback/issues.js` `answerIssue`), and once every issue is answered the feedback leaves Issues - Rejected if any was, else Issues sorted. `PATCH /:id/status` `{ status, remarks }` moves a feedback under Issues with no issues on to Issues sorted or Rejected with a remark - required for Rejected (400 without one), optional for Issues sorted, at most 1,000 characters, kept in `issue_remarks` with who moved it (`issue_resolved_by_user_id`) and when - only from Issues (409 once it has moved, and for a feedback that has issues), and only for a feedback the caller may see (404 otherwise). Guarded by PROJECT → Feedback. |
| `/api/client-management/change-requests` | Client Management → Client Requests → **Raised Requests** ([change-requests/](src/modules/client/change-requests/)): the studio asks, for a booked project, a new shoot with its services and deliverables, services or deliverables for one of its shoots, or a correction. `GET /` lists them on the projects the caller reaches, `GET /:id` one with its items and history, `POST /` raises one (no price is taken - CRQ-000001 from the counter), and `GET /projects`, `/projects/:projectId/shoots`, `/masters/services`, `/masters/deliverables` feed the form - all behind `CLIENT / Client Requests` (Read to see, Read / Write to raise and to answer a reviewer with `POST /:id/reply`). Deciding is behind `CLIENT / Request Approval` Read / Write: `PATCH /:id/review` saves item decisions (approve at an amount and quantity, or reject with a reason), `POST /:id/ask` asks the requester for more, `POST /:id/decide` submits the decision once every item has one. The decision runs in one transaction that locks the request with its first read and checks the `version` the reviewer loaded (a second decision, or a stale one, is 409): approved shoot and lines are written by the projects module's `applyRequestedPlan` as Sale Extras baseline, the approved total is one **approved** extra quotation (`recordApprovedExtra`) so it counts once on Payments, and an approved correction is an HRM task for the project's Project Manager (`tasks.insertTask`). An approved item the shoot already has is refused, never charged. Every step is a `project_change_request_event` row. |
| `/api/sales/crd-documents` | Lists CRD document rows with link/submission status (`GET`), and issues the public CRD document link for a project (`POST /:projectId/link`) or returns the one already issued |
| `/api/public/quotations` | The client's quotation by link token: `GET /:token` for the record (public, rate-limited by `createPublicLinkLimiter`, counting misses only, so a family reopening its quotation is never refused; the sheet, PDF and payment share that one budget, and the PDF marks a refusal it redirects with `res.locals.linkMiss`) - with `brandSnapshot` resolved by `presentationForQuotation`, so the page has the words, pictures, branch and studio name the version was raised with - and `GET /:token/pdf` for the same page rendered to a PDF download (public, rate limited - 20 a quarter-hour per address and quotation, since every fetch is a print by the shared headless browser): one sheet the length of the document. The page's Download is a page load, so a refusal there (`wantsPage` in [downloadNavigation.js](src/services/downloadNavigation.js)) is a 303 back to `/q/<token>?download=failed`, which says so - a fetch keeps the JSON answers. A link that has run out of its five days answers `410` rather than `404` - the quotation exists, the link has lapsed. The PDF answers `403` where the quotation's brand has not switched on **Allow Client Download** (`sales_quotation_brand.allow_client_download`, `false` until somebody says otherwise): the record read carries the same answer as `allowClientDownload` so the page can drop its download control, but the refusal is here, read live off the brand, so a brand switched back to No stops serving the links it has already sent. The record also carries `paymentDetailsAvailable`, whether the pricing card prints **For Payment**; `GET /:token/payment` (public, its own rate limit - 60 a quarter-hour per address - because it hands out bank details) answers the Assigned Branch's payment details from `paymentDetailsForQuotation`, live, behind the same 404 and 410 as the record, and `data: null` where the branch has nothing to be paid into. `quotations-public/controller.js` holds that one handler; the other two stay in `projects/controller.js` |
| `/api/public/invoices` | A payment's invoice by its link token - the link in the client's receipt (`<frontend>/i/<token>`). `GET /:token` answers the invoice number, date, amount, client and studio name and logo; `GET /:token/pdf` the receipt PDF, served inline. Public: the token is the credential - `cash_entry.invoice_token`, 96 random bits from `crypto` made the first time the receipt is shared (`cash-entries/invoiceLink.js`) and kept, so every message for a payment carries one link. Both routes share one `createPublicLinkLimiter("invoice")` budget counting misses only, and the PDF a 20-a-quarter-hour budget per address and invoice. Only a live Received entry answers; a deleted one is a 404. The studio's side is under `/api/accounts/cash-entries` (Cash Management): `POST /:id/share` (Read / Write) answers the receipt message with the link in it - `{invoice link}`, or a line of its own at the end - and `GET`/`POST /:id/whatsapp-group` name and send into the project's WhatsApp group through `crd-documents/groupSend.js`, one message a person pressed Send for, logged as `CASH_RECEIPT_INVOICE` |
| `/api/public/crd-documents` | The client's Customer Identification Document by link token: `GET /:token` to read it, `PATCH /:token` to save progress, `POST /:token` to submit. Submit is refused until every required answer is present (public; rate-limited by `createPublicLinkLimiter`, counting misses only). **Event Details is the studio's half and the client is shown it read-only** — group name, event type, referred by, the point of contact, and when and where the event is, all prefilled from the project and the lead it came from. So `REQUIRED_COMMON` is empty: a field the form will not let anybody type in cannot be one that blocks submission, and Point of Contact Name is routinely blank on a wedding lead, which would have stranded the document with no screen able to fix it. What the client must answer is both sides in full and the terms. The four event fields used to be asked of the groom *and* the bride — two answers to each of four questions about one wedding — and are asked once on the common block now; a document saved under the old shape is lifted onto it on read by `liftSideEvent`, which only ever fills a blank and is safe to run every time |
| `/api/public/signature-albums` | The client's Signature Album by short code (public): `GET /:token` (the PIN screen's answer - names, cover, design, whether a PIN is asked for), `POST /:token/verify` (rate limited per caller as `clientIp` reads one - never `req.ip`, which behind nginx is the proxy for everyone - bcrypt; answers a session of the album's own scope, opened without a PIN when the album asks for none), `GET /:token/content` (cover, highlights, every chapter with its count and first batch, ending, branding, the client's permissions), `GET /:token/chapters/:chapterId/items?offset=` (the next batch), `POST /:token/download` (Download Album, step one: starts the album's PDF - the whole designed album printed by the quotation PDF's shared headless browser from the client's own page at `/a/<code>/print`, which it opens with a 15-minute album session in the URL fragment, built in the background to a temporary file by [signatureAlbum.pdf.service.js](src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.pdf.service.js): one print per album however often it is asked for, two at once in all, reprinted when the album has changed, files deleted after 30 minutes; refused when the album does not allow downloads), `GET /:token/download/status` (step two, asked every few seconds: preparing, failed, or ready with a minutes-lived download ticket of its own JWT scope) and `GET /:token/download?ticket=` (step three: the one PDF, `<title>-signature-album.pdf`; refused again without the permission, and a refused page load is a 303 back to the album with `?download=failed` rather than JSON in place of it; the start and the fetch rate limited per address and album, the status more loosely). Every photo in it is the web preview the album shows, never an original, and the thumbnail / preview fallbacks for pre-R2 photos. Every route re-resolves the album: draft and unpublished answer 404, expired 410 |
| `/api/public/image-selection/galleries` | Client-facing gallery by share token (public). Picking is behind the PIN session; `POST /:token/submit-selection` sends one selection item (`deliverableConfigId`) or, for Submit All, several (`deliverableConfigIds`, 2-50) - each checked by `submissionPlan` first and all stamped in one transaction that locks the gallery's publication row, scopes the write to the project, rolls back with 409 if any item was already sent, and sets the project-level flag once nothing offered is left open, so a refusal sends none (`test/image-selection-item-submission.test.js`, `test/public-gallery-submit-all-write.test.js`); `POST /:token/selection/clear` is Deselect All, which releases every pick for one selection item in one statement and is refused on a submitted item like any other change. `GET /:token/folders` gives each folder its first five photos as `previews` (`id` + `thumbnailUrl` - the R2 thumbnail or the session-checked thumbnail route, never an original; read off the rows already counted, studio summary unchanged - `test/image-selection-gallery-counts.test.js`); `GET /:token/folders?personId=` is the People tab's cards - the same folder summary, counting only the photos every chosen person is in together (a folder with none answers zero; a filtered read is browsing, so only the unfiltered read records the gallery as opened - `test/public-gallery-list-folders-open.test.js`) - and `GET /:token/people` is its people - who was found across the whole gallery (or in one folder with `?folderId=`, or one shoot's cards with `?folderGroupId=`), numbered in the order they were found, with how many photos each is in and which existing thumbnail (and where in it) shows their face, plus `available` (the filter's switch) and `processing` (the worker still has photos of this gallery to look at, so the tab can say people are still being organised). `?search=`, `?page=` and `?pageSize=` are the People picker's, which is what the tab's **More** opens: the rail is handed only its first dozen, and the picker reads a page at a time with `total` and `hasMore` beside them, so a gallery holding thousands of faces is never sent to a browser at once. Left out, the answer is the whole list exactly as before - and `?personId=` on `GET /:token/folders/:folderId/images` narrows that same listing to one person's photos - or, comma-separated, to the photos several people are ALL in together (`faces/filter.js` builds one condition per person, so two people is the frames holding both, never either; at most `MAX_PEOPLE` = 10 of them, the number the gallery's rail also stops at, since every extra person is another condition on a public query); with the listing's `counts` following the same filter; `:folderId` is a folder, `unfiled` (the photos in no folder) or `all` (every folder at once - `controller.folderScope`, tested in `test/public-gallery-folder-scope.test.js`), and `?folderGroupId=` narrows `all` to the cards of **one shoot** - that shoot's subfolders and, where the studio filed the photos in the shoot folder itself, that folder too. `all` + `folderGroupId` + `personId` is the People tab's combined grid: one person's photos across every card of the shoot, in one paged listing instead of a card at a time, and each photo still the card's own - nothing is copied, moved or counted twice, because it is the same row under one more condition. Both read stored mappings only, never run the engine, and answer an empty list when the filter is off or has not run. `GET /:token/scenes?folderGroupId=&personId=` is the People tab's **Scene filter** - the scenes the culling run read in the shoot's eligible photos (and, with people chosen, only in theirs), `[{ id, label, count }]` in the profile's order with each photo counted once per scene - and `?sceneId=` on `GET /:token/folders/:folderId/images` narrows that same listing, and its `counts`, to one scene (`ai-culling/scene/filter.js`; one validated profile id - never the accidental scene, which the list never offers - left out for all scenes, so an unclassified photo is only ever hidden by choosing a scene). Photos no culling run has read are handed to the scene engine in the background on the first ask (`ai-culling/scene/backfill.js`) and `processing: true` says more rows are coming, so the gallery polls; an empty list, not processing, when there is nothing to read, and the dropdown is simply absent |
| `/api/schedule` | The studio calendar behind the topbar Calendar icon: everything booked, from every module that books something — meetings, shoots, the interviews HR has scheduled — plus the studio's declared holidays, in one entry shape. Its own prefix because none of those modules owns the others. **Opening it needs nothing but a signed-in session**, which is deliberate: it used to answer for `SALES / CRM Leads`, and a holiday is the same fact for everybody in the studio. What is *drawn* is still decided source by source — each source is included only for a viewer who holds that module's own Read permission, so a viewer holding nothing gets the holidays and an empty month rather than a 403. Holidays are the one source with no permission, because there is no audience to narrow them to. **Shoots answer to three readings**: `PROJECT / Projects`, `PROJECT / Pre Production` or `SALES / Sales Manager` - the permissions about the studio's work as a whole - see every shoot; `PROJECT / Production` **alone** sees the shoots that person is named on (`shoot-board/read` `boardWhere`, so the calendar and the board cannot disagree about whose shoot it is); and a viewer holding none of the four is never even queried for them. Seeing is not opening: the window a shoot opens into still answers to its own module's permission. **My Attendance** (`kind: "attendance"`) answers to `HRM / Employee Attendance`, the guard on the person's own Attendance page: the viewer's own days from the HRM register that were not simply on time - Absent, On leave, Comp off, Late, Half day, Early exit - read through `attendanceDays` for the signed-in user's own linked employee only (never anybody else's, nothing for a login with no employee), up to today and at most 92 days at once, with a comp-off told apart from leave by the approved `COMP_OFF` request. Meetings are read through the meetings module's own reader rather than re-queried, and a source that fails is named in `unavailable` rather than silently missing. Bookings made by the day carry `day` as plain `YYYY-MM-DD` text so no timezone can move them; a meeting carries its instant instead. The interview source answers for `HRM / Interview` — the whole of the guard the register's own router mounts, because a screen may not be given a finer permission than the endpoint it reads — and reads `interview_date` / `interview_time`, which are **text** columns, through the CRM's own `toIsoDay` and `toClockTime` rather than comparing them in SQL: a `gte` on a text column is an alphabetical comparison, right for the ISO days the register writes and quietly wrong for a `09/09/2026` that reached the table before the column was normalised. The hour travels in `meta` as the text the register shows, with a 24-hour `clock24` beside it for the list's Time column to sort on Every entry carries `branch` - `{ id, name }`, the lead's for a meeting, the project's for a shoot, the candidate's for an interview - or `null` for a holiday and the viewer's own attendance; the Calendar's branch filter is drawn from these (`withBranches`), so it offers only branches the viewer already sees. |
| `/api/sales/crm-meetings` | Every meeting, across leads, behind `requireModule("SALES", "CRM Leads")`. `GET /?from&to&includeCancelled` lists them for the reader: a lead's meeting where its lead is readable (`leadScope`) or the reader booked it; a meeting with **no lead** - booked by the Calendar's Add Meet - only for whoever booked it, a guest whose sign-in email is on its list, or a Super Admin. Each row carries `mine` (the reader booked it). `POST /` books a meeting with no lead (Read / Write) through `crm-leads/meetingService.createMeeting`, checked by the lead meeting's own `meetingSchema`; `DELETE /:id` cancels one, and only one the caller booked (else 404). A lead's own meetings are still booked and cancelled at `/api/sales/crm-leads/:id/meetings`. `sales_crm_meeting.enquiry_id` is nullable since `20261015090000_meeting_without_lead`. |
| `/api/schedule/blocks` | Calendar → a day's ⋮ → Block Period, behind `requireModule("SALES", "Block Period")` and mounted before `/api/schedule`. `GET /branches` the branches a period may be blocked in (Read, `reachableBranches` for that permission); `POST /` `{ branchId, date, startTime, endTime, reason }` blocks one - times `HH:MM`, the end after the start, a branch the caller reaches at Read / Write, and refused with a 409 where another block in that branch that day overlaps; `DELETE /:id` frees one in a branch the caller may write in (`dataScope` on `branchId`, else 404). Rows in `calendar_block`; a block is a placeholder on the calendar only - it refuses no booking; the blocks reach the calendar through `/api/schedule` as its `block` source (`kind: "block"`, `meta.startTime` / `meta.endTime` / `meta.blockId`), under the same permission at Read. |
| `/api/settings/holidays` | The days the studio declares itself closed, as Settings → Holidays writes them - for every branch or for the branches chosen: `GET` the list (optionally windowed with `from`/`to`; each row carries `allBranches`, `branchIds`, `branches [{ id, name }]`, `scopeLabel` and `canEdit` - whether this caller's reach may change it, which is what hides Edit and Remove on the screen), `GET /branches` the branches this login may declare for (`branches`), the ones whose holidays they may see (`viewable`, for reading the month as one of them) and whether All branches is theirs to choose, `POST` to declare one (`{ date, name, allBranches?, branchIds? }` - absent scope is every branch, as before), `PUT /:id` to rename, move or re-scope one, `DELETE /:id` to remove one. `requireModule("SETTINGS", "Holidays")` guards the router, so reading needs Read and every change needs Read / Write; **which branches** follows the roles granting that permission (`reachableBranchIds`): a write must name branches within reach, an all-branches holiday needs every active branch in reach (a Super Admin by definition), and the list is narrowed to the studio's holidays plus the reader's branches'. A `create` rather than an upsert, inside a transaction holding one named lock (`GET_LOCK`, since the first holiday on a day has no row to lock): the day is no longer unique - two branches may each have a holiday on it - and a second holiday whose branches overlap one already there gets a 409 telling them to edit the one that is there. Links live in `attendance_holiday_branch` (none = every branch). Nothing else calls this — the attendance rules and `/api/schedule` read the same table through `services/holidayCalendar.js`, which answers per branch (a person's employee record's branch, else their login's, else the studio-wide holidays alone), so seeing a holiday never needs an administrator's permission |
| `/api/contacts` | The contact directory behind Sales → Contacts: every phone number the application holds — employees, CRM leads, project and gallery clients, quotation clients, vendor payees, vendors, client-storage buyers — **one row per record**, each under its own id, with its own name, its own vertical and its own module — and each opening its own record. It used to fold them onto one row per person and concatenate the ids, which produced a cell reading `DBS-EID-DD-ERD-000012, DBS-EID-QQ-HYD-000007`: not filterable to either, not sortable by either, one string in the export, and a double click that had to pick one of them, so the second enquiry was reachable from nowhere. The phone number still says which rows are one person — `phoneMatchKey`, the last ten digits, the same rule the lead form's duplicate check applies — and it orders the list so somebody's records arrive together. Each row carries a `key` of source plus id, because an id is unique only within the module that minted it and this list crosses eight. Callyzer's calls are **not** in it: no source reads the call log, and the lead source excludes `board_key = 'calls'`, so a number that rang the studio appears here once its caller has been triaged into a vertical and became an enquiry — not before. Its own prefix because it belongs to no one module. Opening it answers for `SALES / Contacts`, its own permission since the catalogue was made to match the sidebar — the screen is its own entry in the menu, so it has to be grantable on its own. That decides only whether the directory opens: nothing is stored, and each module's rows are read live and only for a viewer who holds that module's own Read permission, so holding `SALES / Contacts` alone opens an empty directory rather than a way past another module's guard |
| `/api/recycling-bin` | Resource Management → Recycling Bin, behind `requireModule("ASSET", "Recycling Bin")`: `GET /projects` lists deleted projects and `GET /projects/:id` reads one for the read-only view (READ, narrowed to the project's branch by `dataScope`), `POST /projects/:id/restore` restores one, `DELETE /projects/:id` with `{ confirmEventId }` deletes it permanently with its Image Selection photos, after claiming it through `purging_at` (READ_WRITE). `recycling-bin.service.js`. |
| `/api/assets`, `/api/asset-categories`, `/api/vendors`, `/api/client-storage`, `/api/asset-requests` | Asset management. The register answers for `ASSET / Asset Management`, and that grant decides only whether the screen opens and whether somebody may *write at all* — **what they may do to one asset is decided per row**, inside the controllers, by `capabilitiesFor` in `assets.read.service.js`. An asset is theirs to manage if they are the employee named as its `managerId` or the login in its `createdBy`; on anything else they can see, the only write is moving it to `UNDER_REPAIR`. Beware the two id types: `managerId`/`assignedPersonId` hold an `Employee.id` and `createdBy` holds a `User.id`, which is why the caller is resolved once into `{ userId, employeeId }` rather than compared ad hoc. **`ALLOWED_STATUS_TRANSITIONS` lives here**, not on the screen — it used to live only in `AssetManagementPage.js`, so `PATCH /:assetId/status` took any status in the enum from anyone who could write while the menu politely hid the ones it should not offer; the list now sends each row's `allowedTransitions` and the screen draws that. Import and delete are refused to anybody but a Super Admin (`superAdminOnly`), the narrow carve-out below for a thing belonging to one role: there is one asset permission and it is held widely, so no level on it could separate disposing of a camera from asking for one. `POST /` and `POST /bulk` answer to **`ASSET / Asset Administration`**, not `Asset Management`: putting equipment on the register is held by few, while the register itself is held widely. `POST /api/asset-requests/:id/approve` deliberately still creates the asset on `ASSET / Asset Request Approval` alone — a second, intended way onto the register |

### AI Feature media upload (optimized on the client)

`POST /api/ai-feature/events/:eventId/media/upload` takes one `file` per request **into one
folder**: a staff upload naming no `folderId` is refused with 400 `Select or create a folder before
uploading media.` before anything is stored (`media/service.js`, `FOLDER_REQUIRED`); a guest's
upload through the public route names none and is listed under All Photos (`GET …/media` with no
`folderId`, which lists every live row that is in a folder plus the guests' - `LISTED_MEDIA` in
`media/service.js`, the predicate the event's `mediaCount` and the guest gallery read too; a staff
row in no folder is a leftover and is listed nowhere). **Deleting a folder deletes its photos**
(`DELETE …/folders/:folderId`, `media/service.removeFolder`): the folder's live rows are marked
deleted and the folder removed in one transaction, then each row goes through `discardMedia` - the
same discard a single `DELETE …/media/:mediaId` runs: faces and their private crops, the people
they counted towards recounted, the public keys, the row; a guest's matches cascade with the row.
A cleanup that fails on one photo is logged and the row stays deleted; the answer carries
`mediaDeleted` and `mediaCleaned`. `node scripts/cleanupOrphanAiMedia.js` (a dry run; `--apply`
acts, `--event <id>` for one event) retires two kinds of row through the same discard: orphans
an older folder deletion left live in no folder, and leftovers - rows marked deleted whose
discard failed part way, which a fully discarded row never is because the row itself goes last.
A face job that outlives its photo writes nothing into it (`processing/service.goneMeanwhile`).
The predicate lives in [shared/mediaScope.js](src/modules/ai-feature/shared/mediaScope.js). A client that optimizes the photo
before sending it - the web upload workspace and the Virtual Curation desktop both run the event's
preview recipe on the photographer's machine - may add these optional multipart text fields
(`media/uploadIntake.js`). Older clients send `file` (and `folderId`) alone and are answered
exactly as before.

The recipe is the event's `media` settings (`settingsDefaults.js`, Event Default Settings → Media
Upload): `previewPreset` and `thumbnailPreset`, each one of the five shared presets in
[src/services/imagePresets.js](src/services/imagePresets.js) (the module's defaults `high` and
`standard` ARE the 2048 px JPEG q84 and 480 px WebP q76 the AI Feature has always derived; a row
saved before the presets existed and still naming `previewMaxDimension` / `thumbnailSize` is read
as the nearest preset by `mergeGroup`), `thumbnailFormat` (`webp` | `jpeg`), `preserveMetadata`
(false: EXIF stripped, capture time kept as a column; true: an allow-list - camera make, model,
software, lens, capture times - is written fresh with `withExif`, and the GPS block, maker notes,
XMP and IPTC are never written, because every preview is a public object; `derivatives.keptExif`),
plus `uploadConcurrency` (4) and `compressionConcurrency` (3), which are the clients' alone and
the server never reads. Compression has no switch: the server derives the preview and thumbnail
from whatever arrives, and the web and desktop clients always optimize first. `videoUpload`,
`keepOriginals` and `compressionEnabled` are no longer settings - a stored copy is dropped on read
by `mergeGroup`, an older client sending one has it stripped by the schema, and video containers
left in a stored `allowedFormats` are read without them. `derivatives.recipeFrom(media)` resolves the pair to
numbers; `derive(buffer, { recipe })` without a recipe is the defaults, which is exactly what every
row before these settings was made with. A change applies to future uploads only - nothing stored
is re-derived. The AI Feature's preview never walks the byte ladder the other modules use
(`byteLadder: false`): it is the frame the face engine reads, and its detail is worth more than
its weight.

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `sourceFingerprint` | SHA-256 hex of the **original** file. Stored in `contentHash`, so a photo uploaded whole by an old client and optimized by a new one is one row, from the web or a desktop. The duplicate answer (200) comes before any decoding. |
| `sourceSizeBytes`, `sourceFileName` | The original's size and name; `fileName` shows the name, `sourceSizeBytes` is kept beside the stored size. |
| `relativePath` | Where the photo sat in the picked folder (`Wedding/Bride/DSC_1001.JPG`); relative only, no `.`/`..`. Stored, not yet shown. |
| `clientOptimized` | `"true"`: the bytes already are the preview. An upright JPEG within the **event's** preview edge, carrying no EXIF/XMP/IPTC (whatever the event's metadata setting), under 3 MB, starting at SOI and ending at EOI, holding only the segments a plain image needs before its scan (JFIF, ICC, tables, frame, restart interval - no COM or other APPn, so nothing can hide in a segment a decoder skips; `hasOnlyImageMarkers`), and decoding end to end is kept byte for byte (no second JPEG generation on the frame the face engine reads); anything else goes through the full recipe. |
| `thumbnail` (file part) | The thumbnail the client cut from the same decode as its preview. Kept byte for byte beside a kept preview when it is the event's thumbnail format by its bytes, at most 512 KB, upright, carrying no metadata, within the event's thumbnail edge and decoding end to end (`derivatives.isThumbnailAlready`); otherwise, and always beside a preview that was not kept, the thumbnail is derived here - from the original when the upload was whole, from the preview when it was optimized (the one place a derivative is cut from a derivative, because the original never reached this server). |
| `sourceCapturedAt` | ISO datetime the client read off the original's EXIF before re-encoding (a canvas keeps none). Used for `captured_at` only when the file that arrived carries no date of its own, and only inside 1990 to tomorrow (`saneCaptureDate`); outside, the photo has no capture time. `sourceFileName` is reduced to its last path segment. |
| `folderId`, `deferAi` | `folderId` is required for a staff upload (above); `deferAi` as before. |

Trust: the fingerprint is the client's word for what the original was. When the file that arrived
is the original (not optimized) the server checks it and refuses a wrong one; an optimized upload
cannot be checked and is trusted as far as the upload permission already trusts that person, who
could delete the photo outright with the same permission.
Face processing is unchanged: the row is created only once the stored preview exists, then
queued. **Photos only, derivatives only:** a video is refused with a 415 (`PHOTOS_ONLY`) before
anything is stored, and only `previews/` and `thumbnails/` are written - never `originals/`, and
`original_key` is null on every new row. A file `derive` cannot read is a 422 with nothing stored,
never the original filed in its place. Rows stored before this (videos, kept originals) are still
listed, served and deleted as they were.
Columns `source_size_bytes` and `relative_path` were added nullable (migration
`20260929120000_ai_event_media_source_fields`). The guest route (`POST /api/public/ai-feature/:code/upload`)
takes the same `file` + `thumbnail` parts and source fields - the event page optimizes a guest's
photo on the phone to the event's presets, which `GET /:code` now carries as `media` - but a guest
is anonymous, so of the source fields only the file's name, its capture time and the optimized claim
are kept: the row's `contentHash` and size are always those of the bytes that arrived, never a
fingerprint a guest typed (which could read another photo's row back or pre-empt one not yet
uploaded), and never a folder or a deferral. The route has its own limiter (300 uploads an hour per
address). Every byte-for-byte path - a kept preview, a kept thumbnail, the Image Selection
derivative route - walks the whole file first ([src/services/imageBytes.js](src/services/imageBytes.js)):
a JPEG from SOI through every scan to EOI with only the segments a plain image needs (APP2 only as
an ICC profile), a WebP chunk by chunk with only the image's own chunks and a RIFF size that is the
file, so nothing stored on the public bucket carries a payload a decoder skips.

### AI Feature guest WhatsApp summary

One WhatsApp message to a guest when new photos of them are found, never one per photo. **Off
everywhere until it is switched on twice**: the server's `AI_FEATURE_WHATSAPP_ENABLED="true"`, and
the event's own **WhatsApp guests their photo summary** (`notifications.guestWhatsAppSummary`,
default `false`, so every existing event reads off). With the server switch off nothing below runs -
not a query, not a queue job - and an upload does exactly what it did before. The three studio
switches beside it (`newPhoto`, `processingCompleted`, `newMatch`) are unchanged and still send
nothing.

| Piece | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| Consent and the phone ([notifications/otp.js](src/modules/ai-feature/notifications/otp.js)) | **No code** (owner's decision, 2026-10-29): the join card's unticked box, or Turn on in My Photos, is the guest's consent (`POST :code/whatsapp/consent` → `otp.optIn`), stored as `ai_event_guest.whatsapp_opt_in_at` for the number the guest joined with - refused only for a number with no dial code or outside `SMS_GUEST_ALLOWED_DIAL_CODES`. The message goes to that number (`phone.recipient`); staff changing the guest's phone withdraws the consent. The SMS code routes below still exist for an older page. Recording consent also records the **consent baseline**: the guest's visible matches no summary has reported are claimed by one `CONSENT_BASELINE` row that is never sent, so the first summary after consent counts only what is new from then on - never a catch-up. The code is `crypto.randomInt`, kept as sha256, five minutes, five guesses counted by a conditional UPDATE, never logged. Sends are limited per guest (45 s gap, three an hour), per number (five a day across every guest and event), per event (`AI_FEATURE_OTP_EVENT_HOURLY_LIMIT`, default 60 an hour) and by dial code (`SMS_GUEST_ALLOWED_DIAL_CODES`, default `91`), in one SERIALIZABLE transaction. Under MSG91 it needs `SMS_GUEST_OTP_TEMPLATE_ID` and never borrows the password reset's template. A guest is verified only while `verified_phone` equals their current phone. Withdrawal is one press and immediate. |
| Processing runs ([processing/runs.js](src/modules/ai-feature/processing/runs.js)) | `ai_event_processing_run`: one OPEN run per event (unique `open_key`), noted by upload (web, and the desktop's deferred hand-off), retry-failed, a staff confirmation or person link, and every finished photo. A `settle-run` job on the `ai-feature-media` queue settles it once no photo in it is QUEUED or PROCESSING and nothing has happened for `AI_FEATURE_RUN_QUIET_MS` (2 min) - COMPLETED, PARTIAL or FAILED - or closes it STALLED after `AI_FEATURE_RUN_MAX_MS` (6 h; a stalled run says nothing). Settling is one conditional UPDATE that also requires no activity since it looked, so one worker settles a run, never over fresh work. If writing the summaries then fails the job fails, and the queue's retry writes them again within the hour - safe, since each is unique per run and guest and claims its matches once. The per-photo `ai_status` is unchanged. |
| The summary ([notifications/service.js](src/modules/ai-feature/notifications/service.js)) | When a run settles, each eligible guest gets one `ai_event_notification` row (unique `idempotency_key` per run and guest) that claims, in the same transaction, their MATCHED / CONFIRMED matches no summary has reported (`ai_event_face_match.notification_id`) - the guest found through the match itself, and refused unless that guest belongs to the run's event. What counts is news: matches the face pipeline makes as the studio uploads (`AUTO`), people staff link (`PERSON_LINK`), and any match staff confirm from the review queue; a guest's own `SELFIE_SEARCH` matches are not counted, since their screen showed them at once. A guest with nothing new gets nothing. Sent from the `send-guest-summary` job through the Image Selection provider (`WHATSAPP_PROVIDER` meta or mock) with template `META_WHATSAPP_AI_TEMPLATE_NAME` (`photos_ready`, Utility): body {{1}} name, {{2}} new photos, {{3}} event, and the "Visit Your Gallery" URL button's one variable, the event link's path `e/<code>` - never a per-guest link. Everything is checked again at send. At most once: a timeout or interrupted send is `UNCERTAIN` and never repeated; only a refusal Meta answered is retried, and not one no retry can change (template missing or paused, parameter mismatch, undeliverable number, token or permission error: FAILED at once); SKIPPED and FAILED give their matches back. Only the masked number is stored. The 10-minute sweep re-queues summaries the queue never took. Meta's delivery webhook then moves a SENT row to DELIVERED / READ (`delivered_at`, `read_at`) or FAILED by `provider_message_id`, forward only ([services/whatsappNotificationDelivery.js](src/services/whatsappNotificationDelivery.js)): a FAILED report gives the matches back only for a passing Meta code (rate limits other than 131048, Meta's own errors) and at most three times per guest; any other code keeps them claimed. A report that beats the send to storing Meta's id, or that a handler failed on, is kept in `whatsapp_pending_status` and applied when the id is stored or by the sweep ([services/whatsappPendingStatus.js](src/services/whatsappPendingStatus.js)). |

### AI Feature guest login verification

Joining an event from its link finds the guest by phone. Knowing someone's number is not proof of
owning it, so a guest with something to protect - a selfie on file, a photo shown as theirs
(MATCHED / CONFIRMED on a live photo), or a phone already verified - is opened only after a
six-digit SMS code ([public/service.js](src/modules/ai-feature/public/service.js) `join`):

| Case | What happens |
| --- | --- |
| Unprotected guest, or a new guest | Joins as before: `{ session, guest }`. Joining an existing guest raises their `session_version` unless the request carries that guest's own session, so only the newest device stays signed in: a stranger who joined as a guest before the guest had anything to protect is signed out when the guest joins, and needs a code once the guest has a selfie or a match. |
| Protected guest, request carrying that guest's own current session | Joins as before (the guest's own device). |
| Protected guest, any other request | `{ verificationRequired, challenge, sentTo, expiresInSeconds, resendInSeconds }`; the code goes to the number **stored on the guest**, never one in the request. `POST /join/verify { challenge, code }` answers the session; `POST /join/resend { challenge }` sends another. The challenge is a 15-minute token of its own scope, bound to the link and the guest. |
| Event's **Confirm phone numbers by SMS** on (`guestAccess.verifyPhone`, default off) | Every phone join is asked, an existing unprotected guest and a new one too (a new guest's row is made first, unjoined, and joins once proved; removed at once if no code could be sent, never listed to staff meanwhile, and swept after 30 minutes by the media worker if never proved). |
| A number no code can reach (not in `SMS_GUEST_ALLOWED_DIAL_CODES`, or unreadable) | Never opens an existing protected guest (403). With the switch on it may join only as a new, unverified guest when no guest has that number; an existing one is refused. |
| No SMS gateway (or MSG91 without `SMS_GUEST_OTP_TEMPLATE_ID`) | 503 wherever a code is due - **never** let in unverified. So until SMS is configured, a protected guest can still use the device they already joined on, but cannot open their photos on a new one. The switch cannot be turned on while no code can be sent (409). |

A correct code records the verified number and raises `ai_event_guest.session_version`; every guest
session carries the version it was issued at (`v`) and the guest context refuses one that no longer
matches, so the code ends every other session for that guest, an impostor's included. Sessions issued
before versions existed carry none and read as 0, so none ends at rollout. Proving the phone is not
WhatsApp consent: a guest whose phone is proved turns the summary on with `POST /whatsapp/consent`,
no second SMS. Codes are `ai_event_guest_phone_otp` rows with a `purpose` (LOGIN or WHATSAPP); a code
answers only its own purpose, and the send limits (45 s gap, three an hour per guest, five a day per
number, the event's hourly ceiling) count both together. Per address, every route that sends a code
(`/join` included) shares one bucket of 60 an hour that counts only requests that actually sent one,
and code checks share one of 100 per 15 minutes - wide enough for a venue on one network. Staff
changing a guest's phone also raises `session_version`.

Known trade-offs: `/join` answers differently for a protected guest (a code step, with the stored
number masked) than for anyone else, so whether a number belongs to a protected guest of the event
can be learned by anyone with the link; and someone with the number can spend a guest's three codes
an hour, keeping the real guest out of a new device for up to an hour (their own device is unaffected).
Numbers are matched as stored: an invited guest saved as `9876543210` is not found by `+91 98765
43210`, as before this change.

### Visual Curation compression presets

Every image a Virtual Curation module uploads is compressed **before** it leaves the machine, to a
preset pair - Preview quality and Thumbnail quality, each High / Standard / Compact / Light /
Smallest - defined once in [src/services/imagePresets.js](src/services/imagePresets.js) and
mirrored byte for byte in the web app (`frontend/src/lib/imagePresets.mjs`) and the desktop
(`src/processing/imagePresets.js`), with a test on each side against this file. Image Selection,
Album Proofing and Signature Album read the studio's pair for their module from
`GET /api/image-selection/compression` ([modules/image-selection/compression/](src/modules/image-selection/compression/),
`protect` + any of `IMAGE_SELECTION / Project Management`, `IMAGE_SELECTION / Album Proofing`,
`IMAGE_SELECTION / Signature Album` or `SETTINGS / Organization` at READ - an uploader must never
be refused the recipe chosen for them);
`PUT` is `SETTINGS / Organization` READ_WRITE, the Settings panel's own permission. The pair is one
nullable JSON column, `compression`, on `image_selection_settings` (migration
`20260930100000_visual_curation_compression_settings`), a module left out being its recommended
default (Image Selection Standard / Standard - the numbers it always used; the two albums High /
Standard). The AI Feature's pair lives on the Event Default Settings `media` group above. The
legacy sharp worker (`image-selection/processing/service.js` - NAS registration, album import,
Regenerate previews) reads the same record and walks the same ladder, so a preview made on the
server and one made on a studio machine weigh the same. The Image Selection derivative route
(`POST …/uploads/derivatives`) now refuses a `thumbnail` that is not a WebP or a `preview` that is
not a JPEG by their first bytes ([src/services/imageBytes.js](src/services/imageBytes.js)); a colour profile
(WebP `ICCP`, JPEG APP2 `ICC_PROFILE`) is the image's own and passes - Chrome's canvas writes one
on every thumbnail - while EXIF and XMP do not.

### AI Editing

The AI Editing tab sits between AI Culling and Photo Selection in every Image Selection project
(web and desktop): the editing bay where photos are enhanced before they go on. Module
[modules/image-selection/ai-editing/](src/modules/image-selection/ai-editing/), mounted at
`/api/image-selection/projects/:projectId/ai-editing`, `protect` + `requireModule("IMAGE_SELECTION",
"AI Editing")` on the whole router (`ISAI_EDITING`, migration `20261011090000_ai_editing`, granted to
`SUPER_ADMIN` only). The tab's one send, `send-to-signature-album`, also carries the destination's
own guard - `Signature Album` READ_WRITE - so a role that may edit but may not fill the album is
refused by the destination exactly as it would be on that tab. There is **no send from AI Editing
into Photo Selection** (the route was removed on 2026-10-10): Photo Selection is filled by AI
Culling's send of a finalized run's Keeps (`POST …/ai-culling/sessions/:id/send-to-photo-selection`),
which still runs through this module's service function `sendToPhotoSelection` - the function is
the engine of that send, not a route of this router. Nothing else in the module writes into Photo
Selection: a save, a render, a desktop hand-over or a reset never clears a hold or makes a copy.

**The bay is a view over the project's own photos, not a copy.** A photo is in AI Editing when it
is held for editing (`ImageSelectionUpload.editingHoldAt`: imported through the desktop app's tab -
its `register-batch` takes `holdForEditing` beside `holdForCulling`; the browser's derivatives route
refuses both - or sent from AI Culling while still held) or carries an edit row (`image_selection_edit`, one per source photo:
the recipe, the latest render, where the current version was sent). Both holds sit in the gallery's
`cullEligible` predicate and both publication counts, so a photo in the bay is off every Photo
Selection surface until it is sent on.

**The recipe** ([ai-editing/recipe.js](src/modules/image-selection/ai-editing/recipe.js), mirrored
word for word in `frontend/src/lib/aiEditingRecipe.mjs` and the desktop's `editing/editingRecipe.js`,
tests on each side) is `{ style, adjustments, advanced, tools, reference, referenceProfile }`: ten
professional looks (Natural Wedding, Warm Wedding, Luxury Wedding, Cinematic, Moody, Bright & Airy,
Editorial, Film Look, Classic, Golden Hour - each a tone curve, a grade, HSL tilts, a skin
treatment, sharpening and grain where the look wants it, restrained by design), ten -100..100
sliders (exposure, contrast, highlights, shadows, whites, blacks, temperature, tint, vibrance,
saturation - the tone and white-balance ones may be `null`, "let the AI choose"), the advanced
panels (HSL per hue band, colour balance for shadows / midtones / highlights, clarity and
texture), eleven tools (Auto Exposure, Auto White Balance, Color Enhancement, Skin Retouch with
smoothing, blemishes, under-eyes, skin tone, shine and skin exposure and teeth, Face Enhancement,
Denoise with "keep detail", Sharpen, Remove Object with painted rectangles, Enhance Sky with
exposure and sunset warmth, Background brightness / blur / calm, Smart Crop to an aspect), the
ten **retouching regions** (contract 2.1.0: Facial Skin, Body Skin, Eyebrows, Eye Sclera, Iris and
Pupil, Lips, Teeth, Hair, Facial Hair, Clothes - one `amount` each, `RETOUCH_TOOL_IDS`; the engine's
`retouch_regions` stage runs after the face tools and before sharpening, every change confined to
the region's own mask: facial and body skin from the skin mask split at the face and the subject
outline, with specks replaced by the median and the low-frequency tone evened while pores and
fine detail are put back; eyebrows, sclera, iris and pupil, lips and teeth from the 68 landmarks,
the iris found as the dark disc inside the eye, so those five are unavailable without the
landmark model; hair, facial hair and clothes from heuristic outlines - the band around the head,
the textured non-skin lower face, the subject outline minus skin and head on plain areas only -
reported `partial` in `capabilities` with the reason, where a face-parsing model would make the
region exact; Skin Retouch's own teeth slider and the Teeth region whiten once, at the stronger
amount; a region the engine cannot find is left as shot and said in `notes`, and `applied.retouch`
carries each region's coverage), a reference photo's strength and the **reference profile** - the measured look of a reference as
slider deltas and tone colours, which a batch carries onto other photos with no reference bytes
per photo. `normalizeRecipe` is the only way a recipe is accepted.

**Renders** run in a Python engine ([edit_engine.py](src/modules/image-selection/ai-editing/edit_engine.py),
the scene engine's JSON-lines contract, spawned by [engine.js](src/modules/image-selection/ai-editing/engine.js)
with the face engine's interpreter - numpy, OpenCV, scipy and insightface are already in
`backend/.venv-face`; nothing downloads) through one BullMQ queue (`image-selection-ai-editing`,
embedded worker unless `AI_EDITING_EMBEDDED_WORKER=false`, concurrency `AI_EDITING_CONCURRENCY`).
What each tool genuinely is: every render starts with an **analysis** - the scene (the culling run's
CLIP label when the server has one, through `ai-culling/repository.findLatestSceneLabel`, else from
the faces and the light), the lighting (daylight, cloudy, tungsten, mixed, night, indoor), the cast,
the dynamic range, the faces' key luminance, a bright dress and a dark suit present or not -
reported in `appliedRecipe.analysis`. Auto exposure aims at the faces' key (middle grey without
faces), pulls highlights where a dress or sky is blown and opens shadows where a suit is crushed,
within restrained ceilings; auto white balance measures the neutral mid-tones with skin left out and
names the cast; the Auto Edit adds only a small, scene-aware contrast and vibrance. The sliders are a
float pipeline in linear light, highlights and vibrance skin-protected. Styles are tone curves,
grades and HSL tilts. The reference is measured into a profile (relative to each photo's own light,
so a dark reference never darkens a photo that is already dark) and, when its bytes are at hand, a
skin-kept Lab transfer follows. Denoise is OpenCV non-local-means on luma and chroma apart; sharpen
an unsharp mask under an edge mask with the added detail clamped (no halos); Remove Object is OpenCV
inpainting (classical - wires, poles and small things) and `POST …/photos/:uploadId/suggest`
proposes marks for background people from the small faces away from the subjects; sky a heuristic
mask; background GrabCut seeded from the detected faces; the face tools use InsightFace detection
and, when `1k3d68.onnx` is present, the 68-point landmarks (eyes, under-eyes, teeth); without it
every note and capability reason names `buffalo_l/1k3d68.onnx` and where it was looked for
(`AI_EDIT_HOST_LABEL`, "this server" by default; the desktop app sets its own words, and fetches
the file from the runtime manifest's `optionalFiles` whenever it lacks one the server offers). The engine
reports each stage as it goes (`image_selection_edit.stage`: "Retouching skin"), so the screen says
what is happening. `GET …/capabilities` reports which tools this server can run and why not, and the
UI shows a tool it cannot run switched off with the reason - nothing is faked. `AI_EDITING_ENABLED`
"true" / "false" decides, unset follows `AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED`; off, the tab still adds and sends
photos. **Automatic album sync** (AI Culling's "AI Editing + Signature Album"): `POST
…/ai-editing/photos/add` with `{ cullingSessionId, decision, albumSync: true }` (the album's
`READ_WRITE` is checked as well when `albumSync` is sent) enrols each photo
(`image_selection_edit.album_sync_at`) and queues the ones already rendered. Every render that saves
an enrolled photo - `renderEdit` on the server or `storeRenderedOutput` from the desktop - queues
`album-sync` `{ editId, version }` on the same queue (job id per version, 4 attempts, recovered at
boot); `syncToAlbum` skips anything not RENDERED with an output and an applied recipe, an older
version, or a version already in the album, then compresses the master with
`recipeForModule("signatureAlbum")` and calls `signatureAlbum.service.syncEditedCopy`, which updates
the photo's one album copy (`signature_album_photo.source_upload_id`) in place or makes it - taking
over the photo's by-reference item (same item, chapter, order, favourite, framing) or placing it -
and records `sent_to_album_photo_id` / `sent_to_album_version` only once that row is written (guarded
by version). A sync that gives up leaves the render untouched and writes `album_sync_error`; `POST
…/ai-editing/album-sync/retry` `{ uploadIds? }` (album `READ_WRITE`) re-queues every enrolled photo
